# AFL Dynasties: Director Bible (consolidated draft, 2026-11-04)

## How to use this

1. Authority order: the director's latest word beats this Bible, and this Bible beats every older document (roadmap, DESIGN.md, CLAUDE.md, research, handoffs).
2. Where two records disagree, the later director decision wins. Where no decision exists, the disagreement is listed in "Decisions needed from the director" and nobody picks a winner on his behalf.
3. This draft does NOT replace docs/DESIGN_BIBLE.md. That file stays the standing bible until the director agrees to this one. Nobody edits either file without his consent for that change.
4. Ranking of the three aims (2026-11-03): 1 a fun story generator (the coach's career), 2 a deep and engaging simulator, 3 an easy to understand, approachable game with player agency and gamey moments. Every proposal is ranked by this order.
5. "Nothing is sacred": any system may be cut if the game gets better, but nothing is cut until he decides.
6. Chapters 1 to 9 are verbatim consolidations of his interviews, decisions and rulings. Text in quotation marks is his own words, unaltered; text marked "agent wording" is recorded in a project document but is not his speech.
7. Each rule carries a date and a source tag in square brackets. Status words: active, superseded, cut, done, open.
8. Rules are numbered inside their chapter. A reference such as "rule 1.26" means chapter 1, rule 26; "2.35" is chapter 2, rule 35. Sections are numbered by chapter (1.A, 4.3, 7.P1).
9. "Superseded and cut" at the end of each chapter says what replaced each dropped item, so a stale rule is never quietly revived.
10. "Open for the director" at the end of each chapter lists that chapter's unanswered questions; the last section of this document collects all of them once, grouped by theme, with chapter:number references.
11. The Index of source documents (before the decisions) gives the path of every document that fed this Bible and which chapters it fed.
12. Balance is his call: audits measure and report, dials get built, he sets the sizes, and balance is settled only when his own playtest passes.
13. Agents propose, assess a feature against the feature test in a line or two, and wait. A roadmap entry is context, not authorisation.
14. If you find a conflict between this draft and his latest word, his word stands: report it, quote and date it, and correct the Bible once he agrees.
15. The one-page "The game in ten rules" below is a distillation for orientation only; the chapters hold the evidence and the exact wording.

## The game in ten rules

1. **Story first.** The game is "a simulation that feels like a story, a legacy, a dynasty", and its centre is the coach's career. Aims in order: story generator, deep simulator, approachable game with agency. (Chapter 1; 1.1, 1.4.)
2. **The risk is the player's.** Decisions move the odds, never the certainty, and a failed risk stays the player's to own. The game is objective about outcomes and never softens a failure or takes the decision away. (1.22 to 1.25.)
3. **Show what each option does and costs, never which is better.** Every decision gate states the effect, its size, the odds and the cost in football words; every option carries a positive and a negative; randomness is fine once the gate has named the risk; no best-choice labels. (1.26, 1.33.)
4. **The coach prepares, does not puppet.** The player builds the list, picks the side and sets plans and calls, then watches footballers play it out. Agency is a core pillar: active participation, not a spreadsheet. (1.14, 1.19.)
5. **One football authority.** MatchSim decides what happens; watched and simmed matches derive from the same simulation, the view decides how an event looks and never whether it happens, and stats come from real events. The AI obeys the same rules as the human and is never psychic. (Chapters 2 and 8.)
6. **Only the coach and the weather touch the sim.** What the coach says or decides can affect the sim; how the club looks or sounds never does; weather alone is the exception. Flavour work has zero gameplay effect. (1.29, 1.37.)
7. **Readable at a glance, phone first.** Readability is the second pillar: portrait Android is the reference, sections feel distinct, allegiance is obvious through club colour and bold, no number vomit, no UI vomit, no hover-only information. (Chapters 1 and 3.)
8. **Real AFL, in real AFL words.** Natural footy language, authentic grounds, rules and numbers close to real AFL, art that never breaks AFL conventions. Balance is checked by audits and settled only by his playtest. (Chapters 6 and 8.)
9. **Restraint, and nothing sacred.** Prefer the simplest thing that creates the football fantasy; every feature must pass the feature test against the bible's intents; any system can be cut if the game improves, but only he cuts. No microtransactions. (1.2, 1.3, 1.11, 1.31, 1.32.)
10. **He decides; Claude executes, proves and pushes back.** No roles, art goes through critic agents before it reaches him, no spend without his written yes, every PR auto-merges on green, and every turn ends in a true state. A roadmap is context, not authorisation. (Chapters 1 and 7.)

# Chapter 1. Vision, priorities and the director's rules of engagement

How to read this chapter: each rule carries its date and a source tag. Text in quotes is the director's own words, unaltered. A rule marked "agent wording" is recorded in a project document but is not his verbatim speech. Conflicts are settled by one rule: the later director decision wins, and the 2026-11-03 story-first order ranks everything.

## 1.A The order that ranks everything

1. **Story first.** [2026-11-03, roadmap/32-director-decisions-2026-11-03#1] The priority order that ranks every proposal is: "1 a fun story generator, 2 a deep and engaging simulator, 3 an easy to understand, approachable game with player agency and gamey moments". Shorter form used by the cut audit: "1 story generator (the coach's career) > 2 deep sim > 3 approachable with agency." [2026-11-03, CUT_AUDIT_2026-11-03; release-1-0 plan#Authority]. Earlier shorthand of the same order: "story generator > deep sim > approachable" [2026-11-02, handoffs/scratch-2026-11-02-683516; agent wording].
2. **Nothing is sacred.** [2026-11-03, roadmap/32#2] "nothing is sacred". Any system may be cut, replaced or abandoned if the game gets better. Visual work earns its place only by serving the three aims.
3. **Nothing is cut until he decides.** [2026-11-03, CUT_AUDIT_2026-11-03] The cut audit lists candidates only. Candidates 1 to 6, 9 and 11 are decided and cut (roadmap 32): row 1 the intra-club and practice matches merged into one pre-season match; rows 2 to 5 the match break calls (#874: Play through is the Through stars plan (reversed 2026-11-07: the three picks are back and Through stars is removed, 2.61; key match-ups cut too, 2.61a), Defensive forward and Loose interceptor are one Defensive set-up call, the in-match Main ruck call is gone, rotations are automatic); row 6 manual stat training gone, training plans only (#871); row 9 the draft pool filters collapsed to role, sort and search; row 11 "Play to Grand Final" and the long-press play-ahead menu cut, Sim the week being the only fast path. The rest are undecided.

## 1.B What the game is

4. **Vision.** [2026-10-09, DESIGN_BIBLE#The vision] "a simulation that feels like a story, a legacy, a dynasty".
5. **The defining moment.** [2026-10-09, DESIGN_BIBLE#The vision; brainstorm] "A player you took with a risky draft pick wins the Brownlow, the satisfaction that your vision and faith paid off."
6. **The other side.** [2026-10-09, brainstorm#The moment in play] A failed risky pick: "They should feel disappointed, but understand that the risk was theirs to make... risks can still pay off, they can still fail, its up to the player. The game should be objective about it."
7. **Wider vision (earlier statements, still standing).** [2026-10-04, user_491246e9_c01] "I have a vision of the game where players, coaches, gameplans/matchups, in-match decisions all result in a sports simulation that feels like you're playing a realistic sports-rpg". Also: "i want the game to have satisfying strategic gameplay\nrealistic and deep (yet accessible, not overbearing) simulation\nand rich with emergent storytelling". The brainstorm that produced it asked for: "Ways to improve the roleplaying/choice making/rpg-feel element central to the design philosophy of this game, without compromising the simulation's veracity". Agent framing, adopted: MatchSim stays physics; RPG feel lives in identity, commitment, memory, information and presentation.
8. **Career direction.** [2026-10-05, DESIGN.md#Career direction; GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH#1; roadmap 0.4.2] Priorities: a legible visual simulation; creative team construction with competing viable approaches; consistent saves lasting decades; players whose roles evolve; stories grounded in actual matches and career consequences; meaningful coaching during matches and seasons. A match-triggered decision must connect actual evidence to a feasible choice, applied state and observable consequences (positive, negative or inconclusive). Presentation, events, stats and histories must agree. The club career is strengthened around watching your football ideas play out, building a distinctive side, and remembering the people who made its history.
9. **Learn from rivals without copying them.** [2026-10-05 and 2026-10-06, GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH; AFL_AGENCY research; roadmap 0.4.2] He values Footy Redraft's emergent stories and AFCM's list decisions. Footy Redraft: "learning the best players makes subsequent drafts feel solved". AFCM: complexity and unclear numbers erode faith in the simulation. This game must avoid both without hiding rules; keep stable rules with changing football problems. AFCM is not a visual aspiration; the existing flat editorial design stays the visual foundation, and other games' principles are adapted, not copied. These are his experiences, not claims that a rival engine is defective.
10. **Research conclusions that bind design.** [2026-10-05, GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH#9; agent wording; the Retro Bowl quote is from the 2026-10-18 evidence file] A watched match explains supported events with decision evidence nearby; the same people carry across changing jobs and decades. Beautiful minimal presentation fails if comparison facts are hidden. Persistent characters fail if their past is inaccessible. Extra choices fail if effects converge to a no-op. Protect simplicity: "adding major new features risks upsetting the balance of the game and the simplicity that has brought it success" (Simon Read on Retro Bowl, cited in docs/research/genre_evidence_2026-10-18/mobile_sports_management.md, Key question 1, dated 2026-10-18, not in GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH.md; a reference quote, not the director's).
11. **No microtransactions.** [2026-10-05, GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH#1] "Zero microtransactions. Commercialisation is outside this research objective." Salaries and budgets are fictional football resources; revenue and monetised retention are not success measures.
12. **1.0 goal.** [2026-10-18, release-1-0 plan#Goal; agent wording] A career you can play for twenty seasons on Android, PC and web, where coaching visibly changes the match, every screen is readable at a glance, and no save from 0.1.x is lost.
13. **Party-RPG lens.** [2026-09-29, roadmap 0.2] The club is the campaign, the playing group is the party, matches are where the party is tested. A design lens only: no fantasy classes, ability bars, dialogue trees or genre furniture. Players are recognisable pieces; list building is building a party, not maximising aggregate OVR. The director called the synergy screen excellent [2026-09-29, DESIGN_AUDIT#1.8].
14. **Coach prepares, does not puppet.** [2026-09-29, roadmap 0.2] The player prepares, selects and instructs the side but does not directly control footballers once play unfolds. MatchSim keeps uncertainty: good plans can fail, stars can have poor games, stronger sides can lose.
15. **Game first, within reason.** [2026-09-29, roadmap 0.2] Realism serves believable football and understandable decisions. (Dated 2026-09-29, before the 2026-11-03 ranking that puts a deep, engaging simulator above agency; read it with 1.1; 1.Q11 answered 2026-11-12: the pillars follow the ranking, so a deep simulator ranks above agency.) When realism and agency conflict, prefer the more fun, more meaningful design, provided it still reads as Australian football. If a realistic system's choices barely matter, that is a design problem. Gamification is welcome where deliberate choices materially influence outcomes: a good decision need not guarantee a win but must have enough leverage to be felt and learned from.

## 1.C The pillars and principles (design bible)

16. **Authority.** [2026-10-09, DESIGN_BIBLE; roadmap header; DESIGN.md; brainstorm] "Bible holds the ultimate truth." "The bible is not a development roadmap, its a philosophy that informs all development processes." Where CLAUDE.md, the roadmap or any other document disagrees, the bible wins and the other document is corrected. The roadmap still owns execution, status and order. Authority order for the style guide: "When this guide and a screen disagree, the screen is wrong. When this guide and the director disagree, the director is right and this guide changes." [undated, VISUAL_STYLE_GUIDE]. The bible wins over DESIGN.md: "The design bible is the ultimate truth for what this game is and outranks this document (director, 2026-10-09)."
17. **Nobody edits the bible without him.** [2026-10-09, DESIGN_BIBLE; CLAUDE.md; AGENT_TEAMS ground rule 1.3] Each change needs his explicit consent for that change. Agents propose, never make it. Everything in the bible was said or chosen by him.
18. **Reason for the bible.** [2026-10-09, brainstorm#The idea] "I am happy with progress so far but lets get a consistent vision so development can continue with renewed focus and clear goals."
19. **Core pillar: player agency.** [2026-10-09, DESIGN_BIBLE; the 2026-11-03 ranking in 1.1 puts agency third, after story and a deep simulator; restated 2026-11-12 with his consent ("Add story, reorder"): agency is now the design bible's third pillar, after story and a deep simulator; see 1.Q10] It separates the game from simulators that "lean toward the uninteractive side". Not a spreadsheet simulator; setup and observation are fine, but active participation too.
20. **Who the game is for.** [2026-10-09, DESIGN_BIBLE] A player who plays most matches and sims the odd one. Playing hands-off is the gamer's call and the gamer's risk.
21. **Second pillar: readability.** [2026-10-13, DESIGN_BIBLE#The second pillar] "Readability and intuitive interfaces are a core pillar of this project, paramount to its success. It is a challenge for an information-heavy game, but it is what we have to aspire to." It applies to the entire game: sections feel distinct (club colours and strategic bold, never all one colour), a player's allegiance is obvious, no text becomes overlooked.
22. **One ethos.** [2026-10-09, DESIGN_BIBLE#The principles] The player knows the risk, the risk is theirs to take, and results are explained clearly and objectively.
23. **Principle 1.** [2026-10-09] The risk belongs to the player. The game does not soften a failure or take the decision away.
24. **Principle 2.** [2026-10-09] Decisions move the odds, never the certainty. "In AFL you can never take a result for granted." Perfect coaching with a weaker list maximises chances but guarantees nothing; a poor team can knock off an unprepared opponent.
25. **Principle 3.** [2026-10-09] The game shows what each decision led to. Obvious outcomes (a shot scores or not) need nothing; others (the roaming interceptor's stats, whether the switched defender halted the forward's run) need clear UI and deliberate copy. The player judges whether it met expectations.
26. **Principle 4, current wording.** [2026-10-18, approved "as written"] "Reading the game is the skill. Every decision gate says what each option does for you and what it costs, with the size and the odds, in football words. The game never says which option is better: every option carries a positive and a negative, and the weighing is the coach's. Randomness is fine when the gate has named the risk." His words behind it: "I've had a change in heart regarding my philosophy of only using football language to describe the effects of levers. I still want the football language, but I also want full transparency behind each decision and the impact it will have. How can I expect the players to make sound choices and have agency if theyre constantly rolling the dice. To be clear, RNG is still okay, as long as the risks are clearly defined in the decision gate. Please audit the game regarding this change." and "I want to reverse this philosophy, but the decision should still be relatively opaque and both options carrying positives and negatives." His consent: "Yes, use it as written" (per roadmap/30 row 28: bible section 4 replaced verbatim; section 5 trimmed to keep only "No excessive UI"; CLAUDE.md "No number vomit" and the style-guide copy rule rewritten, PR #812). Audit: DECISION_TRANSPARENCY_AUDIT_2026-10-18. (Status as of 2026-10-18: no code until he ruled on format and order. Since then he ruled "B: percentages" on roadmap/30 row 27 and "Order stands" on the ship order, and code has shipped on the 2026-11-03 rulings, so this line no longer blocks work.)
27. **Principle 5.** [2026-10-09; 2026-10-18 section 5 trimmed] "No excessive UI": the player should not feel they will develop arthritis from clicking.
28. **Core skill.** [2026-10-09, brainstorm#Decided] Taking clearly presented information and making informed strategic decisions, macro (list) and micro (match). The exact science stays "clouded by intuition and observable trends" (softened by rule 1.26).
29. **What can affect the sim.** [2026-10-09, brainstorm; release-1-0 plan constraints] What the coach says or decides can affect the sim. How the club looks or sounds never does. Weather is the one exception and should matter (system not yet designed).
30. **Immersion and the art standard.** [2026-10-09, DESIGN_BIBLE] Immersion breaks when coaching moves do not change the game, ratings do not reflect reality, the visual sim does not update to a strategy change, or the art is wrong (bad anatomy, awkward staging, misunderstanding AFL, including field dimensions and conventions). Faults that must never reach the director: skin through a jumper, a bizarre animation, a ball that hovers or acts independently of its player.
31. **Feature test.** [2026-10-09] A feature must be justifiable on its intent and answer to at least the one it claims. Questions: does it enhance the core philosophy, increase immersion, create better roleplaying, deepen agency, feel and look beautiful and fun, and is it understandable, not obtuse or opaque. The agent says whether a feature deserves to exist and which intent it serves, in a line or two before starting; the director's say is final. The older "useful test" (help the player care about players, understand what makes the group distinctive, or make a meaningful decision about it) is only an aid [2026-09-29, roadmap 0.2].
32. **Restraint and challenge.** [2026-10-09, CLAUDE.md] "Restraint is a feature": prefer the simplest thing that creates the football fantasy. Challenge the director when an idea conflicts with philosophy, is poorly scoped or has a simpler alternative; say so, then propose better. Reaffirmed by the interruption budget (new stories or supporter voice must not become a mandatory stop) [2026-10-18, CPC C-PC-27].
33. **Make rules transparent, do not make the decision.** [2026-10-18, CPC C-PC-33; GENRE P-13; agent wording] No recommendation prompts, best-choice labels or optimal matchup hints on the main surface; control names, never decisions (P-68).
34. **Balance is his call.** [2026-10-18, GENRE P-20; PASS2 P-79/P-80] Audits measure and report, dials get built, the director sets sizes. Balance is settled only when testing shows numbers close to real AFL and his playtest passes the vibe check.
35. **Global design rules.** [2026-09-30 and 2026-10-04, maintenance log; roadmap 9.1] AI parity: AI is never psychic; it may react to observable or scouted information only. No hidden AI advantages, no best-move hints, mobile-first interaction. Fix concrete progression and misleading-UX bugs before adding unrelated feature breadth. Core loop: "understand the side → identify a football problem/opportunity → make an informed choice → observe the consequence → learn for the next decision"; preserve uncertainty and trade-offs while making football logic scrutable (the transparency part restated by rule 1.26) [2026-09-30].
36. **Compact selectors, not sparse rows.** [2026-10-07, roadmap 0.4.1] Standing UI rule: retire oversized sparse scrolling player rows from team editing, never reuse them; use compact contextual selectors with the shared field builder.
37. **Zero-gameplay flavour.** [2026-10-06, roadmap 9.3 common dependencies] Flavour work may not touch ability, fatigue, injury, development, morale, relationships, board confidence, contracts, finances, scouting, AI, fixture rules, disciplinary outcomes or match results. No invented private lives, quotes or scandals for real players.
38. **RPG-009 caveat.** [2026-10-06, roadmap 9.4#RPG-009] Fun flavour, not busywork managing many personalities. Most behaviour stays quiet; only occasional meaningful situations surface. No personality dashboard, routine check-ins, relationship tending (the press standing in 5.4 is not tending: it moves only through answers, 2026-11-06), conversation-energy currency or repeated reassurance. Start with one motivation or remembered decision in an existing event path.
39. **Trailer honesty.** [undated, roadmap 9.3#ARD-M8-010] Sell actual strengths only; do not manufacture outcomes the game cannot produce; no deceptive editing, debug UI or placeholder art; audio only if cleared. Trailer process (ARD-M8-010, 2026-10-06 approval): after approval Claude first produces a short trailer plan for director review before any significant editing, covering length, audience, arc, shot list, saves, capture list, audio, title copy, formats and distribution; the trailer is complete only when he has reviewed the finished cut; its text must be readable on mobile and desktop. [2026-10-06, user_5778f315 / records d2]
40. **Playtest gate and reality check.** [2026-09-29, maintenance log; PR #102/#103 done] Core fun and readability come first: pause unrelated expansion while freezes, implausible waiting, opaque synergies, uninformed choices and weak decision feedback are open. A system must really exist, not be display-only (System Reality Audit after the Momentum discovery).

## 1.D Rules of engagement (how he works with Claude)

41. **He decides.** [2026-10-29 and 2026-10-09, ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md; CLAUDE.md] "Designed by a person, developed with AI, never designed by AI." "I decide; you execute, prove, and push back when something conflicts with the vision." He supplies vision, taste, AFL knowledge, priorities and final judgement.
42. **No roles.** [2026-10-29] No lead, keyer, rigger or bot. Each session is a plain Claude session that does the whole job and reports to him.
43. **Art QA.** [2026-11-03, overrides the old no-critics rule] Before any UI or art capture reaches him, spawn independent critic agents, fix every flaw, repeat until clean; any doubt is a fail. Once clean, every image, board, pose, capture or clip comes to him. He names what is wrong; Claude works out each fix and does it.
44. **A roadmap is context, not authorisation.** [2026-10-07, ten cross-project rules; CLAUDE.md] Do not start a roadmap item, branch or PR until it is explicitly assigned. When done: stop, report, recommend the next step, wait. The ten cross-project rules also state: every system must change what the player sees, decides or remembers (seeded on/off player-effect fixture before merging); proof before done; the sim is the authority, presentation yields; nothing scales until a vertical slice proves the core loop; art via contracts, not prompts; licence first (Qwen-Image 2.1 and Anima are non-commercial and must not ship); capped documents (roadmap 600 lines, status 150, decisions 400, handoffs 120); at most four live worktrees; no idle-monitoring crons; ask the director properly.
45. **Do not add rules in his name.** [2026-10-13, memory/written-rules-may-be-claudes-not-his; memory note, unverified: quote not found in any transcript or roadmap row supplied] A rule in CLAUDE.md or the style guide is his only if it carries his words or a dated "director" attribution. If an unattributed rule conflicts with his ask, ask once to reword. Never add a rule to a must-read doc as if he said it; quote and date him.
46. **One consolidated Bible.** [2026-11-03, user_11437ec9_c01] "there should be lots of documents in the repo or local that incldude transcripts of interviews with me, or things ive asked to add to roadmap, decisiosn ive made, read them all, dont skim anything, create one Bible we will work off of". The existing docs/DESIGN_BIBLE.md must not be overwritten without his consent.
47. **Spend, merging, states, ETAs, concision.** [2026-10-25 to 2026-11-03, ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md] No spend without his written yes in the project decision log (MERCS: Tripo, ask above 500 credits per session). He never merges; every PR gets auto-merge on green and red CI is fixed. Never wait for CI; push, arm a watcher, start the next task. End every turn with a true state (working only with a live named job; otherwise awaiting director, blocked or available). Banned phrase: "running a check" [2026-10-31]. Anything awaiting his yes is shown to him with the question in the same turn. ETAs are measured and reasoned, never guessed. Write concisely, result first. Art and animation: copy a proven source, inspect every frame before showing him (humanoid shape, possible poses, weapon held naturally, no missing or clipping limbs); broken work shown to him is a failure [2026-10-31]. Handoff file per worktree in ~/.claude/handoffs/, current state only, max 80 lines, never committed.
48. **Structured questions welcome; ask when unsure.** [2026-10-05, c01/c02] "feel free to ask me structured questions whenever you are unsure about something"; "ask me" when unsure which option. "Stop asking me to choose between tiny tasks and start executing the authorised roadmap." [2026-10-05]. Delegation: "what's next on the roadmap you can safely do" [undated].
49. **Explain and read.** [undated, c01] "please explain exactly what you're doing and give an eta on the current task" (ETAs now governed by rule 1.47). "Confirm you've seen and read the new research document and updated roadmap"; "read new roadmap and research documents to reorient yourself in the project". He adds roadmap items himself and flags them ("new items added to roadmap", 2026-10-05).
50. **Read-only means read-only.** [2026-10-05, c02] "dont action anything in these, jsut read them and explain your thoughts about them to me" (six Codex research files). He later said "implement all the best findings from that researtch" (done); the order matters: he gates each step.
51. **Skills and tooling approvals.** [2026-10-05, c02] "approve the three AFL skills, keep going". Assess connectors and plugins ("think if our connectors/any OTHER connectors would aid us"; "assess whether our plugins can help at all too"). Projects may gain `afl-<topic>` skills; learnings need an evidence bar [2026-10-08, CLAUDE.md].
52. **Vocabulary.** [undated, roadmap 0.1.1; ChatGPT/agent roadmap definitions, agent wording, not director words] "Poke" is the ChatGPT repository-status job; "Vignette" is ARD-M8-007 tactical vignettes only, unless he says otherwise; "Audit" is SYSTEM_REALITY_AUDIT material, unless he says otherwise or qualifies it; "Claude" is the primary development agent. Check roadmap and repo docs first and surface conflicts rather than silently choosing; he should not have to restate vocabulary.
53. **Queue order and scope guards.** [2026-10-05 to 2026-10-06] Finish defensive forwards, then character and club creation (Club Forge), with the art agent producing assets for it [2026-10-05]. After the viz truth fixes: "all in order": tactical timeline (M8-003 step 2), match-day weather (M4-016), intercepts by zone (M4-012) [2026-10-06]. Pasted scope guards [2026-10-05]: "Do not touch RC-001 or RC-002."; "No new trade/market valuation changes while #228 is unresolved."; "Do not duplicate #232's roadmap/status corrections."; "Do not redo #231's colour work." Research candidates he excluded stay excluded; accepted ones (such as ARD-M5-016 Inherit 2026 lists) are future work, not current behaviour [2026-10-06, README].
54. **Item 17 decisions (section 9.1).** [2026-10-06] Centre-bounce framing prototype starts now; the kick and the press room queue behind hair; no reduced-motion setting for now; the awards walk-on stays as it is; the Stat Guide uses words.

| Bare "yes" answer (2026-10-05, c02) | Unrecoverable: the question was not retained, so it carries no rule |

## 1.Q Open for the director

1. ANSWERED 2026-11-12 ("All but 14 and 15"): the design bible's "What this game will not have" section holds the drafted list less items 14 (family trees, breeding talent) and 15 (a web of player relationships). What will this game deliberately not have? (Left open in the bible session, 2026-10-09; "nothing is sacred" now applies to existing systems, but the list of deliberate absences is unset.)
2. PART ANSWERED 2026-11-12 ("Yes, at a break"): weather may change at most once a match, announced at a break, with the odds in the forecast; the rest of the system is still to design. How does weather affect a match? (Decided that it matters; the system is not designed.)
3. Does the bible's Current focus section (agency fine-tuning, visual polish, "players in the visual sim really react to your coaching moves") belong in the bible, or does it move out? (Placed on an assumption, not a stated decision.)
4. Pre-timeskip survey: a full survey before every timeskip, or the simpler version where the sim follows the club's existing instructions and asks only what nothing else covers? (Unassigned, no priority.)
5. Does the 2026-11-03 art QA rule (critics repeat until clean) fully replace your 2026-10-18 instruction not to chase marginal gains, or should pixel, alpha and easing choices still stop after two rounds?
6. ANSWERED 2026-11-12 ("Yes, reword it"): the design bible's press bullet now reads "Every answer carries a named risk: the odds of each outcome are shown before you answer." The press-conference line "An answer is a dice roll based on logic" still stands under bible principle 4; do you want it reworded to match the named-risk transparency rule?
7. Which remaining cut-audit candidates (everything other than 1 to 6, 9 and 11) do you cut, keep or replace?
8. Should the trailer item (ARD-M8-010, undated, still open) proceed, and is the audio-clearance condition met?
9. Is polish goal 1 (player agency fine-tuning in matchups and rotation changes, "easily measurable") still open as written, given the story-first order puts the coach's career story above deep-sim tuning?
10. ANSWERED 2026-11-12 ("Add story, reorder"): the design bible restates its pillars in the 2026-11-03 order (story, then a deep simulator, then approachable play with agency), readability stays its own pillar, and the feature test opens with "Does it add to the coach's story or the club's legacy?". The 2026-11-03 ranking puts story first, a deep simulator second and approachable play with agency third, yet the design bible still names agency the core pillar and its feature test (rule 1.31) has no story criterion. Do you want the pillars restated under the ranking and a story criterion added to the feature test? (The design bible is yours to change; nobody else edits it.)
11. ANSWERED 2026-11-12 ("Add story, reorder"): the pillars now follow the 2026-11-03 order, so rule 1.15 reads under it: a deep simulator ranks above agency, and "game first" means fun inside believable football, never a reason to drop realism the simulator needs. Rule 1.15 (game first within reason) puts agency above realism, but the ranking puts a deep simulator above agency. Reword it to match, or is this an exception you want kept?

# Chapter 2. Match view, simulation, tactics and match-day flow

How to read this chapter. Each rule is numbered, dated and tagged with its source. Words in quotation marks are the director's own, copied unaltered from the record. Where no quote exists the rule is stated plainly. Date "undated" means the source record carries no date. Status words: DONE (built and merged), ACTIVE (standing rule), HELD (director said later or not yet).

Precedence used throughout: a later director decision beats an earlier one, and the 2026-11-03 story-first order ranks everything: 1) story generator, 2) deep simulator, 3) approachable game. In this chapter that means the match story and the truthful simulation outrank convenience features such as Key moments (cut 2026-11-08, 2.50) or simplified call menus. Where the existing Design Bible and a record agree they are merged. Agent recommendations (audits, research) are marked as such and are not rulings.

Source tags: DB = DESIGN_BIBLE; RM26/27/28/29/30/32 = roadmap chapters 26, 27, 28, 29, 30, 32; ORQ = open-requirements.md; ARD-xx = roadmap item ids; MV = MATCH_VIEW.md; DES = DESIGN.md; SRA = System Reality Audit; cNN = session transcript tags.

---

## 2.A What match day is for

**2.1** Match day is the culmination of list management and decisions made for this specific match. Matchup changes, game plan adjustments and in-game decisions all give the player agency over the result. (2026-10-09; DB, brainstorm)

**2.2** The visual sim is the closest the game comes to its target feel: real footy, a visual simulation of the obtuse stats behind every sports sim. (2026-10-09; DB)

**2.3** Structural moves (game plan, flooding the backline, stacking the contest) change the visual sim greatly. Individual moves (a matchup, a positional switch) show on the ground; the game feed and the post-quarter and post-match copy explain how effective they were. (2026-10-09; DB table)

**2.4** Making players in the visual sim react to coaching moves is "the single biggest thing to improve the game feel". Release 1.0 Phase 3 ("players react to your calls", 0.3.0 Match day): each structural call visibly changes positions; a paired-seed audit proves the call moves odds; a capture proves the visual sim shows it. Done only when he plays a full match on PC and phone and says the calls feel like they matter, in his words. (2026-10-09 and 2026-10-18; DB, release-1-0 plan)

**2.5** Polish goal one is player agency fine-tuning: in-match decisions and pre-game coaching moves such as matchups and rotation changes, each with a real in-match impact that is easily measurable. (2026-10-09; DB brainstorm)

**2.6** Decisions move the odds, never the certainty: "In AFL you can never take a result for granted." Perfect coaching with a weaker list maximises chances and guarantees nothing. (2026-10-09; DB)

**2.7** The game shows what each decision led to. Some outcomes are obvious (a shot scores or not); others need clear UI and deliberate copy (what the roaming interceptor did, whether the switched defender halted the forward's run). The player judges whether it met expectations. (2026-10-09; DB)

**2.8** Every decision gate says what each option does and what it costs, with size and odds, in football words, and never says which option is better. Director, 2026-10-18: "I still want the football language, but I also want full transparency behind each decision and the impact it will have"; "the decision should still be relatively opaque and both options carrying positives and negatives". (2026-10-18; DB principle 4)

**2.9** Immersion breaks when coaching moves do not change the game, ratings do not reflect reality, the visual sim does not update to a change in strategy, or the art is wrong (anatomy, staging, AFL field dimensions and conventions). (2026-10-09; DB)

**2.10** The match-quality test: immediately after the siren, the player can tell the story of the match in a few sentences, including turning points and defining players, rather than only the margin and stat leaders. (2026-09-29; roadmap 0.2)

**2.11** Story first. Each match names its hero, its turning point and one to three reasons traced to numbers in the result ("the match hero and why we won or lost"). Built as MatchStory.gd behind switch match_story, off until he plays it. (2026-11-03; RM32 #5, ORQ row 49)

**2.12** The part of the game that feels most like a spreadsheet is match day: "it doesn't feel like my people are out there". The match view looks like PowerPoint and the live match view has no identity (visual audit, 2026-10-18); the engine is the asset, the match view the liability. (2026-10-05 and 2026-10-18; rpg-feel-vision, AUDIT)

**2.13** Every visual plan names what it does for the match view first, or why it does not. Judge it like a broadcast or premium sports game: ground (turf, stands, crowd, light), players (identity, team colours, readable at a glance), ball and flight, and layered information (who has it, what is happening) that informs without clutter. Decision and crowd tiles are tested at match-view scale; match-view captures at phone and PC sizes on every relevant board. (2026-10-10; match-view-is-the-centrepiece)

**2.14** Release 1.0 plan: a pre-season practice match at the end of week 7. Practice match: "Trial players and plans, no ladder"; guest prospects raised from 3 to 6. (2026-10-13 and 2026-11-03; RM26 #2, RM32, workflow plan C)

---

## 2.B Simulation authority and the match engine

**2.15** MatchSim is the football authority. "Watched matches and skipped/simulated matches must derive from the same football simulation authority." Presentation never invents a second set of outcomes; goals, disposals, frees, injuries, stats and decisions reconcile with sim state; seeded determinism is preserved. (undated; guardrails 1.1)

**2.16** "Football roles are tendencies, not hard rails." Position strongly influences participation, but ordinary AFL actions stay possible for every position group. Absurd role-gate behaviour is a sanity bug first and a balance problem second. (undated; guardrails 1.3)

**2.17** Engine design: possession chains, not a tick clock; the whole event log is generated up front and replayed, so speed, pause and skip are free. 18 on the field (6-6-6) plus 5 interchange; behinds restart with a kick-in; stoppage win clamped to [0.40, 0.60]. Recalibrated: stoppage_share 0.50, metres_gain_mean 8.8, max_touches 14, rebound_from -18. (undated; DES section 3)

**2.18** Matchday squad is 18 on the ground plus 5 interchange (23), no substitute role (it was 18+4). P0, merged in #331. The All-Australian team is also 23. Bench make-up for auto and AI selection: a forward, a defender, a midfielder first. The fifth interchange slot is the second ruck when the coach chooses dual ruck. Balance checks reran for 23-man lists; scoring, stats and development held, no recalibration. (2026-10-06; ARD-M5-001, c02)

**2.19** Dual ruck: a "One ruck / Dual ruck" toggle on the Team screen, off until chosen, saved across seasons; the second ruck takes a bench spot. AI clubs run dual ruck by rule when the spare ruck is within 5 OVR of the bench player he replaces. (2026-10-06; ARD-M5-001)

**2.20** The 2026 laws apply: the centre ball-up replaces the centre bounce ("Yes, from 2026"; 6-6-6 stays, no goal-square requirement, sub rule removed). Player-facing copy reads "centre ball-up" ("Before the first ball-up", "Ball it up"). The view's centre throw-up: ball leaves the umpire's hands, rises, drops to the ruck contest, no turf bounce. (2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07; M8-003, #414)

**2.21** A goal is always followed by a centre ball-up; an end-of-chain clanger can no longer pay free over it (47 of 538 goals had been cancelled). Recalibrated clanger_is_free 0.34 to 0.43, hitouts_per_stoppage 0.81 to 0.78. (2026-11-03; RM32)

**2.22** Clearance rule, director's choice of option 1 ("the director chose option 1"; "go with your recommendation"): the clearance winner takes the first disposal (MatchSim.clearance_keeps), merged #373. Ruck disposals aim 8.0 to 12.0 against real 12.49. Ruck clearances and middle-zone carrying are calibrated toward real per-role numbers; the first attempt (#393) broke the "No position is shut out of big ratings" parity check, was reverted in #443 and reworked in #458 with forwards winning F50 ball-ups at midfielder weight (CLEARANCE_ROLES_F50). Do not schedule the reverted version again. Approved ruck-free reach fix: RUCK_FREE_REACH 15 m, RUCK_FREE_SHARE 0.48 to 0.90. (2026-10-05/06; c03, c05, c06)

**2.23** Intercepts: "any player can intercept, but the loose defender should get more intercepts if he's good at it." Intercepts come by zone ("Yes, by zone"; "Make it real"): a kicked turnover goes to the opposition, taken by whoever is in that part of the ground and often marked, not a 50/50 loose ball. Intercepting player is weighted by intercept and marking. "Bring him to ~8": loose defender about 8.4 a game, defenders 4.9, team intercepts 39 to 53, scoring unchanged. "Merge, gap later": the midfield intercept gap (mids 1.4 vs Champion Data 2.5, forwards 0.6 vs 1.1) is backlog item 25, parked; measure by zone and report before building. Real reference: key defender 5.5, general defender 4.5, midfielder 2.7, ruck 2.1, mid-forward 2.0, general forward 1.4, key forward 1.0. (2026-10-06; ARD-M4-012, #462)

**2.24** Set shots (ARD-M4-013, "M4-013 set shots"): the shooter chooses shoot, pass or bomb, recorded on the event. Director: "should be 3 visibly different sequences, but also add a pack contest, also gives an opportunity for a crumber to pick up a spoiled ball if it is not marked." Bomb becomes a goal-square pack contest (marked, spoiled with crumber snap, defence, through); pass emits pass and receive without double-counting; no invented actors or stats; AI never gets the call. Sim #467, view #471. Status PARTIAL (set-shot-share calibration open). (2026-10-06; ARD-M4-013)

**2.25** Kick lanes (ARD-M4-014): "lanes are recorded **and** affect play, not presentation only." Corridor, switch and down-the-line are weighted by game plan; lanes change kick risk and reward, replace part of the flat plan multipliers, and the random 10% switch of play goes. "Yes, and let lanes matter." TODO, built on claude/kick-lanes with no PR yet. (2026-10-06; ARD-M4-014)

**2.26** Receiver choice (row 10), director: "no: the receiver is capped by a maximum kick distance from the kicker's kicking rating; better kickers reach further; nearest teammate when nobody is in range". MatchSim.kick_range = 30 m + 0.35 x disposal; kick_targets keeps teammates whose line home is in reach; switch kick_range stays off until he checks an exported-PC full match. Simulated matches use the same shared function, "yes, one shared function, no duplicate". (2026-11-03; RM32 #21, #22)

**2.27** Running bounces ("Add real runs to the engine"): a run decision with its own dice stream, RUN_P 0.03, BOUNCE_EVERY 15, about 5.4 a team. (2026-10-07; c07)

**2.28** Match-flow step 1 and 2 (director, 2026-10-10, "Investigate more turnovers."): the winner of an intercept or free plays on; stoppages come from contests (a free that became a random ball-up 42% of the time was a no-op mechanic). Step 2 recalibrates contested ball, spoils and turnovers toward real football (teams about 53 intercepts a game vs real 63; contested possessions 88-130, spoils 17-31, intercept marks 10-15). Sits on unmerged claude/match-flow; balance is the director's call. (2026-10-10; match-shape audit, ORQ row 11)

**2.29** Match-rule items carried by the engine: teams attack opposite ends in alternating quarters and the view mirrors x in even periods (M1-004, #49); no wrong-way kicks, guarded by test_no_wrong_way_kicks (M1-005); in-match injuries visibly affect play and reconcile with the injury record (M3-010, #97); kick-ins restart from the defending goal-square area and never become a centre bounce (M3-009); boundary rules (M3-006, #190): between the 50m arcs a last-disposal exit is a free to the opposition, inside the arcs ordinary out of bounds is a throw-in, out on the full is a free; smothers rare and balanced (M3-004); speccies only from a genuine contested mark, about 0.8 a match, hard maximum 2 (M3-005); open play has no global pause, set shots only from a genuine mark or free (M3-001); spoils produce loose balls, not automatic possession (M3-003, F50 spoils done at 15.2 per team per game, general-play spoils open); GPS distance covered is a readable diagnostic, no RNG draws (M2-010, #208). (2026-09-28 to 2026-10-06; roadmap M1 to M3)

**2.30** Director's AFL knowledge on forwards: "small half forwards will often accrue marks when running up the ground into the midfield, while keyforwards generally take their marks in the forward 50 unless they are presenting further up the ground to take a contested mark in the midfield or halfback". Basis for forward archetypes (#286). (2026-10-05; c02, M3-002)

**2.31** Ruck integrity: audit hit-outs against AFL ranges and feast-or-famine ruck duels (ARD-M1-002); add hit-outs to advantage (HTA), related to clearance probability more strongly than raw hit-outs without guaranteeing it, shown only in detailed ruck stats; diagnose before tuning. Do not make clearances deterministic. (2026-09-30; ARD-M1-002, open)

**2.32** Momentum (M4-010): small, capped, reversible; goals and behinds move a capped state, it fades each chain, halves at breaks, up to a 4% contest edge; the meter reads the exact engine state and is labelled "Momentum" (#102). Team form (M4-011) must not stack aggressively with it and no duplicate season-momentum modifier is added. (2026-09-28; M4-010, M4-011)

**2.33** Features must have a real sim impact: "why do we have these features if they dont have an impact, fix them". Decorative controls, a meter with no engine counterpart, or a call that moves nothing are defects. Every line of copy must be "directly recognisable as something which will impact the game"; no "vague yet declarative statements". (2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07; c03, Boss relay)

**2.34** Forwards and defenders: an assigned defensive forward must still kick goals; he receives forward entries substantially less often (40% / 70% figures in the transparency audit); no zero scoring eligibility or attribute cut. (2026-10-07; roadmap 02-03-16, audit 10A)

**2.35** Competent-AI rule: rival clubs coach every match by the same costs and durations as the player and never read the player's hidden plan (no-psychic-AI, 2026-09-30). AI tactical read from observed play is deferred; a plan's signature at half time is too weak to act on. Rotations run everywhere (2.60). Coach styles move reaction thresholds by at most 25% either way (0.75-1.25; fire-up at 9 or 12 down); no style switches a reaction off; from Q2 each club counters the opponent's plan when the counter is worth within 0.3 of a usual game (PlanFit.plan_score = edge x plan knowledge). Rival coach tendencies must give no free win or loss (home margin within about +-3). Game-state AI (M4-006): behind late means more aggressive, protecting a narrow lead means safer; no hidden boosts, perfect counters or guaranteed drama. (2026-09-30 to 2026-11-03; ROADMAP, RM32, SRA, M4-006)

**2.36** Weather: four conditions (perfect day, wet, windy, hot), one per match seeded by venue and month; Docklands always a perfect day; old saves load as perfect day. "Rain affects play, calibrated against real stats, and has a look." Forecast known in the week as a fact on the Hub, not advice ("Keep as built"). Windy has a breeze end per quarter. Visible "Wet-weather player" trait ("Evidence + stats": Whitfield and Dangerfield by name, stat rule 78/72 for the rest). Look: "Push them stronger". Long sleeves about 15% of a list, up to 25% in the wet. Wet calibration (#449): scoring -5 to -6%, marks -14%, clangers +10%, one-percenters +15%, accuracy about -2 points, tackles +6% against real +12% (documented). The 2026-10-06 replication figures (wet -10%) were an agent relay, not a ruling. "all four conditions; forecast in the week; breeze end yes; wet trait yes". Look has zero result effect; weather is the one look-adjacent thing that affects play. (2026-10-06; ARD-M4-016, M7-006, c05, c06)

**2.37** Weather and plan fit, director: "Certain gameplans should work better in certain weather: contested footy is better in the wet as it's less precise; in dry weather ball handling is easier and it's easier to mark the ball." Wet favours Win contest and Defensive press and hurts Attack corridor; windy favours Controlled tempo; hot favours Attack corridor and Defensive press fades; a perfect day favours Attack corridor and Controlled tempo. This is a rule the player can look up, never a recommendation label. (2026-10-06; ARD-M4-016)

**2.38** Venue and ground: assign actual event venues consistently across fixtures, match prep, live matches and vignettes; a relocated team (Gather Round) gets no normal home advantage. Home-ground familiarity is only "a **small** contextual edge. Do not overpower player/team quality." Ground dimensions subtly influence corridor use and width play (ARD-M7-007, balance-gated, TODO). (undated; roadmap 10-00, 10-04)

---

## 2.C The match view (oval, movement, ball)

**2.39** The MATCH_VIEW rule: MatchSim plays the whole match first and its event log is truth. The view decides how each event looks, never whether it happens; it never writes to an event or to MatchSim; each event is released once through event_played in log order when it visibly happens; ball x at release equals logged fp; the view uses its own seeded RNG and never the global stream or MatchSim.rng; skip_to_end applies the rest at once. Layers: Truth MatchSim.gd, Interpretation MatchDirector.gd, Motion MatchMotion.gd, Rendering PitchView.gd. (undated; MV)

**2.40** "the oval only shows what the match really does": a plan gets a shape on the oval only after measuring what it changes in the play. Tactical shape must be "visibly truthful": choices such as Flood the backline must materially change where players set up and move; verify the sim effect first and fix football behaviour rather than faking a presentation formation. No second corridor bonus because the oval depicts a corridor run. (2026-10-11 and 2026-10-06; plan_shape_impl, ARD-M8-003)

**2.41** No self-propelled or receiver-seeking ball: ball motion follows the authoritative event; no teleporting; no unexplained disposals into empty space; the view never invents a receiver the sim did not select; kicks arc, handballs stay flat and short; the ball never steers. Preserve recorded disposal types (handballs over 18 m drawn as kicks was a bug: stage a short handball then the carry). (2026-10-06; ARD-M8-003, step 1 truth fixes, #438 "Merge on green")

**2.42** Remove distant-contest wait states: play still pauses while a far-away player runs to the contest. "Collection pauses" is a repeated, unresolved "urgent regression" ("despite repeated requests to remove this behaviour"): about 33 collects a match last 1.5 s or more (cap 6 s); three timing patches were reverted; the next approach is role allocation seeing the log ahead (#857) plus kick range (#879). Smallest fix that keeps MatchSim authority; never change football outcomes for smoother animation. A simulation freeze/stall is a separate open defect. Keep the 3 m release rule. (2026-10-07 to 2026-11-03; roadmap 02-03-06, ORQ row 10)

**2.43** Disposals to apparently empty space and unexplained turnovers: show the real cause (pressure, execution error, interception, loose-ball contest) from actual events; do not invent pressure; do not ban legitimate kicks to space, make the intended receiver readable by movement and trajectory. A clear turnover cue: "Turnover" over the winner for about a second, underlined in the club colour (#617, DONE; the director's look gate is not recorded as passed). (2026-10-07; roadmap 02-03-06, ORQ rows 8, 12)

**2.44** Movement is based on real footage: most players hold shape, 6-12 work around the ball; everyone has an opponent; receivers lead, defenders trail. Restarts finish their set-up before play resumes; centre ball-ups 6-6-6 with at most four per side in the square; ball-ups only from MatchSim's explicit ballup event; kick-ins restart from the goal square with the defending side's kick. Limits: nobody faster than 15 m/s of match time; spots 5 m inside the boundary; lines keep at least 12 m spread. Match calls decide who is on whom, recorded as result["timeline"] (tactical timeline, #445). (undated; MV)

**2.45** Coach's calls on the oval (each shape moves its side at least 8 m from Balanced): Defensive press, Flood behind the ball, Stack the stoppage, Attack corridor (about 45% narrower, quicker, no switch), Controlled tempo (wide, slower), Win contest, Through stars, Throw numbers at it, Slow it down. Two faint grass markings (Attack corridor shade, Win contest ring), draw-only, look-gated ("Merge, then mark the oval", #800). The feed marks a plan when it starts, across a break, and when it ends. (undated and 2026-10-18; MV, RM30 #18)

**2.46** Your people on the oval: a light-outline ring (MatchRings) on your own players you have a say about (promised run, Through stars star, tagger, spare, defender on each forward you matched), maximum six, only in a match you coach; sim-set matchups are not rings. Surname captions only on goal-scorers (1.8 s real time) and on a ringed player on touch (1 s), goal outranks touch. "Caption and rings"; "Ringed players and scorers". Role labels only, no overlay ("No, labels only"; "Build it", #480 approved). Presentation-only: never touch MatchSim or the log. (2026-10-04 to 2026-10-06; #221, #480, MV)

**2.47** Oval identity (PitchView): tokens are discs in the club's home kit, never under 18 px; carrier and ringed players named beneath; short ball trail; stand beyond a fence band, turf edge in shadow. Match template: club-duel scoreboard, oval, one line of your plan, transport. Centre-bounce framing "Yes, as built" (#422): contest sits higher on push-in, room above the call, names off the ball. (2026-10-06; VISUAL_STYLE_GUIDE 4.6, #422)

**2.48** Judging look: stills are not enough ("virtually impossioble for me to tell from still iomages"; "1 but impossible to tell"). Motion clips are the proof for any oval or set-shot change; demonstrations come first ("these two first": Flood and Stack, #475), then lanes; the play library is held "only after the demos land". Centre ball-up vignette must be recomposed wider (establish stoppage, ruck, mids before close-up). (2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07; c06, roadmap 11-01)

**2.49** Pace: presentation runs at TEMPO 1.65x the movement clock; a full match takes about 4-5 minutes at 4x and 2-2.5 minutes at 8x. Phone: the full-time card is capped to the viewport; rotating reflows oval and feed without rebuilding the pitch. (undated; MV, DES)

---

## 2.D Taking a match: watch, skip, sim the week

**2.50** Two ways to take your match (P-14): Watch and Skip. Key moments is cut (2026-11-08: "I also think we should remove 'key moments' it's just noise and not really good"): the hub's poster offers Watch and Skip only, the match screen has no Key moments control, and a settings file that still holds Key moments opens as Watch. No switch. From 2026-10-18 to 2026-11-08 there were three ways ("Yes, add Key moments beside Watch and Skip."): Key moments ran at 8x and dropped to 1x at each goal, each coach's call and each quarter break. Skip goes straight to full time on the plan taken in (hub button "Skip to full time"; this is one match's playback, not a season fast path: Sim the week is the only fast path across rounds, "Play to Grand Final" being cut, roadmap 32 row 8). Playback only, the sim, log and result are the same; it never answers a call for the coach; setting match_way, Watch default. "Merge as shown" (#803). Rank: approachable layer, never alters the sim. (2026-10-18; RM28 #3, RM30 #21)

**2.51** Skipping rolls unsimulated quarters using the last coach-box plan then drains the log. Ordinary watching and skipping must run through the same authoritative sim. (undated; DES, genre research)

**2.52** "Play round" is renamed "Sim the week". Sim the week must halt for every mandatory coach decision (finals cards, trade period, draft, contract deadlines, pre-season blocks); none is skipped or decided for him; it is safe by default and surfaces only what needs the coach. Simulated rounds show a popup leading with your match and a Review match button that reopens the same full-time review with nothing re-simulated. (2026-10-07 and 2026-10-18; ARD-M1-007, release-1-0 task 1.5, DES)

---

## 2.E Coaching calls and pre-bounce set-up

**2.53** Each coaching gate is a contract: evidence, choice (including keeping the current approach), application (what changed, when it starts and ends, explain unavailable or failed actions), observation, reflection. Triggered by the match actually played; no quota forced into a quiet quarter; no manufactured routine pairings; resolve once so oval, log, stats and summary agree; never print success for a no-op; a later win is not proof the call caused it. Exclusions: no new decision engine, direct player control, best-choice hints, forced close finishes, hidden buffs. Football Manager lesson: reject optimal-plan hints, dense role matrices and extra shouts without evidence. (2026-10-05; M4-001, genre 4.1 and 3.3)

**2.54** Game plans: six, not eight names ("merge the duplicates", "merge to six"): Fast movement is Attack corridor, High press is Defensive press; same effects, old saves map across (#479). Plan upside was halved so Balanced is the safe call (#110); counters cut to one or two goals; execution scales only a plan's upside (EXEC_RANGE 0.25); Through stars goes through the side's best three; Controlled tempo is list-neutral. One plan description per plan, no percentages. The standing plan survives the first bounce (C1, #104). (2026-09-29 to 2026-10-06; SRA, ARD-M4-015)

**2.55** Game plan drilling: "Drill a plan, it works better" (Knows this plan: well / partly / barely); every plan slips one step over the off-season and an unplayed plan fades slowly in the year ("Slowly"). Own plan builder: "i love the idea of 3 for our club forge, but 1 for our game as is" (builder for Club Forge, unassigned; for the game as is decide after drilling is played; superseded 2026-11-12: a custom game plan is built for the game as is, see "Custom game plan" at the end). Press zone strength: "Keep three-quarters". (2026-10-13; RM26 #2, #3, #8)

**2.56** Pre-bounce and break panels: sections never change column when "Who tags" opens (A2, #749); "Best so far" is hidden until a quarter is played (A3, "best so far pre bounce makes no sense", #749); break panes are spaced out with a per-quarter record of both clubs' calls, the opponent's only where the game reveals it ("Q1: Demons: Defensive Press - Crows: Attack Corridor ...", A5, #755); tag and match-ups stay together (not split), #882, big-call "ready" bar kept (within 3 of 22nd best). Calls are taps, not dropdowns; a break shows up to three facts, surfaces the problem and never names the answer. (2026-10-14 and 2026-11-03; RM27, handoff, DES)

**2.57** Ruck selection: pre-bounce, "Assistant names one, you can change it" (A6: "make the ruck selection compulsory pre bounce"); the selected ruck goes up; the in-match Main ruck call is gone. (2026-10-14 and 2026-11-03; RM27 A6, RM32)

**2.58** Tagging: a tag the coach sets is permanent for the match ("the tag is set and permanent for the whole game"); the assistant never swaps it and the tagger is never rotated to the bench while the tag is on (A8, #758). Tagging targets opposition midfielders only (any with a midfield position incl. mid/fwd, A9; the break's named best is always offered); "no one tags ruckmen, this isnt a football thing" and "ruckmen can be stopped by other ruckmen": options fit the fact (a ruck fact is answered by the pre-bounce ruck selection, the in-match Main ruck call being cut in #874; Tag answers a midfielder fact; Key match-up a key forward; Defensive set-up a small or loose defender, #804; the old "rebounding defender" is Small defender, rule 4.8; these hub option cards were cut 2026-11-08, 9.S entry 23, and Key match-ups 2026-11-07, rule 2.61a). A ruckman is never a fallback tagger; if no eligible midfielder, say so. Tagging has a real cost: tagger keeps about 60% of usual ball share and gives up 40% of his own midfield game; tagged opponent's chance of being picked as the carrier is multiplied by 66% with the lowest-Pressure non-specialist on him, down to 50% with the highest, 42% with a Tagger, and tired legs add up to 13 points (size ruled 2026-11-10, row 120; replaces "keeps 42% not 55%"); that multiplier is not his share of disposals, which is higher and is what match-day copy must show (being measured, tools/audit/tag_keep_impl.gd); hold scales with the tagger's Pressure; a specialist tag is a gain, a non-specialist usually the wrong call, and screens say so. The assignment survives interchange without teleporting: a rested or benched midfielder keeps the tag, and bench players count by the slot they last filled (Boss relay, 2026-10-07T04:34, Task 4; relayed, not his words). (2026-09-29 to 2026-11-03; SRA C4, A8, A9, #804)

**2.59** Defensive set-up (merged Defensive forward plus Loose interceptor, #874): "Who covers X (Club)?" wording, explicit none, suitability cards, reduced-target cost line, and "How it's going" in the pane (A10, #760: contests beaten, intercepts, ratings for both). Interceptor: a structural choice (someone else absorbs his opponent, spare given up elsewhere), changeable during the match. Send-a-forward to the loose defender merged as built, forwards only (#303). Loose-man payoff: taker is the roamer only if he won the spoil (#586). (2026-10-04 to 2026-11-03; M4-004, #874)

**2.60** Rotations are automatic for both sides (MatchSim.auto_rotation) and run in AI-v-AI, Sim the week, finals and practice matches on each club's own fitness plan (no plan = Steady). The rotation policy choice, a separate Play-through call and a separate Main ruck call are cut. (2026-11-03; RM32 cuts, #874)

**2.61** Play through is three picks again (2026-11-07, "Bring them back and remove through stars game plan"): the midfield pillar, the forward target and the backline distributor, each picked at every break and before the first bounce, with a note that says what the pick does or that it waits; the coach report, the rings and the break lines read all three. The Through stars plan is removed; an old save's Through stars plays as Balanced and old pick keys still load. No switch. From 2026-11-03 (#874) to 2026-11-07 Play through was the Through stars plan. Role mechanics he ruled on #508 (2026-10-07, his model; the engine figures below were the Boss's working, relayed, never his): a forward target gets more scoring opportunities (a shooter bias inside 50, about x1.25 as a starting point, redistributing shots without inflating team scoring); a midfielder is given the ball more often (more possessions; a midfielder under focus must not become a shooter); a defender gets first use of the ball out of the back half (a back-zone carrier bias, about x1.25), which suits small defenders more than key defenders. No made-up quality modifier is allowed: good ball users already gain more ground and turn it over less through carry, disposal and kicking. The small-versus-key defender outcome must be verified by a paired-seed audit (at least 200 matches per arm) before the "clean user of the ball" sentence ships. His question "why is a forward target not made the shooter" is the rule: a role line must state a real effect, never a disclaimer. (2026-11-03; RM32; user_6da0c968 2026-10-07T04:06 and 05:44)

**2.61a** Key match-ups are cut (2026-11-07: "I think we should remove 'key matchups' from the game and replace with the player roles section"; he chose "Roles replace match-ups"). The coach no longer puts a defender on a forward: not before the bounce, not at a break, not on an in-match card, and the coach report has no match-ups line. The duels the sim sets up for itself stay. The three player roles (2.61) take the slot Key match-ups had on the set-up screen. An old save's match-ups are ignored. The design bible's match-day Individual row now reads "A player role, a positional switch" (his wording).

**2.62** Assistant coach: your assistant makes routine calls by the rivals' rules in simulated rounds and live matches ("assistant takes routine calls"; "everywhere; yours always win"); anything the player sets overrides it; worth about -0.1 points a match, a convenience not an edge. Routine KPF/KPD pairings automatic; special match-up decisions only when meaningful; a match may have none. (2026-10-04; COMPETITIVE_BALANCE 12, M4-002)

**2.63** Tired star (SUPERSEDED by automatic rotations for both sides, MatchSim.auto_rotation, roadmap 32 rows 2-5, 2026-11-03; the Rest and Keep prompt is no longer offered, and the coach has no manual rotation override, see 2.60; the earlier wording is kept for the record, see 2.S24): "make Rest and Keep a meaningful trade-off": Rest brings a bench player on and the star stays out to the break (final siren in Q4), starting fresh next quarter; Keep stays on however cooked and starts the next quarter tired; both hold only to the break; the card names who comes on and for how long; only offered when a bench player can come on; AI clubs are never asked; the break reports follow-through. (2026-10-05; TIRED_CALL_AUDIT)

**2.64** Pep talks: four options (#624 added "Bring the heat"); every talk, default included, shows its actual effect and trade-off, no percentages. Fire them up lifts the side only while behind on the scoreboard; Calm the group gives up ground (x0.92) and pays only when rattled. Balance size of the fourth talk is unmeasured. (2026-09-29 to 2026-10-07; SRA, ORQ row 3)

**2.65** Comebacks: keep them, feature them narratively rather than by frequency, but make them believable ("not seem like the game hallucinating"; a 52-point last-quarter swing is possible but "very very very unlikely"). Narrative done (#716, #723, #731); rules with numbers follow the audit on unmerged claude/comeback-audit-row42; balance is his call. (2026-10-13; RM26 #9, ORQ row 42)

**2.66** Late-game centre ball-up call: at most once per match regardless of margin (#492), equal option styling (#496). The opposition goal-run prompt must not fire mechanically after every goal of a streak and must not repeat while nothing changed. (2026-10-07; roadmap 02-03-04, 02-03-20)

**2.67** Tactical vignettes (the requirement is the director's; coverage is not yet met and remains open, see 2.Q12): every in-match decision requires a vignette when vignettes are enabled ("every in-match decision requires a vignette when vignettes are enabled"). Candidates are decision gates (freeze before the call, meaningful alternatives, result from the authoritative roll the vignette "must consume that result, never reroll it"), small reusable templates, enlarged 2D/2.5D only. MatchSim remains the authority. (2026-10-06 to 2026-10-07; roadmap 11-04, 02-03-09)

---

## 2.F After the whistle: what the player is told

**2.68** Facts must be ones the coach can act on: "why tell me Rankine is their best player if i cant do anything about it?"; "X is hurting you" appears only when a call reaches him, else a plain fact. Rival cards show only public facts ("why can i see rival player load? hes the only one ive noticed, seems random"): private load, fitness, mood, rest, promises, training plan hidden; injuries stay public (A26, #759). (2026-10-05 and 2026-10-14; RM27, #230)

**2.69** Reports inform and never prescribe: Assistant's Report (#500, #505) lists "How they play" and "Who matters" with no recommended tactic ("Must not tell the player how to win"); the half-time assistant report is replaced by full stats at every break (5.164 verdict, #507); wherever a break report speaks, it states problems in the assistant's words, never the call. No under-performer judgement from low disposals without role and time on ground; "Needs a lift" must not include injured players. (2026-10-07; roadmap 02-03-07, 02-03-18, DES)

**2.70** Break and match stats: box score approved ("1. looks great. but is there a box score as well?"). Best players show actual coaches votes (5-4-3-2-1 from each coach, per match, distinct from the season B&F count of 5.121 and 5.28 which uses only retained votes; "show the actual coaches votes"; unassigned). Best-players panel uses friendly versus opposition colour language by allegiance. Injury wording full ("hand injury"; OPEN defect: the match-summary injury line still reads "to a hand", row 18 of roadmap/27); scoring-run lines must say when and give the score before and after. (2026-10-07 and 2026-10-14; #507, RM27 B6, roadmap 02-03-03)

**2.71** "What your calls did" must be clear: lead with a football assessment ("Sam kept McStay quiet after the switch") only when evidence supports it, otherwise "Too early to judge"; label the matchup and period; a full-time summary of what each call was worth. Show intercepts beside each named loose defender, both teams, live. Match summary injury "3 of 5" style counts must be explained. (2026-10-07 and 2026-10-11; roadmap 02-03-13, 02-03-11)

---

## 2.G Filed under T2 but owned by other chapters (kept so no decision is lost)

**2.72** Positions: Unicorn trait awarded automatically for learning a forward, mid and back position; POT gates (second position POT 70+, third POT 90 after "Rarer Unicorns instead"); key-position roles locked by size; AI clubs learn extra positions "sparingly"; once a career, best 22 and at or above the position bar. Synergies must be harder ("synergies should be harder to achieve"); target 15-25% of clubs after a season, "Reachability, not power". (2026-10-05; c01, c02, user_5fef3e70)

**2.73** Season shape at 21 clubs: "24 rounds 2 byes" (22 games each; byes in rounds 9-20; home within one of half). Sir Doug Nicholls Round: a club with a bye wears its special guernsey the next round. Tough leadership trait idea, dominant foot (no hidden accuracy bonus), flags (trade volume), "The unmanaged collapse is about right." (2026-10-06; c03/c04, roadmap 10-02 to 10-06)

**2.74** The match view goes 3D (2026-11-14). Shown a mock of the live oval as a real 3D scene (our own MPFB footballers running where MatchDirector puts them, jog, idle and carry from the sprite pipeline, the ball between the palms), he chose "True 3D oval" and then "Yes, pursue it"; "Work hand in hand with 3d view 2" (the session that wrote docs/research/AFL2004_ANIMATION_STUDY.md, the 3D animation plan). This supersedes the rejection of real-time 3D figures (rule 6.39, 6.S item 17) and ARD-M8-007's "Do not introduce 3D player models" for the match view's oval. Everything in 2.39-2.49 still holds: MatchSim's log is truth, the view never changes an outcome, no hovering or self-propelled ball, motion copied from real footage, clips judge the look. The vignettes are not part of this ruling. First comes the proof passage (study §2.10), measured on an Android phone. Motion for the AFL moves (kick, handball, gather on the run, marks, ruck tap) comes from a free mocap service turning our own video into 3D motion: asked where it should come from, he chose "Free mocap service" (2026-11-14); he creates the account, and the same day he set the pipeline: "you dont need mixamo"; "I am going to send you a shittone of afl footage, you should extract usable clips which I will then convert into animations with Rokoko"; "This is the new pipeline" (2026-11-14). The animation session cuts each move from his footage into its own clip; a clip may hold other people, who are removed in post ("we can remove other people from the footage in post"). He converts the clips to motion with Rokoko, and the animation session retargets that motion onto our rig. The raw footage and Rokoko files stay out of the repository (only our retargeted clips ship).
