# Chapter 5. Career, story, RPG layer, press, coach arcs, board, history, records, flavour and culture

How to read this chapter. Each rule carries (date; source). "undated" means the record had no date. Words in quotation
marks are the director's own, copied unaltered; where a record has none, the rule is the recorded decision in plain words.
Conflicts are settled by one rule: the later director decision wins, and the 2026-11-03 story-first order ranks
everything (1 the game is a story generator, 2 it is a deep simulator, 3 it is an approachable game). Status words
(built, behind a switch, unplayed) describe the state when the record was written and are not a director verdict.
Balance is the director's to call; nothing here declares any number settled.

## 5.1 The story layer: what it is and its guardrails

1. The vision is "a simulation that feels like a story, a legacy, a dynasty". Defining moment: a player you took with a risky draft pick wins the Brownlow; the director's words: "A player you took with a risky draft pick wins the Brownlow, the satisfaction that your vision and faith paid off." The same pick can fail and the game stays objective about it. (2026-10-09; DESIGN_BIBLE, brainstorm#The moment in play)
2. North star: players, coaches, game plans and matchups and in-match decisions together make a realistic sports-RPG with satisfying strategy, realistic and deep (yet accessible, not overbearing) simulation, and rich emergent storytelling, never at the cost of MatchSim's veracity. MatchSim stays the physics; the RPG feel is built around it from identity, commitment, memory, information and presentation. (2026-10-05; memory/rpg-feel-vision)
3. The story is the coach's career: "the coach's career (the player)". Arcs centre on the coach: hot seat and sacking, being poached, board feud, protege rising, veteran kept too long, the drought, legacy. (2026-11-03; ch32#3)
4. The player is a coach and every decision is roleplaying: event calls, in-game moments (defend now or attack now?), press-conference answers, the look of Forge creations, and list-management strategy. (2026-10-09; DESIGN_BIBLE#The coach)
5. What the coach says or decides can affect the sim. How the club looks or sounds never does. Weather is the one exception and should matter; its look does not. (2026-10-09; DESIGN_BIBLE#The coach, CLAUDE.md)
6. Matches are quests: emergent storytelling is a core goal. After a match the player can recount turning points and defining players without scripted drama. Not every match is forced into drama; comfortable wins and ugly losses are allowed. (2026-09-29; roadmap 0.2)
7. A coach understands the problem, chooses a feasible response, sees it applied and observes consequences that may help, hurt or stay inconclusive. No hidden bonuses, best-move recommendations or arbitrary scripted drama. (2026-10-05; roadmap 0.4.2)
8. Reference games inform, they do not authorise frameworks. Crusader Kings informs factual character continuity (persistent identities and inspectable history across generations with restrained complexity); Esoteric Ebb informs simple characterful questions with logical answer feedback and restrained humour. Neither authorises a relationship or dialogue framework. Refine existing match gates, media and history first. (2026-10-05; roadmap 0.4.2, DESIGN.md#Career direction)
9. Effects must follow from the story. The director on the "They say Rhys Johnson is in the wrong spot" card (PR #692): "why would the players morale and trust drop because you played him in another position, doesnt make sense". His standing order: "be more vigilant in catching arbitrary things like that". Before any card, event or choice reaches him or merges, ask "given this premise, why would that happen?" for every Gain and Cost; builders list the premise beside the effects in the PR body; the test applies to any rule or number that could look arbitrary and to Claude's own facts in questions (an example was a "2025 finish" asked about when the career's last year is 2026). (2026-10-14; memory/effects-must-follow-the-story)
10. The fun clusters around people and time: the youth intake day, a prospect becoming a star, a departed player returning as an enemy. Emergent narrative needs surfaces that reflect it back (feeds, histories, club pages). This is the basis for the People remembered bundle. (2026-10-18; PLAYER.md#Q1 inference)
11. Repetitive mandatory interactions get delegated; a system universally delegated has failed as content. Make it consequential and rare or do not ship it. This is the basis for the rule that press conferences fire only when something happened (rule 5.52). (2026-10-18; PLAYER.md#Q2 inference)
12. Reward and consequence must be real. An amusing line with an ignored answer fails the agency test; improve one existing question and its actual consequence before expanding content. Reject a personality quiz that assigns arbitrary permanent buffs, a separate dialogue engine, mandatory conferences, and authored drama that contradicts the sim. The director "enjoys simple questions whose answers have logical, amusing or insightful gameplay effects" and supports concise football decisions with character, not a text-heavy RPG layer. (2026-10-05; GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH#3.12)
13. RPG work may carry its specified gameplay consequences; FL-001 to FL-008 flavour keeps its separate zero-gameplay-effect rule (section 14). Later approval supersedes earlier research-only exclusions of remembered media or private conversations and the season narrative, only for the scopes listed; unselected research candidates stay unselected. (2026-10-06; ch22 (9.4) header)
14. Decision gates in the story layer follow the bible's transparency principle: every gate says what each option does and costs, with size and odds in football words, and never says which option is better. The director (2026-10-18): "I still want the football language, but I also want full transparency behind each decision and the impact it will have"; "the decision should still be relatively opaque and both options carrying positives and negatives"; wording approved as written. (2026-10-18; DESIGN_BIBLE principle 4)
15. Event choices present meaningful trade-offs, not one objectively dominant choice; outcomes are concise and football or career relevant. Fix observed bad events first; do not redesign the whole event system. PR #44 audited eight weekly club-life events. (2026-09-28; ARD-M1-011)
16. Where not acting has no natural consequence, an unanswered weekly card changes nothing ("neutral defaults": no free recovery week, closed-session XP or group lift). Where not acting has a natural consequence it stands: a sore star plays sore, a contract request waits for the off-season, board patience thins, and silence on an off-field incident costs board confidence -4 and the card says so. (2026-10-04; COMPETITIVE_BALANCE#8)
17. Story-deck facts as audited (state at 2026-10-18, not director rulings): heavy legs 17 cards (88 first-bounce energy, injury x1.25 all kinds); fresh legs 11 cards (x0.75); sore play x6 injury and 90 legs; slow start 20% for 82 energy; promise expects_game -12 morale -8 trust; backing run -10 and -10; press conference: the code rolls each answer (seeded, odds shown); settled; pre-season Load x Focus x 3 blocks; weather effects (wet marks x0.90, pressure x1.18, clangers x1.15, hot drain x1.12); Wing x1.6 open play x0.4 stoppage. The club pick screen (S26) was not audited. (2026-10-18; DECISION_TRANSPARENCY_AUDIT#10D)

## 5.2 Coach career, sacking, offers and arcs

18. The story is the coach's career and its arcs sit beside the random decks, not in place of them. "keep it, and layer coach arcs on top": the 12 random story decks stay; multi-season arc cards are drawn alongside and remember named players and clubs (plan step 1a). (2026-11-03; ch32#4)
19. The coach is a light record with elements of a full career. The director (2026-10-04): "option 1 with elements of option 3. I like the idea of you getting sacked but having the chance to coach a weaker club, maybe you only get one second chance (and winning a premiership renews that chance/life (so to speak, to use video game vernacular))". (2026-10-04; user_491246e9, memory/rpg-feel-vision)
20. After a sacking with a life left, the coach is offered "A choice of a few" clubs with different problems, facts only. (2026-10-04; user_491246e9)
21. Sacking and second chance, as specified: being sacked does not end the save; the player continues at another club if hired; one second chance after the first sacking, and a later sacking normally ends the coaching career; a premiership earns or restores one additional reprieve ("get out of jail"); the remaining reprieve and the consequence of the next sacking must be clear, not hidden behind an opaque board score. (undated; ARD-M6-003)
22. Sacked is not the end: the hub offers jobs open that off-season (usually near the bottom) or retirement with the record read out. Today HubScene._sacked_card() only offers Season review and Main menu. Authorised in the People remembered bundle ("a sacking stops being the end"). (2026-10-18; CPC C-PC-12, ch29#3)
23. Other clubs come calling: after a strong season a rival board offers the job in one letter with club, list in words, board goal and risk stated. Take it and the old club keeps living; decline and nothing changes; no coaching two clubs. Part of the job-letter item of bundle 3. (2026-10-18; CPC C-PC-11)
24. Coach arcs (hot seat and sacking, poached, board feud, protege, veteran kept too long, drought, legacy; coach moves clubs) are built behind the coach_arcs switch (#867, #877, #881, #886), off until the director plays a career. (2026-11-03; open-requirements row 48)
25. A reunion beat recurs at most once per 10 seasons per club; flag clubs get a 10-year premiership reunion (PR #886). (2026-11-03; handoff scratch-2026-11-02)
26. Coaching hierarchy (director request, TODO P1): Head coach, Head assistant, other assistants; the Head assistant is a distinct senior appointment, not cosmetic; one head coach and at most one head assistant per club; no additional ranks; the AI market is consistent. (2026-10-07; roadmap 09-01)
27. Opportunity-based retention: when a rival approaches one of the user's coaches the user can Make counter-offer or Allow departure before it is finalised. "This retention negotiation is not a monetary bidding system": no salary sliders or cash counters; the currency is opportunity, a promotion to Head assistant or a Head coach succession plan with an explicit timeframe or condition. Never silently demote or displace incumbents or appoint two Head assistants; succession must not auto-replace the user or be an empty promise; unfulfilled promises have legible trust consequences; resolve using ambition, hierarchy, credibility, competing role and loyalty; a rival move is held pending response and resolved exactly once; AI clubs follow the same rules with no hidden advantage; avoid bidding loops and negotiations for every staff member; the default timeout rule is explicit. (2026-10-07; roadmap 09-01)
28. Coaching staff gameplay (done, phases 2-5, #62 #74 #77): Teaching to development, Tactics to match performance, Man-management to morale and selection response. Effects are modest and capped; no generic "+10% everything" staff bonuses; role fit, career histories, succession and save compatibility count. (2026-09-28; ARD-M6-002)
29. RPG-006 slice 1 (director decision): staff move the training ceiling (3 plus up to 1 from teachers, down to 2 with a vacant or poor staff), not the XP; same rule for AI clubs. The tactics lever and a staff-identity line are not done; a tactics lever would be its own match-balance decision. (2026-10-09; RPG006_STAFF_IDENTITY_AUDIT)
30. Former-player coaching pathway (done): one deterministic career-seeded roll, same player name or alias linked to the playing career; 1-3 pathway seasons then the ordinary market; playing ability does not determine coaching skill (skills 52-72 independent); fame raises starting reputation only and fades over about a decade; a former club gets a small bounded hiring advantage; same rules as other coaches. As built: 19% base, 22% from 150 games, 25% from 250, plus 1 point per major award, cap 30%; news when a notable former player joins the ranks. Test band 20-30% entry; aspiration of 60-80% of coaching jobs held by former players (not a hard pass or fail); generated coaches are top-up supply; do not inflate conversion to hide the roughly 19 career endings per season (a separate lifecycle problem). Measured: a real 32-season run had 23% of endings coached and former players held 86% of jobs by 2058; if the share keeps climbing, the coaching rate is the lever (lower BASE_INTEREST first if past 80%). (2026-09-28; roadmap 09-01 Phase 4, DESIGN.md)
31. Alumni link (RC-004, "Director decision: ACCEPTED within existing owners."): one factual connection when an actual former player enters coaching, linking his previous club stint and genuine achievement to the coach profile. No mandatory return to the user's club, new relationship simulation, guaranteed coach role or fabricated personality arc. Verified (PR #234): a former player's coach profile shows "Playing career" (clubs, games, goals, draft, medals including Rising Star, Coaches Award, club best and fairests); other clubs' B&Fs are not kept so not claimed; the chance of coaching entry still counts Brownlows and Colemans only. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH#7, ARD-RC-004)
32. Coach-market visibility audit (done): the market is plausible (about 3.1 senior-coach changes and about 17 job moves a season); the problem was visibility. Do not tune to guarantee a carousel each year; quiet off-seasons are believable. Surface notable ins and outs in the post-draft summary, major appointments and sackings, and any coach poached from the user's club as an explicit event requiring a response. The Hub surfaces staff departures and vacancies with a path to appoint a replacement. (2026-10-01; roadmap 09-01)

## 5.3 The weekly rhythm: cards, the big call, interruptions, story calendar

33. A short weekly checklist returns: "yes: a short weekly checklist with one big arc-linked call". Each Hub week shows one big call (drop an out-of-form star? play the kid?) plus routine jobs. The Hub's "There is no weekly checklist" text goes (plan step 3a). (2026-11-03; ch32#6)
34. The weekly big call (built, behind weekly_checklist and coach_arcs, both off): a due coach-arc beat (ClubArcs.big_call), else the most pressing of a pending press question, an open assistant approach, no captain named, a young player ready for backing (within 3 of the list's 22nd best), or the pre-season captaincy review (BigCalls.gd). One per week, in week_checks, ticked when dealt with. (2026-11-03; ch32#Cuts shipped)
35. Weekly cards stay alongside storylines: "Keep both". Cards ask a question; storylines never do. (2026-10-04; user_491246e9)
36. Interruption pacing (G1, decided): at most one optional (flavour) ask a week; new RPG surfaces share that slot with the press question; urgent asks (tribunal challenge, contract windows, retirement persuasion) always show; the weekly card is a coaching choice, not flavour. Losing runs: no change, slumps behave like any other week. (2026-10-09; ch22#G1, RPG_G1_INTERRUPTION_AUDIT #553)
37. Story calendar (done): pre-season has its own block of story weeks before Round 1; StoryEngine.week_abs(year, week) = (year-2000)*52 + 12 + 10 + week; cooldowns count played weeks only (pre-season, rounds, finals), never off-season ("own week block"); old saves convert on load. (2026-11-03; ch32, handoff)
38. Event-card audit rules (row 34): every card is audited for a choice that does nothing, a card never seen in a 10-season career, and wording (scrutable: a card keeps what it promises, football language, stakes true). A card that promised to tell the coach who is fit and named nobody ("The fitness staff want a time trial") was fixed (#678). Audit done #680, costs #688 #818 #830; a 62-line copy sheet (39 cards) goes to the director as one sheet. (2026-10-12; open-requirements row 34)
39. Story-card costs: every option with no downside gets one. The director "Approve all 17" costs (10-15% slow start, selection promise stings, board confidence down 2%, one morale point, role-trial rating gap), with cost lines generated from the effect as a percentage; 33 gain-only options are pinned as a shrinking allowlist. (2026-10-18; ch30#30)
40. Six optional (+) upsides on hard options of rows 3, 6, 7, 8, 9 ("Yes, all six"), for example a player who trains instead gains development worth 11-16% of a senior game; row 3 "earn it" included. (2026-10-18; ch30#31)
41. Pre-season story cards (11, #774): "Approve all 11"; both sides on every option (card 2 "wait" gains open price, card 3 "hold the weight" costs slower growth); gate format per ch30 row 27. (2026-10-18; ch30#24)
42. Event decks share one existing lever for "flat start" or "plays below himself" risks, not a new bespoke effect each. (2026-10-13; ch26#4)

## 5.4 Press conferences and journalists

43. Press answers affect the sim: player morale, board expectations and relationships with the press. Journalists have personalities. "An answer is a dice roll based on logic"; the gamer should know a comment carries risk. (2026-10-09; DESIGN_BIBLE#Press conferences)
44. The conference tests media literacy, relationship management and board compliance. The effect is visible afterwards (criticise a young player and his morale may drop or his resolve may tighten); the gamer sees their words had impact and adjusts or carries on. (2026-10-09; DESIGN_BIBLE)
45. Current ruling on the dice: "Code follows the bible". Each press answer becomes a seeded roll; the gate shows each answer's odds and both outcomes as percentages (22:2x); W7 review applies. This replaces the earlier "direction the copy promises" guardrail wherever they disagree. Still to design per ARD-M6-008 note: how risk is shown without number vomit, what decides which way it lands, where the result is seen. (2026-10-18; ch30#33, ch28#22, roadmap 09-07)
46. Press answers keep their board and morale effects, now shown under each answer: "Director: press-conference answers keep their board/morale effects, now shown under each answer (reverses the earlier flavour-only call)." The director's request included QC on a phone (390x844 at 3x) and on his PC (3840x2160 at 300%). (2026-10-10; ch20#director-decisions, capture_press_effects)
47. Answer wording must clearly cause its effect. The director: "not sure why the players would be felt left out to dry by that comment? im ok with the effects, but the wording should be harsher on the players"; and "how is this not taking accountability? make it more obvious". All 14 answer lines were reworded (#754); every press question is audited the same way. (2026-10-14; ch27#A19)
48. The Stats Geek checks an answer against your record ("P-43: yes", Yes): the set of effects stays but which one an answer earns can depend on the record; a boast the record contradicts costs you, one it supports lands harder. This supersedes the "effects unchanged" note on ch28 decision 2. (2026-10-18; ch29#10)
49. Recurring journalists with stable identities, five approved types: The Muckraker (controversy, contradictions, accountability from actual evidence); The Sycophant (flattering framing, no automatic free rewards); The Stats Geek (trustworthy stats, no invented causality); The Bogan (blunt supporter view, restrained colloquial humour); The Philosopher (club identity and long-term choices, brief). Different personalities ask and follow up differently; neutral or noncommittal answers and neutral skip are legitimate; no compulsory weekly conference. Journalists done (#559). (2026-10-06; ch22#RPG-002, open-requirements row 60)
50. Journalist answers must not always repeat the same board-versus-morale transaction; a substantive consequence can be a relevant effect, an explicit commitment, or a remembered stance with real follow-through. Questions are grounded in actual events, with no fabricated allegations or praise, and personality is perceptible through dialogue and presentation, not a hidden modifier. (2026-10-06, 2026-10-07; ch22#RPG-002, roadmap 02-03-01)
51. Press questions draw on actual match and career context (performances, tactical decisions, comebacks and runs, injuries when salient, debuts, rivalries), use question history and cooldowns without suppressing an important recurring story, have distinct response attitudes and coherent follow-ups, keep the UI compact, and are verified across a season and older saves. The director's complaint: the same injury tree ("You lost [player] during the game. How much did that affect the result?") almost every conference; broaden substance and branches, not paraphrase; audit repetition so a common injury does not crowd out other stories. (2026-10-07; roadmap 02-03-01)
52. Press conferences fire only when something happened: "Yes, press conferences only when something happened." A reason the journalist can name (big result, debut, star's injury, selection shock, streak), never a schedule; fewer, weightier pressers. The code already fires on a notable trigger with a cooldown (MediaConference.gd); remaining work is question variety over long saves and the trigger rate per season (no seeded audit yet). (2026-10-18; ch28#2, ch29#Rules, open-requirements row 19)
53. Post-match conference design (on main #183/#208): a short dramatic vignette; the journalist puts a pointed question from what actually happened; 2-4 multiple-choice answers, one tap, skippable; every answer a real trade-off with no "correct" or do-nothing option; outcome in football language not numbers; occasion-only (big wins and losses, milestones, controversy), no repeat in a short span; 360-390px, Android Back. (undated; ARD-M6-008) 'Outcome in football language not numbers' superseded by 5.45 (odds and sizes as percentages).
54. Media guardrails: effects are modest and decay and cannot outweigh results, selection or coaching; natural AFL language; skip is always possible with a defined neutral outcome. Exclusions: a new dialogue engine, personality quiz, compulsory weekly conferences, permanent coach buffs; do not duplicate the #220/#226 backing promise and payoff. (undated, refinement 2026-10-05; roadmap 09-07)
55. One football answer remembered later (RC-005, "Director decision: ACCEPTED within existing owners."): one media question, its immediate morale and board effect, then one brief later factual reference when saved context makes it relevant. No new promise system, dialogue framework, ~~personality score~~ (superseded in part by the 2026-11-06 ruling, 5.4 entry 56a) or gameplay bonus, and no fabricated caused win, grudge or private motive. (2026-10-05; GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH#7)
56. Pre-season papers (media): "Predict, and you answer" (see section 7).
56a. **Press relationships and personality (2026-11-06).** Rulings on the row 105 press report. Q1 relationships: "Yes I've said this like a hundred times." Each journalist's standing with you runs -2..+2, moved only by your answers (by his stance; the Stats Geek's by the record check), shifting his odds and how he opens the question; skip is neutral. Q2 personality changes what an answer does, shown openly on the gate, never a hidden modifier. Q3 an answer can back or criticise one named player so only his morale moves. Q4: an answer's morale move is capped below a match result's own, and press effects fade (54). How "below" is measured is still open: the sizes in the build (list +-2, one named player +-3, board +-2, each fading a point a round) are the agent's, not a ruling; the star question needs a real standout (6 goals or 40 disposals), so an ordinary game asks nothing; the press reply is added to the board's match reason instead of overwriting it; a full-screen press scene with the result shown in it (ruling 3.52; ordered here, built on the row 105 branch as PressScene); the Stats Geek record check (48: a boast the record contradicts costs you, one it supports lands harder). The answer is a seeded roll with odds shown (45). Do not ask again. **2026-11-07 rulings** (on the press scene review): the look is approved; each answer reads as one short line on a portrait phone, meaning and stated risk kept. Morale after a loss: an answer can lift a player up to just below what a win would have given him (a win +4, a loss +1, so +2 at most; this replaces the Q4 cap). Board: a press answer can move the board's view further than the match result moved it (no cap tied to the match; board stacking limit 3); the press reason is still added to the board's reason. The Philosopher (0 questions in 93 conferences) asks in about one conference in six: "21 or younger" now counts a player until his 22nd birthday, and she waits 7 rounds between questions.

## 5.5 The board

57. Board confidence is a qualitative, persistent state: Very secure 80+, Secure 62+, Stable 45+, Under pressure 30+, In trouble below; it moves slowly relative to expectations, explains why it moved, with no opaque random swings. The hub line reads "Board: Under pressure" style. Goal steps: top four +2/-3, finals +2/-2, top 12 +2/-2, seven wins +3/-1; +1 for a 40-point margin, and after three losses in a row each further loss costs one more. Smaller inputs (finals, development, cap health, trend) wait until the base model is proven. Merged PR #84. (2026-09-28; ARD-M6-003)
58. Board warning: "Warn at 40". The warning with real numbers fires under 40 on every board; "Warn at 40, labels keep their line": "In trouble" keeps each dial's own line so Patient and Fair stay "Under pressure" to 30 (#824); Ruthless is harsher only in how fast confidence falls. (2026-10-18; ch30#34, #37)
59. The board target never moves from talking the club up (see rule 5.79). Press answers and finals cards reward the board only where the board is the point (rule 5.90). (2026-10-13; ch26#2, ch26#4)
60. A job-security consequence line stays explained: the sacking path (rule 5.21) shows its remaining reprieve plainly. (2026-09-28; ARD-M6-003)

## 5.6 Club culture

61. Culture effects (director, 2026-10-13): "standards: fitness gains / togetherness: morale / ambition: more likely to retain players and attract recruits". The Standards effect is "Recovery between games". The slack side also costs: "Both ways". (2026-10-13; ch26#5)
62. Culture gets real effects: "Build the effects", per lead/culture_effects_sheet.md; W7 review; calibration floors untouched; the director's playtest decides. This supersedes the earlier "Small, then measure" stance (Culture.gd header said no effect yet). (2026-10-18; ch30#4)
63. Celebrating a flag properly means the list starts next pre-season a little behind in fitness (finals card B8, rule 5.85). (2026-10-13; ch26#2)

## 5.7 Pre-season (patch 0.2.0 "Pre-season")

64. The director's reason: "a glaring hole in our game: pre season, a period of development, narratives, media speculation, fitness building, game-plan honing etc"; stretch goal "create your own gameplan". It is the 0.2.0 headline. (2026-10-13; ch26#2, preseason_rulings)
65. Eight weeks, full detail "but streamline it a touch". Flow: "Plan in blocks, stop at events ... but reduce it to just 1 pre season match". A new career gets "Yes, the same eight weeks". Sim to round 1 "Yes, on the settings in force". "Keep both" for the time-trial stop and the mid-season card. (2026-10-13; preseason_rulings)
66. The practice match: "Trial players and plans, no ladder"; "Drill a plan, it works better". One pre-season practice match at the end of week 7. All five slices are built and on since 1.0.1 (#706 #730 #740 #747 #734; one match #872 #875; papers and 11 cards #837; #843; story calendar #889). Open: the director's playtest of balance sizes. (2026-10-13, 2026-11-03; open-requirements row 39, release-1-0 plan 2.2)
67. Papers (media): "Predict, and you answer". Talking the club up: "A reckoning later, no new target": the board's target never moves; it lifts board mood now and brings a card mid-season if behind your own words. Papers (#773) have 5 design calls going in a single interview with the 11 cards (#774). (2026-10-13; ch26#2)
67a. Papers and the practice match (director, 2026-11-12, "Nudge it"): the practice result moves the papers' ladder. A comfortable practice win lifts a club a place or two, a heavy loss drops it; every club the same. The sizes are his to tune (start: a win by 24+ points, four goals, up one; 42+, seven goals, up two; a loss by the same down one or two; closer, a draw or no match, no move). The paper says so beside a club it moved ("Up a spot on the back of a 30-point practice win"). The board's goal still never moves (67). (2026-11-12)
68. Papers role trial: three games in a new role then home; the result card is measured against averages of a player in that position; on success position training starts with three weeks banked; the card stands alone, shows for an out-of-form player only, and the button names the position. (2026-10-13; ch26#6)
69. Practice-match look gate (#747) stays with the director. The intra-club match with invited prospects is cut (see Superseded). (2026-11-03; release-1-0 plan 2.2)

## 5.8 Finals and Grand Final cards

70. A whole bespoke set of finals events fires only in finals week (elimination and qualifying week, the week after a season-ending loss, preliminary-final build-up, bye week, club's first final in years, a veteran's last final) with real stakes and finals mood in copy. Slice 1 live (#709); slice 2 built (#864) behind finals_cards_2 (off) until the director plays it. (2026-10-12; open-requirements row 35)
71. A small set of Grand Final events makes each Grand Final unique, keyed to what is true of this one (opponent, captain's last game, kid's first season, injured star's fitness test, parade, week's press, result's aftermath) so no two Grand Final weeks read the same. (2026-10-12; open-requirements row 36)
72. All 25 finals and Grand Final cards were ruled card by card ("Yes, swap and tell me"). Every card shows Gain and Cost lines per choice (#688 style). (2026-10-13; ch26#4)
73. Board rule for the cards: replace filler board rewards with Standards, Togetherness, trust or development as fits the moment, and mark every swap for the director; the board stays only where it is the point (tickets, greats, Grand Final week parade and sponsors). Board gains removed or swapped again 2026-11-03 for the first-final and prelim cards; Morale +3 and +4 removed leaving Togetherness only. (2026-10-13, 2026-11-03; finals_cards_rulings, finals_cards_2_add.py)
74. Approved as written: A1 "We're in", A2 "No finals this year", A5 "First final", A11 "Four tickets", A12 "The club's greats", B2, B4, B5, B6, B7 ("Approve"; A11, A12, B4, B5 "Keep both"). A3 "Our first final since {year}" fires after 3+ years without a final ("Approve, three years not five"). (2026-10-13; finals_cards_rulings)
75. A4 "Could be his last season": "Approve, add a risk to telling them": telling the group lifts the side but carries a small chance morale falls if you then lose that final; the risk shows in the line. (2026-10-13; finals_cards_rulings#A4)
76. A6 week off while wildcards play: "Approve, add rust": resting means fewer injuries next match and a small chance of a flat first quarter; check for an existing lever first. A7 is folded into A6 ("Fold it into card 6"): one week-off card at either bye, with "Watch the semi together" (morale up across the list) as a third choice when the qualifying final was won. (2026-10-13; finals_cards_rulings)
77. A8 Beaten but still alive: "Approve, the captain can take it": third choice "Leave it to the captain" goes one of two ways by how the group rates him (captain-card delegate rule, with "See the rule"). A13 Captain's last word: "Approve, his standing decides": how much the side lifts depends on how the group rates the captain. (2026-10-13; finals_cards_rulings)
78. A9 One win from the Grand Final: "the board isnt relevant, the bonus should be increased standards": dreaming carries a small chance of a flat prelim start; "No Grand Final talk at the club" gains Standards, not board approval; cost stays. Sized Standards +2 (slice 2, behind finals_cards_2). (2026-10-13, 2026-11-03; finals_cards_rulings#A9, ch32#9)
79. A10 Fitness test: "Approve, he has a say": the text says what the player wants (he says he is right to go); ruling him out against his word costs more trust. (2026-10-13; finals_cards_rulings#A10)
80. A14 "Back your players in the press": "Approve, without the board": morale up a little plus Togetherness; "Pin the tips up" is a bigger morale lift only. Sized Togetherness +2 ("Back your players"). A16 One week short: "Nobody's spot is safe" = Standards rises, morale down; no board. (2026-10-13, 2026-11-03; finals_cards_rulings, ch32#9)
81. A15 Knocked out: "Approve, even it up": "Thank them for the year" = Togetherness only (no morale); "Tell them what next year demands" = Standards rises, morale down a little. B9 Runners-up: "Let it go" Togetherness only; "Put the photo up" Ambition rises, morale down. (2026-10-13; finals_cards_rulings)
82. B1 opponent in the Grand Final: "Approve, about them risks nerves": "Make it about them" = morale up a lot plus a small chance of a flat start; "Make it about us" = develops a little, morale up a little. B3 Grand Final in first season: "Approve, enjoying it has a risk": "Let him enjoy the week" = his morale up a lot plus a small chance he plays below himself; "Keep him away" = morale up a little, trust more. (2026-10-13; finals_cards_rulings)
83. A3 "Keep the noise outside": "swap it for Standards +1"; no card of the eight rewards the board. (2026-11-03; ch32#11)
84. All 16 finals cards were approved with fixes: the captain's last word makes no morale change when the side won't listen; Grand Final cards say "Saturday" not "next final"; the old-rival card names the real Grand Final opponent; finals buttons fit a phone (#709 merged). (2026-10-14; ch27#B4)
85. B8 Premiers: "Approve, celebrating costs fitness": "Celebrate it properly" = morale up a lot, Togetherness rises, list starts next pre-season a little behind in fitness; "One week, then go again" = morale up, Ambition rises, no cost. Sized: costs 1 week of running off next pre-season's base; behind finals_cards_2 (off). While the pre-season switch is off, "carry it silently": the debt is saved and paid into the next pre-season's running base when one opens. (2026-10-13, 2026-11-03; finals_cards_rulings#B8, ch32#9, #10)
86. Grand Final history lines come only from "only the save's own history plus data/club_premierships.csv"; no line claims a final or flag the record cannot show. (2026-11-03; ch32#12)

## 5.9 Backing, firsts, storylines and conversations

87. First slice, answer to which itch to scratch: "Match day" (players on match day; "doesn't feel like my people are out there"); first slice "Players on match day", "the kid you backed". (2026-10-04; user_491246e9)
88. Backing a young player: promise of the next three senior games ("Three games", RUN_GAMES=3), for players with fewer than ten senior games (FEW_GAMES=10) on a career on record in full. Initiated by the young-gun card plus a selection tap ("Card plus a selection tap"; the Back button is profile-only; the tap lives in the profile opened from Selection and gives the same +5 mood as the card). Auto-pick names him ahead of better players; left out while fit breaks the promise and costs mood once ("Breaks once, run ends"); the run lapses at season end; it touches no match rule; AI clubs never have one. Built PR #220 (Backing.gd). Constants THRILL=5, STING=10. Selection shows one factual reminder line ("game two of three") in the "This week" block, not the hub. (2026-10-04, 2026-10-05; user_491246e9, DESIGN.md)
89. Firsts (payoff, built, PR #226): the debut and first goal kept on the player (p["firsts"]); only your own players in your own matches on a career on record in full; said in the live feed ("First AFL goal for Calder."), at full time (3 lines max) and on the player sheet "With us" line ("on your say-so"); wording "X debuted." / "X kicked a goal on debut." / "First AFL goal for X." / "X's three-game run is done."; MAX_LINES 3; once per player; the first-goal feed line is cap-exempt. Guardrails: MatchSim untouched (same seeded match gives the same score and calls on or off), no numbers on new surfaces, silence is fine, no causation claims. Assigned as "build beat 5, the payoff lines". (2026-10-05; user_491246e9, DESIGN.md)
90. Storylines before the bounce (beat 3) split by job: selection shows commitments, the pre-match beat shows what the day is about, and no line appears in both; cap of three a match from stored facts. Old-club storylines use "Long record, then in-career": long record (100+ games) first, then in-career ex-players (20+). Not started (docs/STORYLINES.md untracked); a naive old-club storyline fires in about 90% of matches (wallpaper), the young-gun card about 3 times in 4 seasons. The director chose "Beat 4: the oval" (oval rings and captions) next. (2026-10-04, 2026-10-05; rpg-feel-vision, user_491246e9)
91. RPG-001 Connected backing story: connect the three-game promise to a short player conversation, clear selection obligation and remembered outcome; no second promise system or guaranteed breakthrough; debut and first goal appear only when real. Slice 1 #551 (a broken or lapsed promise told at selection and full time, remembered on his "With us" line). Q-RPG001: the sit-down happens when his run ends, about what he actually did, not when he is backed (slice 2, #556; copy to the director first). Numbers are position-relevant: "a key back shouldn't be rated by the goals scored" (key forward goals and marks, key back spoils and marks). (2026-10-06, 2026-10-09; ch22#RPG-001)
92. RPG-003 private selection and role conversations (dropped veteran, overlooked youngster, returning player): explanations, feasible commitments or honest refusals with factual follow-through; refusal valid; no guessing puzzle or silent change. Director decision: they grow out of the existing weekly "unhappy" card (one card slot, not a new system), situation-based, options explain, promise a game, or your call, with follow-through, sharing the one optional weekly ask (G1). (2026-10-06, 2026-10-09; ch22#RPG-003)
93. Development conversations (user-approved): connect training or retraining plans to short private conversations and factual follow-through (discuss the goal and any agreed playing opportunity, later acknowledge the actual trial and outcome); reuse existing rules, no new progression system or guaranteed success. (2026-10-06; ARD-M5-003)
94. Retirement persuasion (director request, done #275): a short chance to convince a retiring veteran at the user's club to play on only while OVR remains healthy; merely being above the low-OVR floor is insufficient; offered before retirement removes him; he can reject; the answer follows recorded circumstances (long injury history, very recent injury, poor form, poor morale), not a random roll; healthy OVR permits the conversation but does not guarantee willingness; explain the main reason in football language; no OVR boost, erased decline or persuasion spam. Balance and phone check remain with the director. (2026-10-06; ch22)
95. RPG-005 selective role observations: highlight a few event-backed contributions and struggles; useful low-disposal performances recognisable; no every-player report, automatic praise, invented causal attribution or overlap spam; distinct from FL-006 decoration. (2026-10-06; ch22)
96. RPG-008 connected season narrative: connect real opportunities, role changes, arrivals and departures and turning points to recorded coaching decisions; about 5-8 curated beats with a concise football identity; separate from awards night; honest quiet and disappointing years, no forced redemption or causality. (2026-10-06; ch22)
97. RPG-009 restrained living characters: Crusader Kings as the model for emergent stories and consistent living people (durable identity and history, modest motivations, remembered choices, independent credible careers at rival clubs); BALANCE-GATED. Priorities evolve only for an explainable reason and use ordinary development and market rules; cosmetic interests and nicknames stay mechanically separate; measure interruption and repetition burden as well as morale and market balance. (2026-10-06; ch22)
98. Programme order: verify the existing Backing flow, connect one private scene and one media topic; reconcile synergy work before one hybrid interaction and familiarity slice and its role observation; extend recruiting and season stories from real facts; broaden motivations modestly only after pacing works. Independent supported slices may proceed. (RPG programme 9.4 RPG-001..010: Backing first; one combined balance audit at the end; none starts until the director assigns it.) Each slice gets its own targeted checks; the one combined balance audit for RPG-001..010 runs once at the end; an approval of any slice claims no balance result, and balance stays the director's call after his own playtest. (2026-10-06; ch22#Execution, roadmap 0.4a)
99. Special banners and milestone banners (see section 14). Nothing here makes a story surface carry numbers the player does not need.

(2026-10-12; ch25#3)

When a captain retires or leaves: "Yes: a vice-captain steps up by the same rule as the AI clubs (the best-rated vice-captain; the highest Leadership on the list if none is left), with a short note to the coach, and the coach can change it on the Captaincy page. If the coach took the captain off himself, that choice stays: no auto-fill for a spot the coach emptied on purpose." (2026-10-12; ch25#4)

Avoid blanket attribute boosts." Leadership is independent of ability, OVR, age and captaincy; a strong leader need not be the best player; appointing a captain does not auto-grant Leadership; same rules for AI; honest about uncertain real-player values; backward-compatible saves. Acceptance: the player understands the captain's role and its bounded limits; no automatic captain-superstar, forced comeback stories or new relationship subsystem. Design follow-up only, "not an instruction to apply immediate ratings changes". (2026-10-06 to undated; roadmap 10-02)
105. Leadership ideas (concept to refine, possible expressions of the number, "not a request to create three mandatory traits or parallel systems"): "On-field coach: a strong leader could increase gameplan potency through better organisation/execution", tied to the actual chosen plan, personnel and participation; "Leads by example: leadership could support a captain's goal in a clutch moment or last-quarter heroics", influencing tendencies and composure only, never scripting a guaranteed goal, win or comeback, and reporting a leadership moment only when it occurs. Claude assesses overlap with coaches, composure and clutch, morale and reputation and chooses the smallest useful design. (undated; roadmap 10-02)
106. Player personality types are a deferred P3 stretch goal ("stretch goal"): "stretch goal: personality types, for example 'egotistical', 'natural leader'" and "each personality with its own strengths and weaknesses" (natural leader steadies a young group but expects a say; egotistical star lifts in big games but sours the room when dropped). Before design, map against traits, synergies, mood, trust, promises, captaincy and story cards and decide whether it replaces, groups or sits beside them; rules stay transparent and looked up exactly; nothing starts until the director says. Not assigned (2026-10-14 23:14). (2026-10-14; ch25 stretch-goals)
107. Ideas floated but not endorsed: player "leans" (flag, games, security, money, home), persistent promises, wider club memory with callbacks, one defining trait per player, tiered synergies tied to game plans. The director chose to keep weekly cards rather than replace them. (2026-10-05; rpg-feel-vision)

## 5.11 History, records, the club annual and honours

108. Career records are kept apart from history so potential never moves; real players start with AFL careers to 2025; draftees, generated and expansion players start at nothing; old saves mark already-played seasons unknown and totals show a dash. (undated; DESIGN.md#Career records)
109. History, records, leaders and recognition (P1, SUPERVISED): career games and goals, stints, leaders, records, biggest wins, POTM, coaches' votes, honours, Hall of Fame, famous finals, dynasties and droughts. "build from stored facts, not fabricated retrospective text"; count each season once, no duplicate aggregation on reload, coherent generated careers. Acceptance: "records never confuse another player with the same name or new guernsey"; stable IDs survive decades and reload; an ageing contributor is remembered after losing his starting role; finals and dynasties recognised without rewriting quiet seasons as dramatic. Exclusions: a second archive, generic story cards, guaranteed career arcs, fabricated relationships, alumni or bookmark candidates. Continuity check passed on a 12-season autopilot run (two Bailey Williams stay separate). (undated, 2026-10-06; roadmap 10-03)

Answers to 1b/1c: a loss names both heroes; season story now; records for every club but focus on yours; a named season-hero award; legend = 400 goals / 200 games / 3 B&F / Brownlow / 5x All-Australian / Norm Smith / 8 seasons captain (built as story-almanac #890). Trophy room spec (PR #517): history of the director's time at his club: premierships, runners-up, minor premierships, finals reached, club players' honours during his tenure (league best and fairest, leading goalkicker, Rising Star, Coaches Award, All-Australian, club B&F) plus Achievements; three groups kept apart (club honours, players' honours, own tenure); never credit an honour from before his tenure; honest empty states ("No premierships yet."); compact shelves, premiership = cup plus that year's flag, about 96px honour art with name and year, tap for sheet; art via assets/honours/honours.json (ids premiership_cup, premiership_flag, league_bnf_medal, leading_goalkicker_medal, rising_star, coaches_award, club_bnf, all_australian, minor_premiership); persists across saves; works with names standing in until art lands. ## 5.13 Career starts, draft and custom prospect

134. Inherited-list career (ARD-M5-016, P0 TODO, documentation only): "Still missing on main; promote this to the next major career-start feature priority ahead of unrelated content/presentation expansion." New-career setup offers League redraft and Inherit 2026 lists; all 18 founding clubs keep inherited groups from the end-of-2026 lists (before off-season changes) including zero-appearance players; prepare for the 2026 National Draft with the 2026 prospect cohort and sourced pick ownership; play 2027; no fictional fillers; no silent cuts, explicit pre-draft list decisions; contracts shown as estimates; League redraft remains distinct. Urgent correctness and save blockers and five-interchange stay ahead. (2026-10-06; ARD-M5-016)
135. Custom draft prospect: "The fantasy is not "build a 99 OVR player". It is: create someone you care about, watch where they are drafted, follow whether they become a star, journeyman, role player or bust". "A lightweight Follow / Watch affordance is preferred over extra bespoke dashboards." Marked user-created, entered name preserved regardless of real or fictional display mode, easy to find from draft and career flows, history kept after retirement; milestones through normal systems, no separate ruleset. Named first use case: "Include the director's custom Alastair McNeil prospect through this feature, with that exact entered name preserved through draft, club moves and career/history screens." Generic feature implemented #309; ordinary National Draft rules, hidden POT roll, no guaranteed user-club access or buffs; do not substitute the real Lachlan McNeil, fabricate him as a real AFL player or insert him into inherited 2026 lists; works in League-redraft and inherited-list starts. (2026-10-06; roadmap 10-04)
136. Coach as the new club: ruling 8 "yes, built after the package (not built yet)". (2026-10-14; roadmap 10-05 Director rulings)
137. Preferred guernsey number resolves transparently by club numbering rules ("never duplicate active squad numbers."); the preference is preserved; a nickname or commentary short name is optional and must not replace the legal surname in records. (undated; roadmap 10-05)
138. Pre-draft coaching and recruiting meeting: a starting mental model, not a solution; a few scouted names only, not a ranked top 20; reasons in football terms ("We only have two credible rucks on the list."); scouting quality changes detail; never label an objectively correct pick; allow disagreement and uncertainty; useful even for late picks. (undated; ARD-M5-014)
139. Footy Redraft lesson: keep recognisable real beginnings but let future performance emerge from the game's own rules; a recruit's value depends on the side, price, timing and deployment; reject importing the historical endpoint, predetermined retirements and scoring or medal structure; do not secretly scramble ratings or add hollow draft classes. The director values Footy Redraft's emergent stories and wants them sustained after obvious recruits are known (repeat runs feel solved), and finds AFCM long saves lack narrative thrust (its list management is compelling but complex and inscrutable). (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH#1, #3.1, AFL_LONG_CAREER_STORY_RESEARCH)

## 5.15 Genre lessons that bind the story layer

154. Battle Brothers: attachment grows from repeatedly relying on a footballer's useful contribution, including a non-star; an old important player is remembered when he loses his place or retires; reuse the "kid you backed" foundation; reject permadeath, fantasy recruitment and more injuries for drama. (2026-10-05; GENRE#3.9)
155. RimWorld: recognise significant events and connect consequences across time; an archive of numbers is not narrative thrust and generic dramatic prose is no improvement; reject an AI director that rigs close finishes, rubber-bands rivals or inserts disasters; a quiet professional win needs no fabricated turning point. (2026-10-05; GENRE#3.10)
156. Crusader Kings (director input): keep character persistence and connected history with substantially less administration; a footballer can become a veteran specialist, leave, retire and later enter a supported coaching role; keep canonical football records; reject genealogy management, inherited talent optimisation, routine intrigue, personality spreadsheets and a new relationship graph (a per-journalist standing -2..+2 is ruled in, 2026-11-06; a wider graph stays out); rivals must not react to information they could not know. (2026-10-05; GENRE#3.11)
157. Wildermyth and Pyre: the uninterrupted football timeline preserves age and identity (no legacy-style reset); strong prose does not guarantee attachment, so measure whether actual decisions and persistent consequences become remembered stories. A player's role can evolve while identity persists (fringe recruit, role player, central contributor, leader, veteran specialist, retired club figure) as a possible history, not a script; career IDs, stints, achievements and former-player coaching links survive. (2026-10-05; GENRE#3.19-3.20, #4.3)
158. One rival club's identity across generations (RC-007, "Director decision: ACCEPTED within existing owners."): one concise earlier-versus-current squad or pattern comparison within an existing club surface, only where recorded data supports it; do not change in-flight #228 balance; no scripted rivals, immutable club bonuses, fictional feuds, new world-sim layer, mandatory notifications or invented tactics; quiet clubs get no forced story. (2026-10-05; GENRE#7)
