# Chapter 6. Art, visual identity, figures, club colours, animation, audio, themes

Ranking rule for this chapter: a later director decision beats an earlier one, and the 2026-11-03 story-first order
ranks everything: 1 story generator, 2 deep simulator, 3 approachable game. Art, look and sound are presentation. They
serve the story and the simulation and never decide a result. Source tags are short names of the record sources.
Where a record carries no date the tag says "undated". Every quoted phrase is the director's own wording, unaltered.

## 6.A Authority, gates and proof

1. **The director approves every look.** Figures, poses, kit drawing, skin tones, type, palette, layout and scene
   treatment. Nothing ships until he has seen it in the game with pictures. A code PR never changes a figure look
   without his phone check. Green CI is not enough. (undated; STYLE-01..08 clause, 2026-10-06; art-pipeline skill; CLAUDE.md)
2. **The art agent leads visual direction; the director approves the final appearance.** "The art agent leads visual
   direction; I approve the final appearance." The art agent outranks ChatGPT on visual direction. Claude implements
   the art agent's treatment and reports engineering constraints rather than substituting its own taste. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; c02 directive, 23-9-5)
3a. **Animation and camera composition work starts with an assessment.** "Present your assessment before implementing anything." (2026-10-07T05:19, relayed brief) Before any animation or camera composition work, write an assessment against the existing branches and the art agent's work, then recommend small prototypes with visual review evidence; implement nothing before that is presented. (2026-10-07; records tr4)
3b. **Asset checks and audio ownership.** Asset checks (research C11) are the art agent's call; audio (C12) is done only when audio work is assigned. (2026-10-05; records tr3 T6)
3. **Smallest useful prototype first.** "Present the smallest useful prototype before whole-roster regeneration or broad
   rollout." "Do not merge an unapproved final visual treatment or mark unfinished work DONE." Scope approval permits
   scoped prototypes and reviewable implementation, not a final font, palette, geometry or layout. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; c02, CLAUDE.md)
4. **Art goes through internal QA before the director's verdict.** "without my constant babysitting". Obvious
   aberrations are the agent's to catch. Faults that must never reach him: skin showing through a jumper, broken
   anatomy, a bizarre animation, a ball that hovers or moves independently of the player using it, a ground that breaks
   AFL conventions (field dimensions, markings). (2026-10-09; DESIGN_BIBLE, brainstorm, CLAUDE.md)
5. **Art QA with critic agents (current).** "Art QA (2026-11-03, overrides the old no-critics rule): before any UI or art
   capture reaches me, spawn independent critic agents, fix every flaw they find, repeat until clean; any doubt = fail.
   Once clean, every image, board, pose, capture or clip comes to me. I name what is wrong; you work out each fix (exact,
   measurable) and do it." (2026-11-03; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
6. **Copy, do not invent.** "Art and animation output (2026-10-31): copy, don't invent." Humanoid and creature motion
   comes from a proven source (Mixamo clip, reference video), copied exactly. Never improvise poses or probe axes when a
   working example exists. (2026-10-31; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
7. **Look at every frame yourself.** "Showing me broken work is a failure, not a draft." Before showing an image, GIF or
   clip, check every frame from the game angle and the front: a humanoid shape, anatomically possible poses, the weapon
   or ball held naturally and gripped, limbs placed naturally and none missing, sunk into the torso or clipping. Any
   "no" means fix and look again. (2026-10-31; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
8. **Judge motion in motion.** Looks and animation are judged from motion clips at game speed, not stills: "virtually
   impossible for me to tell from still images". Capture tooling supports a movie output (#485). Vignette motion is
   judged as a played clip, never a still. (2026-10-06; c06, style guide section 6)
9. **Capture on current main.** "Rule for every capture from now on: take it on a branch that has current main, so the
   director sees the real typeface." Captures use Fremantle, Melbourne, Bulldogs or St Kilda, never Collingwood. A
   capture or test of a figure proves the intended sheet or atlas loaded, not a fallback. Show normal-size dark phone
   captures of the actual slice, hold content constant for style-only comparisons; a mood board does not validate
   implementation. (2026-10-06; c05, release-1-0 plan, CLAUDE.md, style audit)
10. **Do not over-analyse once it is close.** On the speccy pass c: "really impressed ... its close, dont over analyse".
    Pass d went to him for verdict with no more critic rounds unless plainly broken. (2026-10-13; art.md round 16)
11. **Sound is judged by ears.** Machine checks only for music and sound; the director listens; never write "sounds
    fine". (undated; proof-evidence skill)
12. **Taste questions go as A/B captures**, one decision per question; UI phases first. New moves, poses or facings need
    asking first. (2026-10-10; VISUAL_AUDIT 9; art-pipeline skill)
13. **Textures and downloads.** Any texture download (Poly Haven, ambientCG, Material Maker) needs his explicit approval
    first (Wheelo download was approved). Free software and freely licensed assets only; no paid pack, subscription or
    integration dependency. (2026-10-06; backlog, c04, roadmap 11-04)
14. **Spend.** Tripo allowance for "beautify our UI" is 15,000 credits (ch. 26); the lead tracks it; nothing beyond the
    allowance without his written yes. (2026-10-13; AUDIT ch. 26, release-1-0 plan Task 5.4)
15. **Limits on the assigned STYLE and FL work.** Preserve gameplay, information, touch/Back, existing 2.5D art and
    correctness and performance gates. No new scene authority and no competing design system. FL approval marks no
    implementation complete and authorises no unrelated research candidate. A scene in the style guide is staging data
    plus a capture tool; it plays only moves the sheets hold (a new move is an art request). (2026-10-06; CLAUDE.md, style guide 7.3)
16. **Before and after evidence.** Readability changes show before and after screenshots at 390 px and 1920 px, and the
    ball is always attached to its player in every figure and vignette capture. (2026-10-18; release-1-0 plan 5.2/5.3)

## 6.B Visual identity and the style guide

17. **Visual direction.** "extremely clean, readable and bespoke; preserve the established flat editorial identity,
    typography, alignment and restrained club colours. AFCM is a list-management/friction reference, expressly not a
    visual aspiration." Beauty supports football and decisions and never hides comparisons. (2026-10-05; DESIGN.md)
18. **Immersion standard.** The feel is "clean and crisp like a great app does, but also personal, like a hand-crafted
    game": it should not look like an app. "the interface feels like a game, not an application". (2026-10-09; DESIGN_BIBLE, brainstorm, roadmap 02-01)
19. **Review test.** "without the AFL names and logos, would the screen still look like this game?" A screen that could
    belong to Footy Redraft, AFCM, a generic AI-generated sports manager or a SaaS dashboard after swapping the logo has
    drifted. Footy Redraft and AFCM are negative aesthetic references for styling only, not for gameplay or density.
    Competitor screenshots are evidence, not templates. (undated; style guide intro, roadmap 02-01, UI audit, 23-9-5)
20. **Identity in five sentences.** A football club's evening: warm near-black page, ink type, club colours as the light.
    The voice is the hand-painted scoreboard and signage of a suburban ground, one typeface for every word and figure.
    Football facts as a coach would say them. The people and the ground are the art (pre-rendered footballers in real
    club kit on a ground drawn to real dimensions, never diagrams dressed up). Everything else quiet: one hero element
    and one decision per screen. (2026-10-10; style guide 1)
21. **Anti-slop.** Avoid soft rounded cards, big radii, pills and chips, muted green/teal/blue SaaS palettes,
    glassmorphism, every section in its own rounded rectangle, condensed or all-caps fonts. Use sentence case and
    flatter, sharper geometry; hierarchy from type, spacing, rules and club colours. Apply incrementally to authorised UI
    work; it is not permission for a broad unreviewed redesign and not an instruction to remove stats. The generic green
    base was removed (ARD-M8-002, PR #58): charcoal near-black, warm off-white text, rusty football red/orange accent,
    green only for semantic success. (undated; roadmap 02-01, 11-00, CLAUDE.md)
22. **"Not boring".** "dark mode doesn't need to be boring mode". The UI must have life. Approved pillars: club colour
    as atmosphere, big type and motion (scoreboard face, count-ups, reveals), the 2.5D art and textures of the ground.
    Deliberate decoration (turf, chalk, crowd motifs, club-tinted surfaces) is allowed, never at the cost of
    readability. (2026-10-08; CLAUDE.md) See Open question 1 on how this sits with rule 6.25.
23. **Dark theme only.** "im also happy to abandon 'light mode' for now, i dont want to waste time on it". Dark Android
    portrait is the primary target for builds, captures and tests. No light-only fixes, captures or tests until he says
    otherwise; the code stays and is not deleted unless he asks. (2026-10-13; CLAUDE.md, ch. 26 s11)
24. **Colour tokens.** BG #121110, PANEL #1b1a17 (no border, RADIUS corners), PANEL_ALT #24221e (rare), INK #0d0c0b,
    LINE #363229, TEXT #f1eee6, MUTED #a39e93, FAINT #6e695f (disabled only), GOOD #8cc49a and BAD #e38b73 (genuine state
    only, never information or decoration), ACCENT red only when there is no club colour. Role colours (DEF teal, MID
    green, RUCK gold, FWD red-orange) are ink only in a fixed-width column, never a box. Accent red is for the primary
    action only; selection is a light outline; gold is not "important". (2026-10-10; style guide 2.1, CLAUDE.md)
25. **Space and shape.** GAP 8, SECTION 18, gutter 16 phone / 12 sheet / 10 around the oval; RADIUS 6, nothing rounder;
    panels have no border; rules 6.1 px LINE; no drop shadows, top-edge highlights, glows, glassmorphism or gradients
    except the club wash; thumb targets at least 44 px; scrollbar rail 20 px with a 6 px thumb growing to 10 px.
    (undated; style guide 2.4)
26. **Allegiance and sections (second pillar).** Wherever a player or club appears his side is obvious at a glance (club
    colour as rule, bar, tint or heading ink; a guernsey marker for dark-colour clubs). Sections are set apart by weight,
    size and club colour, not spacing alone. A screen whose text is all one colour and weight fails review. A club colour
    that fails contrast is never used as ink; a red club's colour is never placed where it could read as BAD or the
    primary action. (2026-10-13; style guide 2.2, DESIGN_BIBLE)
27. **Where colour goes.** Atmosphere: club wash fading to the page at the head of the screen, a 2 px club rule under a
    heading, the guernsey marker, both clubs on match day. One primary action per screen, filled in club colour (ACCENT
    if none), never two filled buttons. Selection is TEXT ink plus a 2 px TEXT outline, never a fill or club colour. Bold
    for headings, names and the one fact per block. MUTED only for what can safely be skipped; nothing he decides on sits
    in uniform grey. (undated, answered 2026-10-10; style guide 2.2, 11)
28. **Typeface.** The game's face is ARD Signwriter, drawn by the art agent from old scoreboard numerals, built by
    tools/typeface/build_font.py, owned by the project, used at every size (Regular, Bold, Display with painted shade).
    "I do not want to use barlow or any other generic font because it is immediately remoniscent of AI slop". Approved:
    "Approve and merge" (#419). No Barlow pairing for small sizes. Free fonts only with clear permissive licences, source
    and licence recorded. Font changes are project-wide decisions. (2026-10-06, 2026-10-10; STYLE-02, style guide 2.3, 11)
29. **Type roles.** TITLE 24 Display, HEADING 18 Bold, NAME 16 Bold, BODY 15, SECONDARY 14 MUTED, FINE 13 MUTED (stamps
    and fine print only, never a fact the player decides on), RATING 30, SCORE 24, NUMBER 22. Literal sizes are a defect
    and a test fails them. Sentence case except AFL conventions (OVR, POT, Grand Final); no letter-spacing, no all-caps
    labels, tabular digits. The Display cut is for scores, ratings, titles and the "v" between clubs only. The rest of
    STYLE-02 (numeral refinement, 1/I/l, 6/8/9, 0/O checks; score forms like 12.8 (80) and money without clipping)
    remains to do. (2026-10-10; style guide 2.3, 23-9-5)
30. **Motion.** A screen eases in over 0.18 s; headlines reveal; a figure counts up in under a second. Nothing bounces,
    slides from the side or shakes except approved match haptics and shake. Headless runs see the final state
    (UiKit.motion_on()). (undated; style guide 6)
31. **Contrast and score ink.** UiKit.score_colour picks the first of accent, secondary, primary reaching 4.5:1 on PANEL
    (#407, approved). Colour must not imply active information is disabled or that one side's score matters more; both
    teams' scores keep comparable legibility. The director approves final colours. (2026-10-06; c05, STYLE-03)
32. **Decorative versus real information.** "decorative banners and flavour must be clearly distinct from actual game
    information". Distinction by hierarchy, placement and context, not colour alone; never copy tactical-card styles or
    imply stat changes. Every flavour addition has zero gameplay effects. (2026-10-06; flavour research, CLAUDE.md)
33. **What can affect the sim.** What the coach says or decides can affect the sim; how the club looks or sounds never
    does. Weather is the one exception: the condition affects play, its look does not. Copy must be precise on this: the
    director challenged "no effect on results" because weather does affect results. (2026-10-06, 2026-10-09; c9dce4bf, DESIGN_BIBLE, CLAUDE.md)
34. **STYLE package scopes (all eight included, "Include" to each, no rejections).** STYLE-01 bespoke dark controls (no
    card piles); STYLE-03 action, label and club-ink pairings; STYLE-04 formation oval and match-screen composition
    (blank space deliberate, no names, commentary or controls removed, no best-choice hints, no 3D); STYLE-05 distinctive
    headers and roster-number marks starting with Main, Hub, Full list (no new portraits, invented histories, unlicensed
    logos, extra destinations or badge piles); STYLE-06 align match overlays and transitions with the approved 2.5D
    figures, existing scenes only; STYLE-07 PC fullscreen readability (done, #371). Validation log separates observed
    problem, art-agent treatment, director decision, verified build. Training player-row name/secondary-line stack is a
    confirmed vertical-alignment defect. (2026-10-06; 23-9-5, UI audit, CLAUDE.md)
35. **Club Forge flow.** Name club, pick guernsey design, pick colours, apply colours; colour options stay greyed until a
    pattern is chosen because not every pattern carries three colours; "no need for two colour bars, just let me click on
    the part of the jumper I want to colour". Colours changeable repeatedly, optional slots clearable, live preview is the
    real game rendering, save and reopen preserves look. Done (#493/#498). Guernsey designs share one construction
    vocabulary (plain, stripes, hoops, sash, yoke, band, chevrons, panels, chevron, sides, tiers, shoulders, map; sock
    hoops, independent shorts); more vocabulary, never protected artwork. (2026-10-06, 2026-10-07; c06, roadmap 02-03-19, 10-05)
36. **App icon.** Coherent Aussie Rules Dynasties identity across platforms from the supplied reference (bold condensed
    cream block lettering, red brush-script accent, very dark background); "ARD" authorised as compact icon lettering;
    installed app name stays the full name; legacy AFL Auto-Battler branding removed; adapt the existing title, do not
    introduce a competing brand. Art-agent direction and his approval before DONE. (2026-10-06; ARD-M8-008)

## 6.C Match view, vignettes and the ground

37. **The oval is the centrepiece.** "the centrepiece of our visual experience is the simulated matches on the oval.
    This should feel like a really premium, beautiful, and informative visual representation of our game." It is "its
    not an afterthought, its the main thing that sets our game apart from its contemporaries." (2026-10-10; match-view memory)
38. **Vignettes read like a broadcast.** "it looks bizarre how there are no background players or atmospheric details,
    it reads as a very unnatural scene to anyone who's ever seen an AFL broadcast." Judge each vignette against a real
    broadcast frame. Centre bounce: both teams' 6-6-6 set-ups, wingers, forwards and backs inside the arcs, other field
    umpires, boundary umpires on the wings, goal umpires at the posts. Atmosphere: lit crowd in club colours, banners,
    cheer squads. "Who is missing from this shot?" is a defect check, not taste. Cheap tiers (distant players as simpler
    sprites) protect phone draw cost. Backgrounds and foregrounds remove uncanny voids (crowds, rooms, audiences,
    foreground tables, microphones). (2026-10-10, 2026-10-06; vignette memory, roadmap 11-04)
39. **Every in-match decision requires a vignette** (his requirement 1.11); dropping vignettes was rejected. Real-time 3D
    figures and generative restyling were rejected. Tactical-scene expansion needs his phone finding that the player can
    explain why the decision is being asked. (2026-10-08, undated; VISUAL_AUDIT, roadmap 11-04)
40. **All old vignette figures migrate to the shared pre-rendered 2.5D style.** "entirely replace the old vignette art
    style with the new pre-rendered 2.5D footballer style". No stick figure or silhouette stays player-facing; inventory
    every renderer and fallback; retire obsolete paths. Awards ceremony currently reuses BroadcastVignette silhouettes
    and must be replaced (no per-club scenes, fabricated likeness, votes or outcomes). The awards walk-on otherwise stays
    as it is (no presenter, no handover). (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; roadmap 11-04, 10-03, 9.1)
41. **Flavour scenes authorised.** Necessary new ritual, milestone, retirement and awards scenes (FL-002/003/007: first
    goals, milestones, retirements, awards, farewell) are authorised through the shared art pipeline, with art, readability
    and performance gates kept. Exclusions: 3D, narrative event cards, fabricated movement implying an unapplied tactic. This
    supersedes earlier no-expansion wording for those occasions only. (2026-10-06; 21-9-3, 11-04, CLAUDE.md)
42. **Rendered plates, not drawn rooms.** On the Tripo desk in a drawn room: "an exercise in futility, trying to marry a
    placeholder artstyle with far superior assets". "do what you think is best, but I expect beautiful results".
    Environments (press room, awards stage, MCG with crowd) are one rendered plate each, built in Blender under the
    figures' own lens, key light and ground plane at 2x. Bar: real scale against the 1.88 m figure, light from the key
    direction, contact shadows under furniture, a lit crowd not cut-outs. Drawn rooms stay only as fallbacks; do not
    propose prop-by-prop dressing of drawn scenes. Props are rendered through the same camera and light, never a runtime
    mesh. (2026-10-10; VISUAL_AUDIT 9, rendered-plates memory, style guide 7.3)
43. **Ground and crowd.** Real AFL dimensions through the scene camera (posts, square, arcs, circles, mown stripes,
    apron, fence, stand). Both clubs' colours in crowd and boards. Venue plates replace the painted stand where one
    exists, an impression and never a labelled copy of real marks. Weather is restrained and never covers the ball or a
    name. Weather looks go "Push them stronger". (2026-10-06, undated; style guide 7.2, c06)
44. **Home ground sets oval size and look.** "I want the dimensions and appearance of the visual simulation to change
    based on the home ground (only AFL listed grounds will be necessary, aka MCG, Marvel Stadium, Optus Stadium, Adelaide
    Oval, Metricon, GMHBA, SCG, etc)." MCG long and wide, GMHBA long and narrow, Adelaide Oval long, SCG short; other
    grounds fall back to the default oval; Grand Final at the MCG. Acceptance: "a footy fan recognises the MCG, Marvel,
    Optus, Adelaide Oval, the Gabba, GMHBA and the SCG without the name." The MCG look is built (#537: three stand tiers,
    fascia band, blue-grey night seats, two end screens, "like the lighting"), shown only for MCG games; every other
    ground unchanged. FL-003 excludes new venue scheduling, explorable towns, pitch-geometry changes, weather generation,
    home bonuses and travel effects. Do not invent a stadium where a real football oval exists. (2026-10-09, 2026-10-10;
    ARD-M8-011, 21-9-3, 10-05)
45. **Match ball.** Sherrin approved (#469); "Yellow at night". Ball colour is chosen once per match from context and is
    the same across prep, live view, stoppages, vignettes and replay; stable default for old saves; never hard-code red in
    shared vignette assets. The hub poster ball is "footy is too big": about 1.4 times a guernsey's width. (2026-10-06,
    2026-10-14; c06, roadmap 11-04, playtest E1)
46. **Club identity on the oval.** "crows and demons look the same basically. why is there no yellow for the crows?
    audit readability of all discs" ... "and all matchups". Each club disc carries its defining colours (Crows gold), all
    306 pairings are scored for distinctness with a clash strip when too close, and on-oval name labels never overlap
    (#763, all at least 40 after clash strips, zero overlaps in 500 frames). Kit clash is also run through deuteranopia and
    protanopia simulation across all 306 pairings; there is no separate colour-blind mode. Adelaide players' names in text
    are gold. (2026-10-14, 2026-10-18; playtest A11/E3, C-PC-06)
47. **Game-plan tint.** The oval's game-plan tint shows every quarter: standing half-strength tint whenever the plan is
    Defensive press, full when pressing without the ball, slower fade (#767). That is the lead's call, his to veto.
    (2026-10-14; playtest A13)
48. **Training props show real colours.** "remove the team tint from these things. looks like random shapes without the
    colours." Cones, tackle bag and stopwatch in real colours everywhere (#730, #737). (2026-10-14; playtest A15)
49. **Oval-broadcast bundle.** Bundle 2: P-34, P-31, P-32, P-33 yes, P-35 skipped. Breeze end shown (the engine already
    picks and swaps it each quarter), crowd and a goals replay make the oval read like a broadcast without touching the
    sim. The goals replay after full time comes from the event log, is skippable, reuses the broadcast vignette, no 3D or
    free camera. (2026-10-18; PASS2, C-PC-19)
50. **Specific scene decisions.** Speccy staging B "low, tight, freeze on the grab", the knee goes on the shoulder beside
    the head of the man he climbs; "Yes, put it in the game" (#711); speccy_front is behind a switch, off by default
    (#725, #726). Centre-bounce framing approved "Yes, as built" (#422), camera turns instead of sliding so turf lines do
    not skew. Round-5 notes on the PR #299 GIFs (2026-10-06, art agent actioned): speccy lands square, not angled, and the pack comes down after the contest; boundary snap about three
    steps with the ball visible as he carries it, two defenders more than 5 m off who close in and leap at the snap (higher and nearer on a miss, the pressure fail gate); pre-match runs last 3.8 s; the centre-bounce pan turns the camera instead of sliding it, so turf lines do not skew. The post-match press conference is
    "arguably the game's best vignette": preserve it. The speccy must show who kicked the incoming ball, without
    inventing a kicker. (2026-10-06 to 2026-10-13; art.md, roadmap 11-04, 02-03-02)
51. **Atmosphere audio.** Natural crowd sound with breathing room and volume controls: done (#405, #409). Important
    information is equally clear when muted; no constant announcements, no music over every score, no second audio
    system or licensed-song dependency; watch, skip and replay never repeat a football event. The crowd knows the score
    and the siren is on the tick: crowd bed rises with goal runs, home louder than away, siren at every quarter end,
    measured event-to-sound offset, never affects the sim, no spoken commentary (a team with no voice budget uses written,
    varied, well-timed text). Game feel sounds are footy-real (boot on leather, stud on concrete, siren blip, thump with
    crowd-lift sting). (2026-10-06, 2026-10-11, 2026-10-18; 21-9-3, C-PC-20, POLISH, build_feel)
52. **Haptics and shake.** Restrained, tied to authoritative events, mainly the visual match sim; independent controls
    including Off; built as goals-only capped shake (#701) with Screen shake On/Off and Reduced motion. (2026-10-07;
    roadmap 02-03-14, open-requirements row 7)
53. **Rendering quality.** Whole-game antialiasing: "well hold on, I want AA in the entire game including vignettes..."
    (#472 pitch AA "Keep it"; #478 project-wide 2D MSAA 2x plus antialiased vignette lines, "Yes, turn it on"). Free
    shader polish and lighting stay high priority (restrained character shadow and highlight in figure.gdshader, soft
    contact shadows, baked lighting first; runtime lighting only if baked is insufficient and phone measurement justifies
    it; no bloom, blur or dynamic shadows). Unsupported: whole-roster regeneration before an approved sample. Idle and
    battery audits are report-first. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; c02 directive, c05, roadmap 11-04)

## 6.D Figures, looks and kit

54. **Figure method.** Still-2D pre-rendered 2.5D footballers from the MPFB base body via the ard-asset-pipeline repo,
    recoloured per club by figure.gdshader, numbers printed by the shader, hair overlays; code never edits a sheet or
    VignetteFigures.gd. Render 2x drawn size (200 px per metre), magnified at most 1.5x; strips carry contact markers,
    ball anchors, rendered side, timing limits; planted foot within 1 cm of turf; contact shadow under boots; no twins;
    a mirrored kick is a left-foot kick; idle figures watch the play. Faces are never likenessed (curated row or neutral
    look, never a guess). Keep MPFB; the bizarre motion came from hand-keyed poses, so fix motion with reference motion.
    (2026-10-05, 2026-10-10; style guide 7.1, FIGURE_PIPELINE_REVIEW)
55. **Poses are never from text prompts.** "you need to make sure the generations arent creating SOCCER actions, AFL is
    nothing like soccer". Poses come from the own rig and approved strips; Tripo is for look and props. Style is
    semi-realistic, never cartoon or chunky ("remember our semi-realistic style"). Check against a real AFL photo: ball in
    hands or dropping onto the boot, never on the ground, no leg above the waist on a kick backswing. (2026-10-14; poses memory)
56. **Players must not look like clones.** "they look like clones". Faces and genuinely different hairstyles. Built behind
    switch player_looks (off), PR #873: 15 hair styles, a minimal face ("minimal: eyes, brows, skin tones"; no mouth, nose
    or lashes), five brows chosen by player id ("brows vary per player") tinted to hair colour, eyes near black; "buzz
    keeps no hair overlay"; 30 player strips re-rendered ("re-render them all"; coach and carrier_near unchanged). He plays
    it before the switch goes on. (2026-10-10, 2026-11-03; open-requirements row 6, ch. 32 rows 14-17)
57. **Skin and diversity.** Never default every vignette player to one light-skinned appearance. Neutral appearance data
    only (six skin tones, hair colour, palette indices 0-7; style ids strings; guernsey colours hex), no gameplay race or
    ethnicity attributes, no inference from names. data/player_appearance.csv is drafted from squad photos and reviewed
    by him (draft, unsure, confirmed; no row = one neutral look). Generated players vary by id; hair and facial-hair
    colour may differ within a natural range. (2026-10-05; roadmap 02-03-20, 11-04)
58. **Hair.** "this hair/beard stuff is no good" ended the first look-dev. His own hair research (Claude_Hair_Quality_Research.md)
    is the brief: three prototypes (Fritsch-inspired, rooted dreadlocks, short textured) reviewed in Blender and at game
    scale before any library rollout; #394 prototypes "Close it". Hair #442: bare scalp, stubble cap is the buzz, smooth
    close crop. Dark-hair palette lift approved (#411): black #16110e to #241b16, dark brown #33211a to #40291f. Hair, beard
    and boot ids are not final until the lead says go; beards and boots went back to the art agent. Library aims: large,
    Bald explicit, afro, mullets, curly, long, dreadlocks, Fritsch-inspired; facial hair independent of hairstyle; beards
    never obscure numbers or faces; headbands sit naturally. Hair strips: check every frame before showing him.
    (2026-10-06, 2026-11-03; backlog, c04, c06, roadmap 10-05)
59. **Cut from the look.** Freckles ("just remove freckles from the game, unnecessary detail"; "i alreeady said to delete
    freckles from the game"; "yes for the third time remove freckles") and scars ("remove scarring from the game,
    unnecessary detail") are gone from the player look and the Club Forge spec; old saves load and ignore the keys.
    (2026-10-06; #357, #382, #354)
60. **Kit.** KIT APPROVED (strap v4); neckline is V everywhere for now. Long sleeves are per player, not per kit: "the
    entire team shouldn't be in long sleeves... only like 15% of the list should wear them, up to 25% if it's raining";
    deterministic per player id, a dry-weather wearer also wears them in the wet. Long-sleeve look approved (#522): top
    runs over the shoulder, sleeve below in base colour, no bare shoulder; sock hoops approved (off until a kit sets them);
    zero result effect. "AFL players don't wear t-shirts": the Forge crest marker is a singlet, "looks great" (#385). Fix
    guernsey neckline and shoulder seams at the real mesh, UV or material cause, not by avoiding an angle. (2026-10-06 to
    2026-10-12; art.md, backlog, #522, #385, roadmap 02-03-05)
61. **Club colours.** Corrected primary, secondary and accent for every club, signed off 2026-10-05 and applied to
    data/clubs.csv except Carlton's and Melbourne's navies, which keep the game's lifted navy so they do not read as
    Collingwood and Essendon. "apply all the colour changes, keep Carlton and Melbourne navies". The assistant proposes
    colours, the director approves; palette snapshot test in the matchday suite. Guernsey designs per data/clubs.csv with
    reference-image clubs (Brisbane, Gold Coast, GWS, Port Adelaide, West Coast); Tasmania 2024 foundation guernsey;
    Canberra none; club emblems not drawn. (2026-10-05; post-Italy playtest findings, c01, roadmap 11-04)
62. **Indigenous Round guernseys.** "you design it yourself, ask the boss to add import/export functionality so users can
    add their own designs": the art agent designs original guernseys with structural moves only, labelled original, with
    cultural-review state; 5-10 per club over time; 5-10 samples per club go to the director first. Never copy
    artist-owned artwork, Dreaming stories or sacred imagery, or invent cultural narratives; "Indigenous artwork is
    culturally specific, artist-owned work: do not turn it into a selectable decorative pattern unless an original/licensed
    design is created for the game." "The designs do not need lore attached to them." Vignettes read the actual match kit
    assignment. Commissioning First Nations artists is the audit's Option A, the director's choice and payment.
    (2026-10-07; INDIGENOUS_GUERNSEY_AUDIT, roadmap 10-06)
63. **Tattoos.** Requested motifs: "rose, snake, barbed wire, bird, 666, love heart, Southern Cross stars, and Asian-script
    lettering", plus dreadlocks. Original artwork only; no copying a real player's layout, no tracing, no gang or
    extremist symbols or protected logos; lettering uses real characters with checked meanings. Premiership-year tattoos:
    some players adopt one on the bicep, others never; once adopted each later flag adds a year; no re-roll; adoption stored
    once, independent of football RNG, kept through transfers and reload; restrained configurable rate. All cosmetic.
    (2026-10-06; AFL_TATTOO_REFERENCES, roadmap 10-05)
64. **Club Forge appearance options** are gameplay-neutral cosmetics: socks, headbands, bandaging, tattoos, boots,
    independent hair and beard colours, skin tone, dominant foot, preferred number, optional nickname. User-designed
    guernseys and logos are optional, never mandatory. Forge import/export: 256x128 PNG, ~1 MB cap. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-07; roadmap)
65. **Tripo for UI.** "im interested in using tripo models to beautify our UI and stuff, just like how we did with our
    club guernsey logos on the ladder". Built: culture emblems (#704), cup (#718 ball, #736 cup), lectern, gear,
    stopwatch; no further emblem work is assigned. Real-time 3D vignettes are a low-priority probe only; three.js is not
    the route. (2026-10-13; ch. 26, open-requirements rows 38, 43)
66. **Banner text and music removed.** "We should remove banner text from the game for now, replace with placeholder text,
    i will create original banner text movingforward - clearest AI tell in the game which cheapens the experience": every
    rhyme in data/banners.json is placeholder "{us}", keys kept, his originals drop in later. "remove the music too": four
    generated tracks deleted (#790); the music system, bus and saved setting stay; the Settings Music row is hidden while
    the playlist is empty. (2026-10-18; ch. 30 rows 9-10)
67. **Register consistency.** One off-register element (cartoon portraits, rough faces, jarring commentary) is called out
    even when everything else passes. (2026-10-18; POLISH)
68. **Research shelf.** The optional Art Agent resource shelf (MakeHuman CC0, Poly Haven, ambientCG, Kenney assets and
    others) is reference only and creates no task. Do not feed ordinary PBR maps into packed figure shader inputs; keep
    drawn oval markings; soccer balls are not AFL balls; resolve Quaternius licence terms before use. (2026-10-06; roadmap 01-0)

## 6.S Superseded and cut

1. Light theme, STYLE-08 and "both themes" art check (2026-10-06, #205 Dark/Light palette, light appendix): cut 2026-10-13, replaced by dark only (rule 6.23).
2. "No agent judges art" (2026-10-29): replaced 2026-11-03 by critic-agent QA (rule 6.5); the release-1-0 plan's art-qa-critic line was retired the same day.
3. DESIGN.md Platform line (dark green panels, terracotta actions, Barlow): stale; replaced by the style guide (rule 6.24, 28).
4. Barlow as baseline or pairing for small sizes, and the style guide's small-sizes note: replaced 2026-10-10 by ARD Signwriter at every size (rule 6.28).
5. Typeface "PROTOTYPE until director's final OK" and #408 specimen: replaced by "Approve and merge" (#419).
6. Anti-slop ban on gradients, glows and bold decoration: partly overridden by "Not boring" (2026-10-08); the style guide of 2026-10-10 limits gradients to the club wash. See Open question 1.
7. Hair and beard look-dev of early October ("no good"): replaced by his hair research and prototypes, then 15 styles behind player_looks (rule 6.56, 58). The contact sheet of 24 hair styles and 10 beards stands as not rolled out.
8. Freckles and scars (Forge spec bullets, BASE_LOOK): cut (rule 6.59).
9. Team tint on training props: cut, real colours (rule 6.48).
10. Long sleeves per kit (#368): replaced by per-player rule and #522 (rule 6.60).
11. Tripo desk placed in a drawn room, and prop-by-prop dressing: replaced by whole rendered plates (rule 6.42).
12. Old no-expansion wording on vignettes: superseded for ritual, milestone, retirement and awards scenes only (rule 6.41).
13. Legacy stick-figure and silhouette vignette art: replaced by pre-rendered 2.5D (rule 6.40); the migration is stated as complete in part, not fully verified.
14. Pitch-only AA and "MSAA stays off": superseded by whole-game AA and project-wide 2D MSAA 2x (#478) (rule 6.53). His question about icon smoothing is answered by the same ruling.
15. "No reduced-motion setting for now" (2026-10-06): a Reduced motion setting was later built (rule 6.52); treated as superseded in practice.
16. Generated menu music and rhyming banner text: removed 2026-10-18 (rule 6.66).
17. Real-time 3D figures, generative restyling and dropping vignettes: rejected (rule 6.39). Real-time 3D players on the match view's oval: ruled in 2026-11-14 (rule 2.74).
18. Light-theme score ink, contrast and 8-defect audit findings: inventory only, no work.
19. Capture practice of 2026-10-06 that showed #383 without the typeface: replaced by the current-main capture rule (rule 6.9).
20. Style guide questions (all answered 2026-10-10): selection is ink plus outline with no club tint; mini figures on the match view only as a phone prototype; venue plates after media room and stage props (rules 6.27, 42, 44).

## 6.Q Open for the director

1. The "Not boring" decision allows gradients and glows when deliberate, but the style guide of 2026-10-10 allows no gradient except the club wash and no glows. Which governs?
2. Should each screen lead with one hero element, to be shown on the hub first? It is proposed, not confirmed.
3. Do you want the player_looks switch (15 hair styles, minimal face, 30 re-rendered strips, PR #873) turned on after you have played it?
4. Are hair, beard and boot ids final, and what happens to the 24-style hair and 10-beard contact sheet and the boot sheets that still await your look?
5. Is the press-conference vignette now free of the black bands (layout #754), and do you accept the press-room plate not yet being on main?
6. Is the centre ball-up scene on PC acceptable, and does the umpire's held ball no longer float?
7. Does the goals-only screen shake feel right on your own device?
8. Will you supply your original banner text and music tracks, and when?
9. Is ground size presentation only, or does it affect the sim (R33, Q6)? What grounds do fictional clubs use? The bible allows only weather to affect play.
10. How does weather affect a match, and do you want unique weather icons later (parked, D1)?
11. Do you keep the standing half-strength defensive-press tint (his to veto, #767)?
12. How many Tripo credits may still be spent beyond the 15,000 allowance, and is any further emblem work assigned?
13. Should the live-3D vignette probe (low priority) and the bronze statue "come to life" idea for oval figures be pursued or dropped?
14. Which First Nations artists do you choose and pay for Indigenous Round, and do you want the 5-10 per club samples first?
15. Do you approve the ARD app icon after seeing full-title versus ARD candidates at icon scale?
16. What premiership-year tattoo adoption rate do you want?
17. Which of the open styling looks (STYLE-01, 03, 04, 05, 06, the hub poster ball size, #508, #511, #512, #540 awards scene, guernsey seam fix) do you approve?
18. Which unassigned defects should be assigned: club ink under 4.5:1 on club-tinted surfaces, hub ladder empty right side at 2560, break-pane 13 px notes, ellipsised draft subtitle, the boundary-snap curved white line, snap-for-goal hovering ball?
19. Do you want any of the long-save wishlist built (away and clash guernseys, honour board, club legends wall, season poster, club museum)?
20. Do you decide the Cascadeur Indie spend and the open animation-pipeline decisions (section 12 of the proposal), including the gather-on-the-run proof? (Partly answered 2026-11-14: motion comes from a free mocap service, rule 2.74.)

---

# Chapter 7. Release scope, platforms, cuts, process, team and agent rules, spend and budget, CI

How to read this chapter. Conflicts resolve by one rule: the later director decision wins, and the 2026-11-03 story-first order (1 story generator, 2 deep simulator, 3 approachable game) ranks everything. Every rule carries its date and a source tag. Words in double quotes are the director's own, unaltered. A line with no quote is a record of a decision or a rule with no verbatim words on file. Where a source is the director's global file, the tag is `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`; the repo file is `CLAUDE.md`. Status words: done means the order was carried out and is history; open means a question or gate still awaits the director.

The design bible (docs/DESIGN_BIBLE.md) carries no release, CI, spend or team text. This chapter is the home for all of it. Where this chapter and the bible disagree, the bible wins and this chapter is corrected.

---

## 7.P1 Rules in force

### 7.A Authority, assignment and the bible

1. **Bible first.** Read the design bible in full at every session start, before the roadmap, and judge every feature with its feature test. Found 2026-10-11 after a lead worked two days without reading it. (2026-10-11; memory/design-bible-is-the-top-doc.md)
2. **Nobody edits the bible without the director.** Teammates and agents propose; they never edit it, never declare balance settled, art approved or a feature accepted. Plan approval granted inside an agent team is not a director gate. "Nobody edits the bible. Teammates propose, under "For the director"." (2026-10-29; docs/agents/AGENT_TEAMS.md#Ground rules)
3. **A roadmap is context, not authorisation.** Do not start a roadmap item, branch, PR or follow-up until it is explicitly assigned. When an assigned task is done: stop, report, recommend the next step, wait. A plan that disagrees with the bible loses to the bible. "This plan is context, not authorisation (CLAUDE.md). Nothing in it starts until the director assigns it. Phase 0 ends with his rulings". (2026-10-09, restated 2026-10-18; repo CLAUDE.md#Project philosophy; docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-18-release-1-0.md#Authority)
4. **Rulings on proposals are not assignments.** A yes to a proposal or theme bundle authorises the list; nothing starts until he assigns it. (2026-10-18; roadmap/28#intro; PASS2#How-to-decide)
5. **Within assigned work, do not ask permission.** "do both now, stop asking my permission to do work": do not ask which of two assigned tasks to do first; questions are for design, taste and spend. Stop for him only on a genuine unresolved player-experience or design decision, a failed or ambiguous balance gate, destructive data or save risk, unexplained failing tests, unclear conflict resolution, or a task that has grown well past its intent. Dependency order is not an approval gate. (2026-10-09; 2026-09-28; memory/no-permission-for-work.md; roadmap/01#standing-development-authority)
6. **Standing instruction from the 2026-10-05 directive.** "You do not need to ask me for permission to investigate, implement, test, document, or open the PR. Only stop for my input if you reach a genuine design decision that materially changes balance and cannot be resolved from the roadmap/guardrails." (2026-10-05; session directive)
7. **Roadmap items are not authority to merge by themselves.** The 2026-09-28 "standing development authority" (merge clean completed work without per-PR permission; old "do not merge" lines void) survives only as far as the 2026-11-03 merging rule and the appearance gate allow. A newer task-specific HOLD placed by the director beats it. (2026-09-28, tempered 2026-10-09 and 2026-11-03; roadmap/01#standing-development-authority)
8. **Audit ownership.** When he says audit, investigate or check, it is an engineering task Claude owns; Claude runs the final measured audit and reports evidence. He is the final design authority. (2026-10-05; roadmap/01#0.5)
9. **Execution contract.** Read the roadmap first; inspect current code before writing; smallest plan; implement only the authorised scope; no opportunistic redesign of neighbouring systems; targeted regression tests; GitHub CI owns the full suite; a save-schema change needs backward-compatibility proof; check UI at narrow Android portrait; stop and report if the feature grows into a broad rewrite. (2026-09-28; roadmap/01#0.5)
10. **Queue rules.** A new P0 correctness fault, soft-lock, fake or no-op mechanic or core-fun failure jumps ahead of feature work. System Reality Audit no-ops become repair work before new systems. Do not chase roadmap completion percentage: the objective is a good game, not a finished checklist. (2026-10-07; roadmap/01#queue-rules)
11. **Autonomy tags.** SAFE proceeds. SUPERVISED is not an automatic approval gate: proceed when the roadmap already resolves the player-experience decision, stop only if a material design choice is truly open. BALANCE-GATED proceeds through build and measurement but merges only when the evidence supports the intended effect without unacceptable side effects; ambiguous or bad evidence is a stop. Balance-gated work needs seeded before/after measurement and the director's playtest; diagnose and measure before tuning. (2026-09-28, 2026-10-06; roadmap/01#0.4; 04-m1 to 07-m4 headers)
12. **Status legend.** KNOWN BUG, TODO, PARTIAL, IN PROGRESS, VERIFY, DONE, DEFERRED; priority P0 to P3. Never mark DONE because code was written locally: it must be merged and verified. "Add this to the roadmap" means search for duplicates, then update the file. (2026-10-07; roadmap/01#0.1-0.4)
13. **Where the single highest priority sits.** The Stats menu patch (section 1.11) alone held highest priority; it merged as #509 on 2026-10-08. Earlier "highest" declarations (desktop scaling, Club Forge, AI drafting, potential inflation) are history, not a second top priority. (2026-10-07; roadmap/01#0.4.1)
14. **Hard gates before unrelated feature expansion (phone playtest, 2026-09-30).** Match flow without freezes or artificial waits; decision inputs enough to form a reasoned expectation; synergy clarity in football language; decision feedback after a choice; no number vomit. "Pause unrelated new feature work until this gate is addressed." Existing PRs may finish through CI and merge. (2026-09-30; roadmap/02-03-19, 02-03-21)
15. **System Reality Audit rules.** "This is an **audit before repair**." Do not fix individual findings while still discovering scope unless a destructive or soft-locking fault makes testing unsafe. Trace what the player sees and chooses, the state created, stored, read by the engine, the calculation changed, the outcome changed and the result surfaced; a break anywhere is a finding. The test is whether a mechanic truly changes what the player is told it changes, not whether it is perfectly tuned. Use paired-seed on/off comparisons. Each system gets exactly one status: WORKING, WORKING BUT QUESTIONABLE, PARTIALLY CONNECTED, UI / REPORT ONLY, NO-OP, DEAD / DISCONNECTED, UNVERIFIED. Never mark working because code exists or a test is green. (2026-09-29; roadmap/02-04-1-12)
16. **Player agency over Forge acceptance.** Club Forge repair: "screenshots alone do not establish that selecting, clearing and previewing work". Supply a runnable playtest build with a short before/after walkthrough; check desktop fullscreen and phone layouts; save and reopen must keep identity, colours and guernsey with the in-game club matching the preview; preserve existing saved clubs. (2026-10-07; roadmap/02-03-19)
17. **Roadmap execution order.** M1 Correctness and Low-Risk UX, M2 Match Event and Stat Foundation, M3 AFL Rules and Match Authenticity, M4 Tactical Matchday Layer, M5 Selection, Roles and Development, M6 Coaching, Board and List Management, M7 Competition Identity and Long Careers, M8 Release Polish and Long-Save QA. Dependencies matter more than labels; later self-contained tasks may proceed. (roadmap/03; undated)
18. **No duplicate systems.** Cross-cutting umbrellas stay consolidated (Match Report, Opponent Preparation, Team Form, Forward-50 and Scoring, Match-ups and Accountability, History and Records, AFL Rules and Restarts, Simulation Controls). Check the duplicate and merge map before adding any roadmap line; no second "advanced report" and no second scouting or season-momentum feature. FL items are accepted extensions inside existing owners. (2026-10-06; roadmap/12, 18; 21-9-3#header)
19. **FL and research numbers are budgets, not caps.** Prototype quantities in the flavour research are starting budgets, not fixed content limits; approval is not implementation; record evidence and outstanding work per extension. (2026-10-06; 21-9-3#Common dependencies 7)
20. **Research does not replace judgement.** ROADMAP.md stays the sole execution backlog; research candidates need director selection before entering Claude's queue; Claude owns final engineering measurements; the director judges the experience; research supports a testable hypothesis only. ARD-RC IDs keep research provenance, they are not milestone tickets; before building a refined card re-read code and PR state and require regression, balance, save and phone evidence before DONE. (2026-10-05; docs/GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH.md#1, #7, #8)
21. **Register discipline.** A row leaves open-requirements.md only when its section file records DONE with the PR. Owner "none" means no plan or branch covers it; "verify" means the code is unchecked; a row with an old owner name (boss, lead, art, story) has no live owner until assigned; the later director chapter (26 to 32) wins. Read the register in full at session start. (2026-11-04; docs/roadmap/open-requirements.md#header)
22. **Documents.** "no flowery prose, functional only": terse, every must-read file under 60 KB (CI: tools/check_doc_sizes.py). "skimming the documents is a sin": agents read memory, handoff, skill and checklist files in full; a long doc is split, never skimmed (ROADMAP was 775 KB and never read whole). Delete outright when he asks; archive-moves were rejected. (2026-10-10; 2026-10-29; tools/check_doc_sizes.py; agent-handoffs/hygiene.md; handoffs/scratch-2026-10-29)
23. **Every claim carries its evidence.** After the 2026-10-10 evening hygiene session he called "really poor": a claim to him names its PR, file or test; no promises in place of evidence; no "all five pending PRs" when three were another session's. (2026-10-10; agent-handoffs/hygiene.md#Open work)
24. **Skills improve over time.** A finding goes into a skill's references/learnings.md only once proved effective (a quality check that caught a real defect, a recurring fix verified afterwards, a measured saving), with date, project, evidence and lesson. A finding that contradicts the skill corrects the skill itself. Project spin-off skills are named afl-<topic> and say which general skill they extend. Every rules file, skill and memory note keeps a line only if it changes what an agent does and is costly to work out again (framework review, BMAD not adopted). (2026-10-08; 2026-10-19; repo CLAUDE.md; roadmap/30#43)
25. **Connectors, skills and tools stay minimal.** "only Casc is needed, the rest can be called if and when I need them": all skills, plugins, connectors and MCP servers were cleared (tier 1 done). Connectors the director attached earlier (Firecrawl, Adobe) and Google Drive, Gmail and Calendar are his own accounts: use only when asked and confirm before sending. gh CLI stays for GitHub. (2026-10-29; handoffs/scratch-2026-10-29-0399de.md; memory/connectors-available.md)
26. **Seeded paired audit only when asked.** Run it when the director asks, before a release, or for a change meant to move the numbers; not on every sim tweak. Answer a balance question with a seeded audit through audit.yml, not a long local loop. (2026-10-19; afl-lifecycle-review and afl-proof-evidence skills)
27. **Probe before captures.** Probe what a new system produces (deals, numbers) across seeds before captures and his look; "LOOKS fine" is not a pass when the offers are absurd. (2026-10-25; agent-handoffs/lead.md)
28. **Balance sign-off.** Phase 6 balance sign-off is the director's call. An agent may report numbers close to real AFL; balance is settled only when his playtest also passes. Difficulty dial sizes are his, from playtest. (2026-10-18; plan Phase 6; roadmap/28#4)

### 7.B Never idle, report state, ask well

29. **Never idle.** "Never idle, never invent busywork, never park a question in a wall of text. End every turn with your state: working, awaiting director, blocked, or available." While anything is pending (audit, CI, capture, a peer), set a watcher and do the next useful part of your own queue; if nothing is left, ask him for a task. (2026-10-29; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
30. **"Working" means a tool call is running right now.** Never end a turn with "working". Say "working" only with the live job's name (for example "working: background build, task id X"); otherwise the state is awaiting director, blocked or available. (2026-10-31; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
31. **Banned phrase.** "Banned phrase (2026-10-31): never write "running a check" or "state: running a check", in any form or wording that means the same thing." A pending check is not a state; report "working" and name the actual task. (2026-10-31; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
32. **Never wait for CI.** "Never wait for CI (2026-10-29): push, arm a background watcher, start the next task at once; merge or fix when it reports. [work-while-waiting]" He caught idle agents repeatedly (2026-10-05 to 06: "The director has caught medium and low idle repeatedly."; "note that agents should never wait unless absolutely necessary, get him working"). Pull work, do not wait for it. A turn that ends waiting on an audit or CI is idle. "get to work", "keep working", "keep going", "carry on" and "continue" are nudges to keep going, not new rulings. (2026-10-06; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md; agent-handoffs/backlog.md#The rule)
33. **Ask explicit questions.** "ask me explicit questions rather than pondering my thoughts in inscrutable walls of text, delaying merges because they haven't bothered asking my opinion is lazy work". One decision per question, recommended option first with a one-line trade-off, a PR or image link, gameplay words not raw numbers; for anything visual send a clearly labelled image (a clip for motion) in the same turn, then action his answer. "you have explicit instructions not to leave merges waiting on my decision, you MUST ask me and show me the pics so we can merge". "im frustrated that you keep forgetting your Memory and the conventions of how you should ask questions". "ask me questions that you need answers to". (2026-10-06; agent-handoffs/backlog.md rule 7.5; memory/ask-director-explicit-questions.md)
34. **Anything awaiting his yes.** Never write "awaits your yes" or "needs your OK" in a doc, handoff, report or table and leave it. Show the thing itself and ask the question directly, as a question, in that same turn. If he already answered, remove the stale line. "wdym, just show me." (2026-10-31; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
35. **Do the work; do not hand him tasks.** "stop asking me to do things, do your job"; "stop asking me to merge you weirdo, do it yourself". (2026-10-06; user_c9dce4bf_c01)
36. **Be proactive on trouble.** "just make it work properly, be proactive, fix CI". (2026-11-03; handoffs/scratch-2026-11-02-683516.md#State)
37. **ETAs are measured.** Never guess. Measure first (work done, elapsed time, rate, work left), show the working in one line, add every step to the end result (push, PR, CI), say what is unknown; if it cannot be measured, say so and say how it will be found. He asks for honest minutes and the bottleneck. (2026-10-31; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
38. **Write concisely.** Lead with the result; no preamble, narration or closing recap; short plain prose; tables and lists only for real structure; caveats only when they change what he should do; full detail when asked; never trade correctness for brevity. Errors, failing output, warnings and destructive-action confirmations stay complete. (2026-10-25; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
39. **Handoffs.** He switches between two Claude accounts. Each project folder (each worktree) keeps one handoff in ~/.claude/handoffs/ (the SessionStart hook names it). Read it first; after a turn that changed files or git, edit it: current state only, no log, max 80 lines (State, Task, Where things are, Unfinished, Next, Open questions, Gotchas). Open questions list his decisions not yet in the roadmap. Never commit it. (2026-10-29; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md; afl-handoff skill)
40. **Handoff hygiene across a team.** A handoff names task, branch or commit, files owned, unfinished changes, evidence, open decisions, dependencies and running jobs with PIDs or run ids. Context target roughly 50 to 100k with a look when it passes 150k (working targets). Never restart with undocumented in-flight work and never restart mid-task. (2026-10-06; roadmap/01#team-workflow)
41. **Directing the director.** He wants decisions in a single question with the thing shown, not stalling. "do not proceed, wait for next instruction" means stop and wait. A dismissed question is not a no. (2026-10-06; c06)
41a. **A session with no task reconciles the roadmap and the register.** He said "there is a roadmap... update it", and the 2026-11-03 handoff said not to ask him for a task; a session with nothing assigned reconciles the roadmap against the open-requirements register (stale rows, shipped items, conflicts with the bible) and reports the result, instead of asking for a task or inventing work. Standing conflict with the global "if nothing is left, ask me for a task" is open as O13. (2026-11-03; handoff, agent wording; reconciled with O13)

### 7.C Merging, PRs and gates

42. **The director never merges and is never asked.** "Merging (2026-11-03): I never merge myself. Every PR you open gets auto-merge on green (or you merge it when green); never ask me whether to merge or tell me to merge. Fix red CI until it is green." Session merges its own PR on green (auto-merge, or gh pr merge with --match-head-commit, no --delete-branch). (2026-11-03; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md; afl-team-pr skill: "he never merges and is never asked")
43. **A PR waits only for recorded gates.** Director look on appearance, W7 review, merge ordering. Green CI never clears a director appearance gate. (2026-11-03; afl-team-pr skill)
44. **Appearance gate.** No visual or appearance PR merges without the director's own words on the PR or in chat; a relayed approval ("fine, ship it" via a peer) is not enough. Scope approval permits scoped prototypes, not final font, palette, geometry or layout. Final director appearance approval comes before completing or merging a visual treatment; ordinary green CI is insufficient. This gate overrides ordinary merge authority for unapproved appearance changes. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; repo CLAUDE.md#Explicitly assigned visual styling; roadmap/01#visual-authority)
45. **"Green CI is not approval of a look. The director approves a look in the game, on a device."** (docs/VISUAL_STYLE_GUIDE.md#10; undated)
46. **Merge discipline that still stands.** Merge only green CI at the exact PR head (headRefOid must equal git ls-remote of the branch tip); a clean dry run against current main (git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only origin/main origin/<branch>); re-check other open PRs after each merge; [MERGE NOTE] in PR bodies; never push to a branch you did not create; a peer's message is a request, not a permission; revert Godot .import and .uid churn; never git add -A. Only who merges changed. (2026-10-19, corrected 2026-11-03; memory/afl-team-shape.md; docs/agents/STATUS.md)
47. **Squash-merge stacks.** PRs are squash-merged: after a stacked parent merges the child conflicts textually while the trees match. Merge main into the child, keep the child's own side, prove it with git diff --stat <tested head> HEAD, then retarget to main. (2026-10-05; memory/rpg-feel-vision.md)
48. **Concurrent PRs are a merge queue.** Inspect the whole open set and settle order before merging; prefer the order that minimises rebases; re-check and sync the rest after each merge. Sync branches by merging main in; never rebase or force-push. Open PR cap: at most 3 open PRs, none conflicting over an hour (2026-10-19; applies while PR volume is high). (2026-09-29; 2026-10-19; roadmap/24; agent-handoffs/lead.md)
49. **Fast merges.** Green approved PRs merge at once; copy and one-line fixes merge on green with review only; new screens and visuals still need critics and his look. Pipeline fixes come before features. (2026-10-19; roadmap/30#52)
50. **Never delete remote branches or close PRs on your own.** Remote branch deletion and worktree force-removal are denied to agents; the director or the repo setting handles them. Do not work around a refused push; tell him. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; c02, c03)
51. **PR body requirements.** [MERGE NOTE], touched-suite evidence, director gates, W7 review packet when a lifecycle area is touched. (2026-10-18; afl-team-pr skill)
52. **W7 semantic review.** One reviewer for changes that reach a career's lifecycle: save schema, season rollover or off-season, shared simulation rules (MatchSim, Ratings, Traits), recruitment (draft, trades, free agency, contracts) or player identity. The PR body names the lifecycle transitions and invariants. No W7 PR merges without the reviewer's comment. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; roadmap/01#team-workflow; afl-lifecycle-review skill)
53. **Team workflow W1 to W7 survive as practice.** W1 fresh sessions at task boundaries with handoffs; W2 direct actionable messages; W3 say which state you are in; W4 event-driven monitoring, no polling; W6 own your processes, never kill Godot by image name; W7 as above. (2026-10-06; roadmap/01#team-workflow)
54. **Auto-fix authority.** When enabled, fix CI failures and merge conflicts on PR branches, merge FETCH_HEAD of base (never rebase or force-push), commit and push without asking. (2026-10-05; desktop-app event, user enabled)
55. **Floors and shared files when PRs collide (history, now moot).** Expected-check floors are retired (see CI). (2026-10-18; replaces 2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07 floor rules)
56. **Hot files get one owner or none.** MatchSim.gd, GameState.gd, UiKit.gd, MatchNotes.gd, the roadmap section files, CLAUDE.md and tests.yml. Check git worktree list and gh pr list before taking an item. (2026-10-06, 2026-10-29; roadmap/01#0.4a; AGENT_TEAMS.md)
57. **Squash a stats-hub style stack with merge commits.** Sub-PRs into an integration branch merge with --merge and keep branches; only the integration PR into main is the gated one. (2026-10-07; user_c9dce4bf_c01)
58. **Commits and PRs.** Stage by name; never commit another session's untracked files; commits end with the Co-Authored-By line. (2026-10-05, 2026-11-03; docs/agents/STATUS.md)
59. **Phone-before-merge for rendering and performance.** "PC now, phone later": baseline and compare on this PC's Mobile renderer; the director runs an Android build on his phone before anything of that kind merges and reports back. Merged does not close a native-phone check. (2026-10-06; agent-handoffs/backlog.md#Art; roadmap/01#0.4.1)

### 7.D Release scope, versions, platforms and itch

60. **Release scope is the director's.** Never drop a named feature from a build to save CI time. Before cutting anything he or the release plan named, ask with the question tool (what is cut, what it saves, recommendation). A feature behind a switch still counts in or out; switch state at ship time is his call. "be faster" means parallel work, tighter polls, merging greens first, never a smaller release. Born of "wtf. 1.0 isnt 1.0 without pre season". (2026-10-19; memory/release-scope-is-his.md)
61. **A patch asked for "with those fixes" carries the top-priority fix he named.** If it is not ready, hold the patch or ask. Cancel lower-priority CI runs so his priority PR gets runners first. Club colour markers use real colours as vertical bands (UiKit.club_marker; three-colour clubs listed in GameDB.THREE_COLOUR_CLUBS), no imported logos. Named-prospect path ships first; "Prioritise a minimal functioning named-prospect path rather than waiting for the entire cosmetic library." Release polish final pass has "no silent failure/fallbacks". (undated; roadmap/10-06, 11-00, 11-04, 10-04)
96. **Match-visualisation guardrails.** "Do not perform a movement-engine rewrite without evidence that local fixes are insufficient. Do not paper over authoritative simulation defects with presentation-only fakery." Vignette motion audit: ball, contact and identity correctness first; "Do not rerun unrelated broad audits or rewrite the motion engine without demonstrated need."; never DONE from a static screenshot or claim native-phone motion was checked when it was not. (undated; roadmap/11-01, 11-04)
97. **Contract and trade guardrails.** No club profit or loss expansion, MTX, psychic AI, hidden rival money, blanket harder-AI discounts or six routine negotiations every offseason; do not attempt all player-movement systems in one mega-PR; no contract-admin busywork; report realised value as well as projected. Draft: no quiet AI bias, no second generation pipeline, no multiplayer or share-code V1, no flooding the class, deterministic draft-class generation kept; "Do not let the user assign a club, draft pick, OVR or potential." (2026-10-05; roadmap/09-03, 10-04)
98. **Trailer (ARD-M8-010 trailer gate).** Hard-gated, deferred, P3. Claude must not begin until the roadmap is mostly complete, major visual and presentation work substantially finished, polish near shipping quality, core systems stable, no major placeholder visuals, and the user gives an explicit go. Roadmap status alone does not authorise. Leaving DEFERRED takes two steps: Claude recommends the gate is ready, then the user explicitly authorises. Before approval do not edit, capture footage, install tools, create music, render title cards, assemble cuts or open a trailer PR. A high-effort run should periodically reassess and tell him when it thinks the gate is ready (recommendation only). Free software only, open-source preferred; no paid licences, subscriptions or charging trials. After approval first produce a short trailer plan (length, audience, arc, shot list, saves, capture list, audio, title copy, formats, distribution) for his review. Complete only when he has reviewed the finished cut: accurate to current gameplay, polished footage, clean audio, no unlicensed material, release-format exports, text readable on mobile and desktop. (2026-10-06; roadmap/21-9-3#ARD-M8-010)
