### 7.F Spend, budget and licences

108. **No spend without his written yes in the project's decision log.** "No spend without my written yes in the project's decision log. Where credits already exist, the project's threshold applies (MERCS: Tripo, ask above 500 credits per session)." For this project the Tripo threshold is the 15,000 pool below. Any other spend (Play Store fee, signing service, font licence, domain) needs his written yes first. (2026-10-29; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md; open-requirements row 43)
109. **Tripo pool: 15,000 credits.** "you're being too frugal with tripo credits, you have a 15,000 budget to blow on whatever you want". Written yes for Tripo spend on AFL up to 15,000 credits total; do not ask per item or propose small caps; keep a running tally in the handoff and decision log (0 used at record); come back only when near 15,000 or for non-Tripo spend. "im not saying you HAVE to spend 15k in one go, im just setting that 15k aside to you to use as you see fit with over the next few hours,days, weeks": a pool, not a mandate. The budget is for the project, not one session. It replaces the per-slice caps (~300, ~1000, 10,000). (2026-10-13; memory/tripo-budget-15000-ui-beautify.md; roadmap/26#10)
110. **Tripo use.** "im interested in using tripo models to beautify our UI and stuff, just like how we did with our club guernsey logos on the ladder". Stop rules stand: provenance and six-view inspection on every generation; art checked before he sees it; Tripo one at a time, private; not for player bodies as a shortcut past motion; not text-to-motion for ball skills; never ship a raw mesh; via Studio in the director's Chrome, API only when credits exist; key in an env var only, never in repo or logs; art flags credit-heavy batches. (2026-10-07, 2026-10-13; VISUAL_STYLE_GUIDE#8-9; roadmap/26#10)
111. **Free software only for art and trailer tooling.** Free (open source preferred) tools for bespoke UI assets, textures, icons and layouts; paid software, subscriptions, paid plugins and charging trials need separate explicit approval; note each new tool's source and licence. Fonts: may research, download and use free or licensed fonts suitable for distribution; paid font licences and subscriptions need approval. Font changes are project-wide decisions for the director. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; roadmap/24#2026-10-06; memory/connectors-available.md)
112. **Downloads need his explicit approval.** CC0 texture sites included; state filename, source and size when asking. He approved downloading Source Sans 3, Source Serif 4, fonttools, skia-pathops, ttfautohint and the Wheelo player-stats data ("Yes, download them"). (2026-10-06; c06; user_5778f315_c07)
113. **Licences and provenance.** Fonts SIL OFL or owned (ARD Signwriter); figures MPFB CC0 plus our renders; props our Blender files, Tripo on a paid plan (owned, private), rendered only; Hunyuan3D geometry reference only. Banned: non-commercial models (Qwen-Image 2.1, Anima, GVHMR, free DeepMotion, QuickMagic, Cascadeur free), broadcast footage as derivation, any likeness. Nothing ships without a provenance row (source, licence, task id, credits). Motion provenance allowed: owned footage, CMU, Tripo presets as reference only. (undated; docs/VISUAL_STYLE_GUIDE.md#8, #9)
114. **Allowed tools.** Blender plus MPFB for every shipped figure, prop, plate and honour; ComfyUI/SDXL concept boards only, nothing shipped; Ollama text for copy lint or second review, never judging a look; Ollama vision (qwen3.5) truncation pre-pass only, not art QC; Godot capture tools for every review image and clip. (undated; VISUAL_STYLE_GUIDE#9)
115. **Art-agent permission to source its own free tools.** "note that if the art agent is able to source his own free software, and its actually good, then he should do it of his own volition without asking me to source it". Limited by the download and paid-software rules above. (2026-10-05; user_bdd340e9_c01)
116. **Helpers and model choice.** "only assign them tasks they will be able to perform to a high standard". "only spawn opus medium effort helpers, you stay fable high", then "you can spawn sonnet helpers too, just dont spawn fables". Helpers are Sonnet or Opus at medium effort, never Fable. (2026-10-07, 2026-10-18; memory/afl-team-shape.md; roadmap/30#20)
117. **Token efficiency.** "if you have ideas to increase token efficiency which wont meaningfully reduce production quality by all means implement them or direct me to implement them". "batch independent tool calls" and "test runners quiet by default" are in token-and-ci-efficiency.md. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-19; agent-handoffs/process.md)

### 7.G CI, tests and the local machine

118. **CI shape (current).** Fast PR gate under 10 minutes plus nightly.yml running the full long suites. "fast PR gate + nightly long suites". A suite whose median CI time is over about 240 s (story, save, match_game, career, contracts) runs nightly, not on pull requests or the main-push gate; tools/ci_long_suites.txt lists them. PRs skip long suites entirely. #893 merged (fast gate plus nightly.yml, which opens an issue on failure); #894 adds retry-once, a FLAKY flag and a warning above 10 minutes. (2026-11-04; tools/ci_long_suites.txt; handoffs/scratch-2026-11-02-683516.md#State)
119. **Nightly red is fixed first.** A red nightly is fixed by the agent first thing each session: check open nightly issues at session start. (2026-11-04; handoffs/scratch-2026-11-02-683516.md#State)
120. **Check-count floors are retired.** Since 2026-10-18 (test suite audit section 4: they caught no real defect). Each suite runs through tests/functions_ran.gd and reports "K of M functions"; a function that never ran or made no check fails by name; a deliberate skip prints SKIP: <reason>. Suites run on a fixed frame clock (--fixed-fps 60). "Yes, all of it" for the test-suite audit plan: shards from real timings, "every test function ran" in place of floors, preflight replaced by a compile check, suites found by filename with no hand-kept registries, save and match_game split, .gitattributes, the four-word-phrase check a warning; no player-facing coverage lost. (2026-10-18; TEST_SUITE_AUDIT; roadmap/30#35; roadmap/01#0.4a)
121. **Test audit finding.** Asked: "do an audit of the tests we do, are any of them superfluous? are we wasting time being overly fastidious on easily avoidable things?" 50 of 53 suites worth keeping; the waste was in the CI machinery. A test count is not a goal. (2026-10-18; TEST_SUITE_AUDIT#Summary)
122. **Seeding.** Every test suite pins SUITE_SEED (2027) or GameState.replay_seed, or carries "## Seeded by design: <why>"; tools/check_suite_seeds.sh guards it. A rule counts rules, not data: collect failures in a loop and _check once. (2026-10-06; #349, #375)
123. **CI plan.** tests.yml is plan, shards, extras and an aggregate required check named "test". audit.yml for long balance or behaviour runs; capture.yml for captures; nightly.yml for long suites. Docs-only changes (docs/** or Markdown only) skip the Godot suite; mixed docs and code still run it. Branch protection blocks admin merge. (2026-10-05, 2026-09-28, 2026-11-03; memory/ci-sharded-workflow.md; roadmap/24; handoffs)
124. **Validation tiers.** Targeted fast loop, feature gate, PR gate in CI, long-run gate. Do not routinely run the full suite locally (only for harness or CI changes, CI unavailable, a hard integration failure, or unusually high risk). Standard validation matrix by change type (pure UI, MatchSim, stats, balance, availability, season, salary, schema, visualisation, long-save, docs-only). Use phone-width checks for UI, regression tests for behaviour, seeded balance evidence for simulation changes, and update the roadmap as work completes. (2026-09-28; roadmap/01#0.6, roadmap/14)
125. **Local machine rules (current).** Godot runs off-screen (the one-process rule was removed 2026-11-04 for the parallel balance harness, tools/balance.sh), own user data (APPDATA=<scratch dir> inline for the test command only, never exported, because gh keeps its login under APPDATA). Run only the suites your change touches locally, at most 2 at a time, with run_in_background; CI runs the rest and audit.yml takes long audits. Never taskkill Godot by name; kill only your own PIDs. Blocking waits show as "stalled" to the director. Cap parallel Godot agents (16 cores; about 6 concurrent suites makes agents crawl). Windows user:// is shared across worktrees. (2026-10-05, 2026-11-03; docs/agents/STATUS.md; handoffs/scratch-2026-11-02-683516.md#Gotchas; memory/godot-tests-share-userdir.md)
126. **No focus stealing.** "for the love of god stop alt tabbing me": captures are offscreen (override.cfg no_focus) or better via capture.yml, never a windowed Godot on his desktop. "Keep tools in the background. Do not open or focus windows or interrupt my desktop." (2026-10-06; memory/no-focus-stealing-captures.md)
127. **Captures use current main.** Before any capture for the director, merge current main into the branch so the real typeface shows (the #383 capture predated #419 ARD Signwriter and he asked "where is our typeface gone?"). (2026-10-06; memory/captures-on-current-main.md)
128. **Windows workflow gotchas.** Worktree text files are CRLF locally (autocrlf) while blobs are LF: edit Python with newline=''; do not add .gitattributes eol=lf for this (419 tracked CRLF files); scripts committed from Windows are mode 100644; windowed screenshot capture hangs intermittently; a --script tool must load classes with load(), not by static name. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; memory/ci-sharded-workflow.md)
129. **Parallel-work rules.** Check git worktree list and gh pr list before taking an item; one worktree per session from origin/main; do not push to a PR branch while its CI runs unless needed; prune only MERGED or CLOSED worktrees after checking for real changes; one long local Godot run per agent. (2026-10-06, 2026-11-03; roadmap/01#0.4a; docs/agents/STATUS.md)
130. **Lean validation ownership.** Claude uses targeted tests; CI owns the full-suite PR gate, log triage, selective reruns and merge ordering; [CI HANDOFF] comments are work on that PR. CI-waiting PRs do not count toward the two-active-implementation-branch limit. (2026-09-28, 2026-09-29; roadmap/01#0.6)
131. **Device-only checks stay on the phone.** Native mobile sensor rotation, software keyboards and notches need device checks; the draft UI is exercised in the Godot 4.7.2 web renderer plus browser touch tests. The first 1.0 performance run needs his debug APK. (undated; tests/README.md; plan Task 1.6)
132. **Match-view checks.** tests/test_match_motion.gd (640 events, all calls both sides, phone upright oval at 1x and 4x), tests/test_match_marks.gd, tests/test_match_visual.gd (log, score and stats unchanged, no event mutated, ordered release, no NaN or jumps, ball at the logged spot, global RNG isolation, rings and names), tools/visual/capture_match.gd under xvfb-run. (undated; docs/MATCH_VIEW.md#Checking it)

### 7.H Proof and art checks

133. **Verification.** Real taps prove a UI flow (a tap at the control's position, not only emit_signal("pressed")); new or changed touch flows get at least one real-tap check. A capture or test of a vignette or figure proves the intended sheet or atlas loaded, not a fallback. After two similar failed fixes of one problem, stop patching and gather different evidence (a capture, trace or measurement). Adopted into CLAUDE.md via #433. (2026-10-06; repo CLAUDE.md#Verification; roadmap/19-9-1)
134. **Proof for every visual change.** Before and after at phone 390 (and 360), PC 1920x1080 and 4K, dark first; 2x crops and a clip; right art loaded proven; real tap on touch flows; run tools/run_screen_lint.sh and fix every FIX line (taps under 44 px, text under 13 px, duplicated fact, contrast under 4.5:1); tests for no literal font sizes and no truncated names; one decision per question, recommended option first, labelled images in the same turn. Hold content constant when comparing treatments; no pixel-exact tests encoding subjective taste. (undated; VISUAL_STYLE_GUIDE#10; roadmap/23-9-5)
135. **Art QA: critics first, then the director.** "Art QA (2026-11-03, overrides the old no-critics rule): before any UI or art capture reaches me, spawn independent critic agents, fix every flaw they find, repeat until clean; any doubt = fail. Once clean, every image, board, pose, capture or clip comes to me." Looks need motion clips for motion; "youre disobeying your Memory by not automatically showing me these images, clearly labelled, and actioning my response". (2026-11-03; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md; 2026-10-06 c9dce4bf)
136. **Captures without Collingwood; art checked before he sees it.** Art is inspected by the session before any capture goes to him; showing broken work is a failure, not a draft. (2026-10-19; agent-handoffs/lead.md#HIS RULES; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
137. **Art and animation: copy, don't invent.** For humanoid or creature motion use a proven source (Mixamo clip, reference video) and copy it exactly; look at every frame from the game angle and the front before showing him. (2026-10-31; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md)
138. **Tripo, 3D and similar budgets for art checks.** Art role caps, one Blender job at a time, never commit The MCG.glb. (2026-10-12; agent-handoffs/art.md#Role)
139. **Gradients and glows are not on the avoid list.** "Yes, remove them": his 2026-10-08 "not boring" ruling allows them (#840). (2026-10-19; roadmap/30#45)
140. **Memory and CLAUDE.md upkeep.** "Yes, merge and shorten": merge duplicate or stale memory notes; shorten the 10-06 flavour and styling sections to roadmap pointers (#840). "Yes, adopt it" for the framework-review playbook. One source of truth for standing rules: repo CLAUDE.md plus ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md. (2026-10-19; roadmap/30#43, #46; docs/agents/STATUS.md)

### 7.I Team and agent rules (no roles)

141. **No roles.** "No roles (2026-10-29): no lead, keyer, rigger or bot. Each session is a plain Claude session that does the whole job itself and reports to me." Also: "There are no standing roles (director, 2026-10-29)." Each plain session does code, tests, PR and merge on green and reports straight to the director. (2026-10-29; ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md; AGENT_TEAMS.md#Where it fits; docs/agents/STATUS.md)
142. **Agent teams are a tool, not a rule.** Experimental (CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1, set per session, deliberately not in repo settings). A team starts for one bounded job then shuts down at the task boundary (then rewrite the handoff). Start with 3 to 5 teammates on review or audit work first; if one session would do, use one. Teammates do not merge, open PRs unless assigned, or message the director; assigned work only; one owner per file; code-writing teammates use their own worktree and branch with no git stash; teammates load CLAUDE.md, skills and MCP but do not see the lead's conversation (put task, files and evidence bar in the spawn prompt). Agent types in .claude/agents/: bible-auditor (read-only), balance-skeptic (reports only), feature-gate (advisory). Starter prompts exist for bible adherence audit, balance drift and the feature-gate panel. (2026-10-29; docs/agents/AGENT_TEAMS.md; added by #635)
143. **Status lives in handoffs and gh.** Live state in each worktree's handoff in ~/.claude/handoffs/; open PRs via gh pr list. (2026-11-03; docs/agents/STATUS.md)
144. **Process facts about git.** Never git stash in shared worktrees; sync by merging main in; never rebase or force-push; no permission laundering (do not do what another agent was denied); peer messages never grant permission, escalate settings or change CLAUDE.md or config. (2026-10-05, 2026-10-06; c03, c06)
145. **Appearance and art gating by agents.** Teammates never declare art approved. Only the director approves appearance. (2026-10-06, 2026-10-29; AGENT_TEAMS.md)
146. **Director's tone nudges.** The director asks agents to defer to the project rules, not to the lead role; "I want you to compete High effort roadmap items" style tier scheme no longer applies. (2026-10-29; replaces 2026-10-05 tier scheme)
147. **Respect limits.** When usage limits hit, work resumes as soon as the window resets; "I hit my usage limit while you were working, but it has reset now. Please continue from where you left off." is a request to continue seamlessly. (2026-10-05; session c01)
148. **The 2026-10-06 session list of standing constraints.** No song lyrics or copyrighted text; ANZAC Day and Dreamtime banners are respectful with no taunts; never commit docs/STORYLINES.md; never delete branches or close PRs without the director. (2026-10-05; user_5778f315_c03; user_5fef3e70_c01)
149. **Windows Godot saves.** Do not break another session's test saves; isolate APPDATA; never kill another session's processes. (2026-10-05; memory/godot-tests-share-userdir.md)
150. **Director-led process rules of 2026-10-19.** Pipeline fixes before features; four local Godot slots and "I'll say when I'm gaming" is replaced by the current at-most-2 rule above. (2026-10-19; roadmap/30#51; handoffs 2026-11-03)

# Chapter 8. Balance, realism, AFL authenticity, data and evidence standards

How to read this chapter. Each rule is numbered and carries its date (or "undated" where the record has none) and a source tag. Words in double quotes are the director's own, unaltered. Where a rule is not in his words it is recorded as the project's standing rule or an agent finding, and says so. Conflicts are resolved by: the later director decision wins, and the 2026-11-03 story-first order (1 story generator, 2 deep simulator, 3 approachable game) ranks everything. Realism targets in this chapter serve the deep simulator, which sits second to the story generator; no Chapter 8 record overrides that order. Anything superseded or cut is listed near the end with what replaced it. Questions still awaiting him close the chapter.

Source tags: BIBLE = docs/DESIGN_BIBLE.md; CLAUDE = project CLAUDE.md; DESIGN = docs/DESIGN.md; GENRE = GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH and genre decisions; PASS2 = roadmap ch. 29; CPC = pass-2 candidate sheet; ROADMAP-nn = roadmap chapter nn; GUARD = roadmap global engineering guardrails; CB = COMPETITIVE_BALANCE; LB = LEAGUE_BALANCE; DIFF = difficulty evidence and rerun docs; HO = handoffs and backlog; SESSION = session transcripts relayed by the lead.

---

## 8.A Who decides balance, and how it is settled

1. **Balance is settled only when two things are both true.** Testing shows numbers similar to real AFL, and the director's own playtest passes the vibe check. Player ratings that reflect reality are part of this work, not a separate goal. Balance is still settling. (2026-10-09, BIBLE#Balance; brainstorm#Decided; CLAUDE; ROADMAP-02-1.10; release-1-0 plan; balance-skeptic agent)
2. **Balance is the director's to call.** An agent may report that numbers are close to real AFL. It never declares balance done, and an approval alone claims no balance result. (2026-10-09, CLAUDE; ROADMAP-02-1.10; ROADMAP-22-9.4#Execution; release-1-0 plan Phase 6)
3. **Agents measure and report; the director decides.** Agents give distributions against real AFL, with options and a recommendation, and he picks before any tuning. Setshot, wet-weather, trait-decay, free-kick and similar audits are measurement only. In the record on calibration: "the director decides on any calibration". (2026-10-06, afl-audit-runs skill; SESSION c03/c04; FREE_KICKS; HO)
4. **Balance sizes go to his own playtest.** How much familiarity matters, the fitness base, and training injuries are balance for his playtest (2026-10-13, ROADMAP-26#2; preseason rulings). Culture effect size: "Small, then measure". It needs his playtest before any step up, the sizes live in one data file, and the rule is the same for every club. (2026-10-13, ROADMAP-26#5)
5. **Gameplay before analytics.** Revisit hidden simulation mechanics only when play exposes a real problem, a feature needs it, or the director asks. (2026-10-09, CLAUDE)
6. **Balance sign-off at 1.0.** BALANCE_1_0.md compares scores, disposals, marks, tackles, inside 50s, goal accuracy, margins, comeback swings, ladder spread, draft outcomes and ages with real AFL seasons, on paired seeds with errors. It reports what is close and what is not and declares nothing. Only what he rules is fixed. His full-season playtest follows and his words are recorded. (undated, release-1-0 plan Phase 6)
7. **BALANCE-GATED changes.** Any change that can materially alter wins, scoring, production, development, availability or tactics needs deterministic seeds, a before/after, the existing harness, 1,000+ matches per condition when cheap, mean and distribution, positional and archetype side effects, strong-vs-weak results, and the same rules for AI and player. Do not tune until one screenshot looks right. (2026-10-09, ROADMAP-02-1.10)
8. **Balance assessment template.** Each balance-gated task states hypothesis, control, variant, sample (seeded, mirrored or randomised), primary and side-effect metrics (scoring, margins, positional stats, strong-vs-weak, comebacks, concentration, injuries, development), and an acceptance reading of absent, too weak, plausible, too strong or unstable. Never change several tuning constants at once. (undated, ROADMAP-15#6)
9. **Proof comes from outcomes, not storage.** Features that change play use their own dice stream (run_rng, mark_rng); bookkeeping uses stat_rng or none; byte-identical seeded results are verified; calibration within 0.07 tolerance is re-measured per role against real AFL 2026 (afl_role_rates.json) with paired seeded audits on GitHub audit.yml. A stat extra must use stat_rng so seeded results do not change. (2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07, SESSION c02, c07; ROADMAP-04/05)
10. **Proof practices.** Touch flows are proven with real taps (tests/tap.gd); assert the actual art used; for audio, machines check loading, clipping, levels and pause, and how music sounds is the director's to judge by ear and is never reported as "sounds fine"; change evidence after repeated failure; never the clock in a test (set GameState.replay_seed); say what was not exercised. (2026-10-06, ROADMAP-01#proof-practices; CLAUDE#Verification)
11. **Named-player corrections are evidence, not the model.** Spot-checks (Toby Greene in the low 80s) are calibration checks for the broader model, not manual buffs, and there is no patch list of famous names. Fix the classifier or the measurement, not the name. The director's own named corrections live in Ratings.ATTR_ADJUSTMENTS (Langford 1.15, Bowey 1.022, Rozee 1.10) and data/ovr_overrides.csv; test suites run without those rows except the ratings suite. (2026-09-28 to 2026-10-01, ROADMAP-08#ARD-M5-009, M5-010; undated, sim_harness/run_tests)

## 8.B Evidence standard

12. **Label every claim.** Observed mechanic, developer interpretation, player account, published research, or ARD hypothesis. Interface observations are not playtests. Promotional claims are not released behaviour. Simulations establish behaviour, never enjoyment: pair them with observation. (2026-10-05, GENRE#2, #8.2)
13. **Replayability comes from real constraints.** Personnel, cap, age, role, fixture, opponent, development time and identity. Test whether one recruitment policy or tactical response dominates. Scouting uncertainty alone cannot rescue a system with one universally best answer. Preserve real-data starting identities and reproducible seeds. (2026-10-05, GENRE#4.2)
14. **Seeded comparison set.** Keep-current vs context-informed vs mismatched call policies; AI parity (identical action effects both sides, hidden-information isolation); equal-resource compositions against several opponent types; markets and development with no policy given hidden information; staged 5/10/20-year career samples plus longer integrity soaks; the inherited start asserts roster counts, unique ownership and no ghost 2026 players. (2026-10-05, GENRE#8.2)
15. **Phone-session recording standard.** A compact questionnaire (miniPXI) may supplement observation but is not an enjoyment score. Record build, device, theme, source of confusion, repeated lookups, mistaken taps and observed completion. Ask separately about appearance, football understanding, agency, attachment and voluntary desire to continue. Small formative sessions find problems; they do not establish retention or causal enjoyment. (2026-10-05, GENRE#9, #8.1)
16. **Comparison base for flavour.** Compare the same seed and commands with flavour watched, skipped, muted, disabled and reloaded; football events and state must agree. Cosmetic RNG must not consume the football generator stream or change draft generation. (2026-10-06, ROADMAP-21#Common dependencies 5)
17. **Difficulty evidence limits.** Seven five-season autopilot careers with no premierships in 35 seasons do not reproduce the director's actively managed dynasty. Do not infer all careers are too easy or too hard. Do not tighten profile bands or boost the AI without a demonstrated cause. (2026-10-05, GENRE#5.1)
18. **Verify repairs before another rebalance.** Verify the draft and potential repair (#494: club horizons, age and career contribution, attainable veteran POT, tighter scouting reads) before proposing another rebalance. The director's original examples and five-season competitive checks are retained, and the broad outcome is not DONE solely because the repair landed. (2026-10-07, ROADMAP-01#0.4.1 item 5)
19. **Desired mechanics are "fixed, not deleted".** (2026-09-29, ROADMAP-02-1.12) A mechanic is fixed, not deleted, merely because its implementation is incomplete, unless the director explicitly changes the design. Repair priority: player deception and no-ops first, then core gameplay importance, severity, simplest fix. The audit (PR #103) classified 40 systems (26 working, 7 questionable, 2 partial, 2 report-only, 1 no-op, 1 dead, 1 unverified).
20. **System reality audit (P0 gate follow-up).** Check every gameplay lever for no-ops, wrong effects, silent overwrites and placebo flags. Trace control, state, rule, consequence with seeded before/after proof. Audit all player-facing lever copy for plain, concrete football language (no vague "adds pressure"); neutral options explain real behaviour. Deliver a lever / claimed / actual / evidence / mismatch / fix register. Do not mark DONE from a few controls or a green suite; present unresolved design discrepancies for director decision. The section 1.12 follow-up adds exhaustive control-to-effect verification and no cosmetic or no-op gameplay promises. (2026-10-07, ROADMAP-02-1.12; ROADMAP-24)
21. **Test weaknesses found by that audit.** Several plan descriptions quote effects that do not match the engine; tests "often prove storage/multipliers not outcomes" (2026-09-29); some guarded matchday checks can silently skip under load while the suite still reports pass; Balanced was materially worse than several alternatives in the measured setup. The Momentum meter once had no real MatchSim state under it; that is not a reason to remove Momentum.
22. **Noise-safe checks.** Match-motion checks average seed-sensitive measures over games 40-44 (director option A, 2026-11-03): ball-up closest pair at least 1.5 m (was 2.5); Attack corridor longest file at most Balanced +0.75, shift at least 7.5 m (was 8), spread 9.5-11.5 m (was 10-11); worst-case limits stay on game 42; five games keep the suite under 900 s. The director chose noise-safe bounds because per-seed data was noise (PR #885 recorded the same decision as bounds from main averages over seeds 40-51; gap at least 1.5, file at most Balanced +0.75, corridor/spread +0.5, 5 seeds). (2026-11-03, ROADMAP-32; SESSION)

## 8.C Parity, fairness and honesty of the AI

23. **Parity.** "The human player gets no hidden mechanical advantage over AI-controlled clubs." Human and AI obey the same football, list, development and competition rules. Any mechanic that improves the human club needs an equivalent AI route. No hidden rating boosts, favourable RNG, easier ceilings, cheaper costs or extra information. Player-only interface conveniences are allowed; any intentional parity-breaking difficulty setting must be explicit. (undated, GUARD#1.4)
24. **No psychic AI.** "AI must never be psychic." The AI reacts only to information it could plausibly know (public match state, observed behaviour, scouting and known tendencies). It must not counter an unobserved game plan, a private moment-card choice or a future player decision. A stronger AI comes from inference and preparation, not omniscience. (undated, GUARD#1.4)
25. **No hidden help for either side.** Difficulty is not solved by hidden AI boosts, rubber-banding or psychic tactics. Training, ageing, contracts, cap, depth, drafting, selection and AI list building must create meaningful long-term consequences without weekly busywork. Exclusions: using simulation as proof of enjoyment, forcing dynasty turnover, universal win-rate targets, hidden balance assistance. (2026-10-04, ROADMAP-19-9.1#P1; ROADMAP-11-03)
26. **Morale and fatigue run on one rule for every club.** Morale fix (done 2026-10-04): the user-only morale edge (about 7.6 points a match, win share 53% to 60%) is gone; GameState._rival_morale_after_round gives every rival the same rule (+4 win, +1 loss, a fit player left out loses 3, a star 6, softened by man-managers). Form and morale must follow the same rule for every club. Fatigue parity was audited (#213, 2026-10-05) with no defect. The user's side can look more tired only because a saved selection keeps players in until changed; user-only extras are the development budget (neutral at Standard) and weekly-card XP (+8 closed session, +12 heavy, +26 development week), which only reach the season cap sooner. Whether morale should drift back toward settled is a later design question. (CB#1, #5; ROADMAP-01-1.4)
27. **Leadership effects stay small.** "**All leadership buffs must be small**, including the gameplan, clutch, morale, retention and recruitment ideas above." Cap stacking from several leaders or co-captains, avoid double-counting, and validate fatigue, training and the contract economy with AI parity. (undated, ROADMAP-10-02)
28. **Media conference honesty.** Do not claim real players' private intent. Do not let amusing wording conceal an ignored answer. Each answer applies its promised existing effect once; later callbacks need RC-005 selected first. (2026-10-05, ROADMAP-09-07)
29. **Expectation fairness.** Sacking is the hard career-ending state, so the game must never kill a long save from an opaque or miscalibrated expectation. Use bands, not one-point rank boundaries; consider previous finish, trajectory, age profile, injuries and rebuild; scrutinise first-season and redraft careers (rank 4 must not automatically mean a punitive top-four mandate). Boards may be demanding ("earned pressure, not arbitrary safety"), the reason for a goal is concise and visible, and long-save probes check that the human is not disproportionately sacked. (undated, ROADMAP-09-02#Phone-playtest follow-up)

## 8.D Difficulty and the dynasty curve

30. **The unmanaged collapse is about right.** The director answered on 2026-10-05: (1) the League Draft board shows scouted estimates; (2) measure live-match calls, the trade market and free agency; (3) "the unmanaged collapse is about right". His call as quoted by the rerun: "the unmanaged collapse is about right, so leave it and make active play less dominant instead". (2026-10-05 and 2026-10-06, DIFF)
31. **Flags a bit more reachable.** Director ruling, relayed: "keep the autopilot slide, but make flags a bit more reachable, aiming for a well-managed club to win one every 8-10 seasons" (2026-10-05, SESSION). The lever is where effort earns the upside, not a blanket buff. He chose "Make flags a bit more reachable (Recommended)" and "Reward management (Recommended)". On 2026-10-06 he decided on "a bit more reachable"; the lever is how high good management can lift a club (it needs an average of 3rd-4th). The sim league is not too random (premier from the top four 90% vs real 92%). He had also chosen "Measure more first (Recommended)" on autopilot before any change, which this ruling followed.
32. **Playtest findings of 2026-10-04.** Overall difficulty too low (three straight premierships with minimal engagement); List Profile top end too easy; extreme margins (175-28); early extensions. Evidence led to: no change to List Profile bands, margins rarer than real AFL (100+ about 0.5% vs about 1.9%), early-extension price +15%. (ROADMAP-9.1; DIFF)
33. **Second-pass difficulty changes, each by the director's call.** An unanswered weekly card gives nothing; only stars ask to extend early, one a season; an unsettled best-22 player tests free agency; trade contract values are fixed and contending rivals can buy a rebuilding club's starters (up to 15 asks); the assistant makes routine match-day calls worth nothing either way. Result: autopilot fades 9th to 16th strongest, working the list holds it 7th (with trades 4th), trades no longer buy flags alone (3 flags vs 11), premierships stay earned and dynasties stay possible. This is harness evidence, not a playtest: confirm on the phone. Caveat: every career now starts from a weaker opening list (strength rank 9.5, not 6.8); the season-5 gap between autopilot and management is the list-management change alone, and ladder and strength columns are more reliable than flag counts. (2026-10-04 to 2026-10-05, CB#Before and after; ROADMAP-19-9.1)
34. **Two named difficulty dials.** "Yes, two named difficulty dials; I set the sizes in playtest." (P-20, 2026-10-18, GENRE#Directors-decisions item 5) The dials are board patience (poor seasons before you are warned) and rival recruiting (how sharp other clubs draft and trade), each said in one sentence, with no hidden information and no boost to the AI. Built in #797; the sizes are the director's to set in playtest. Before this, difficulty only changed the trade margin the AI accepts.
35. **Levers evidence.** Live-match calls are worth about +3 points of win rate; the Defensive press outlier did not hold at 1,080 matches (62.2% vs 59.8%, +2.4), so there is no dominant default and no tuning; the trade market was exploitable (stars for unproven teenagers; #224 discounted unproven potential in TradeValue); free agency is not a lever (pool mostly 33+). Rerun the defensive press at about 1,000 matches before tuning. (2026-10-05, LEVERS_EVIDENCE; ROADMAP-19)
36. **Trade value of growth.** For Trade E the director chose: "Rule, 60%". Project growth with the game's own development rule to age 28 and count 60% of it (TradeValue.future_rating, GROWTH_COUNTED := 0.6), because the old model counted no growth past 24.5 while Potential.growth closes 15-22% of the POT gap yearly to 28. (2026-10-05, TRADE_E_HANDOFF)
37. **Synergy and List Profile findings.** Any synergy is +12.5% win, +11.3 points; Running machine stays at x0.50 (+6.3%, just under the +8% bar); PRESS_TACKLE_SHARE 0.95 holds tackles near 1.03; league scoring holds (83.9 vs 83.8). Synergies are not inflating league-wide (about 1.4 of 6 per club). 'Elite' is a rank (top three of eighteen, ELITE constant 0.15, by the director's T8.Q2 answer; it was top four, under 17%), not a threshold; the playtest profile (four Elite, two Strong) occurs in 0.6% of club-seasons. Strong sides should keep identifiable weaknesses. League strength spread is 2-3.4 SD and the strongest list wins about one flag in five. (2026-10-04 to 2026-10-06, CB#5; ROADMAP-19-9.1)
38. **All-AI league targets.** They bound how far scouting error can shrink: stronger side wins 57-61%, skill share 45-60%, r(strength, wins) 0.45-0.65, premier from top-3 strength about 40-60%. (2026-10-04, CB#10)
39. **Extreme margins.** A 175-28 win must remain possible. Preserve rare massacres and prevent routine runaway percentage farming. Evidence: 100+ margins in about 0.5% of drafted matches (real about 1.9%), none 150+; momentum is the main compounder; no tuning indicated. (2026-10-06, EXTREME_MARGINS; ROADMAP-9.1)
40. **Custom prospects.** A user-created key forward, winger or rebounding defender (now Small defender, rule 4.8) "should feel meaningfully different without one archetype being an exploit." Starting ability stays inside a believable draft band. Potential "is hidden and randomly rolled once when the career is created. The player cannot choose it, see it exactly, or reroll it." Bounded distribution, a usable AFL role-player floor, most prospects project role player through good AFL player, stars uncommon, S-tier ceiling deliberately rare but possible, no special development, durability or longevity, and the roll is saved so reloads cannot fish. (undated, ROADMAP-10-04)

## 8.E Ratings, OVR, POT, roles and data

41. **Data sources and attribution.** players_2026.csv holds real 2026 totals for 669 players with 2026 appearances (not a complete registered-list manifest); draftees_2026.csv has 56 prospects (Rookie Me Central August-2026 top 50 plus six); player_history_2026.csv holds careers 2004-2025 (career "" means no senior game before 2026). Attribution: AFL Tables; no endorsement; no player imagery or club badges. validate_data.py checks 220 club x column aggregates against published totals (220/220 pass). The director approved the Wheelo Ratings download (afl_player_stats_2025/2026.json, fetched 2026-10-06, saved in docs/research/wheelo/). Real trade volume 2019-2025 is in the data (#376) with a validate_data check. (2026-10-05 to 2026-10-06, DESIGN#1; SESSION)
42. **Chronology.** Source data is the completed 2026 season; the career starts in 2027 (GameDB.START_YEAR); ages are as of 1 March 2027. The 2027 League Draft pool is the 2026 league plus the 2026 class on the same terms (father-son and NGA ties do not apply to a full redraft). No 2026 development or retirements are simulated; old saves keep their own timeline. (undated, DESIGN#Chronology; ROADMAP-02-1.9)
43. **Inherited-start data rules.** Record club counts, named omissions, source dates and the rating basis for non-appearance players. No fictional filler, no silent cuts. Simulated contracts stay estimates. Verify pick ownership and order sources (AFL draft hub, draft order, Draftguru secondary). National draft 19-20 November; trade period 5-14 October. (2026-10-05, GENRE#6)
44. **Real lists after the trade period.** "the afl trade period is over in real life, you should assess the new lists and include it as an urgent post 1.0 patch", then "i mean post 1.0, so 1.1 patch". Real 2026 lists are brought up to date after the exchange period and free agency as the first item of 1.1, with two sources per row (docs/research/TRADE_PERIOD_2026_LIST_CHANGES.md); data PR "2026 lists after the trade period (1.1)". (2026-10-19, ROADMAP-30#40)
45. **Rating construction.** Shrinkage K=5 toward the per-player league rate; normalise by the 98th percentile capped linear (not percentile rank); Brownlow cap 1.0; role is computed (RUCK only if hitouts a game are at least 7); a second role only by gate; about 2 rucks per club. Overall = 0.70 role core + 0.22 star + 0.08 durability, position-stretched; the best 2026 players are about 90, the middle in the 50s; role-core weights are measured from the engine (+20 lift per attribute); the forward 98th-percentile target is raised to the midfield one (ARD-M5-010: Greene 79 to 82; best forward 88 to 92). OVR is derived and recomputed on load. (undated, DESIGN#2, #Overall & salary)
46. **OVR must predict football strength.** Fix the measurement first. Do not revert the pressure and OVR work because headline ratings moved (PR #80, 2026-09-28). The forward core is goalkicking .30, pressure .20, marking .15, accuracy .15, creating .12, carry .08. Validation: new OVR predicts season wins better (AI-club leagues r 0.35 to 0.46; real lists r(OVR, Squad.strength) 0.80 to 0.87); a 3+ OVR favourite wins 55%; Spearman with the 2026 Brownlow 0.46 to 0.51; Daicos (47 votes) rates highest. The history CSV is rebuilt with build_history.py --offline whenever the formula changes. Keep #47's measured pressure weighting. (ROADMAP-08#ARD-M5-010; DESIGN; ROADMAP-24)
47. **Role labels match football identity.** Fix the classifier, never patch famous names (merged 2026-10-01, PR #147): George Wardlaw should not read as a wing; Harvey Langford is not forced into inside mid. Pure forwards and defenders are not converted to midfielders by disposal volume; wings are distinct from inside mids; plausible FWD/DEF draft supply without quota stuffing. The classifier is to be re-audited against football reality and source positions, not overrides. (2026-10-01 and 2026-10-06, ROADMAP-08#ARD-M5-009; ROADMAP-02-03-20)
48. **Second role from listed position.** Ratings.listed_secondary gives a listed forward (0.35 goals or 0.5 marks inside 50 a game) or listed defender (2 rebound 50s plus one percenters) his line back as a second role (71 MID/FWD, 13 MID/DEF); first positions and ratings are untouched; hand corrections Maurice Rioli and Cody Weightman (ROLE_CORRECTIONS); listed forwards with no forward numbers (McKercher, Delana, Scerri) stay MID. (undated, DESIGN#Role)
49. **Director's playtest corrections of 2026-10-14 (A23, A24, A25, A27, A28).** These are his instructions and stand.
    - A23, "buff kozzy pickett, his potential should be 90": dozens of hand-set potentials in data/potential_overrides.csv (#750), including Harvey Langford 90, Wanganeen-Milera 90, Bontempelli 95/95, Gulden 90, Uwland 90, Lalor 91, Dawson 90/91, Rankine 90, Hayden Young 90, Caleb Serong 87, Murphy Reid 86, Ben King 85/85, Bolton 85/85, Dunkley 85/85, McCluggage 85/85, Rozee 83, Fletcher 83, Ashcroft 83, Will Day 82, Wardlaw 81, Tholstrup 80, Daniel Turner 80, Sam Taylor 80, Dempsey 80, Humphrey 80, Kako 80, Zac Bailey 80/80, Wilmot 88, Jordan Clark 88/88, Windsor 78, Bergman 78, Weightman 77, Ginbey 76/86, Chandler 75, Ginnivan 75, Nic Martin 75/80, Voss 79/82, Gibcus 72, English 71/71, McReery 70, de Koning 70, Conway 70, Lachie Jones 70, Jai Serong 70, Smillie 70, Neal-Bullen 70/70, Butts 70/70, Rayner 70, Duursma 70/95, Dudley 62/75.
    - A24, "Tom Green gws is incorrectly labelled a small defender, he needs to be audited vigorously because he is one of the competitions elite inside mids": Green becomes an inside midfielder (small defender capped at 186 cm, 94 to 59); open item 21 (Miers forward, Uwland not key, Langford wing) fixed; then a full role audit of every player against real 2025 data (#756, #764).
    - A25, "Blicavs should be 65 ovr"; "Tom Stewart incorrectly labelled small defender, should be 85 ovr": hand-set OVR table data/ovr_overrides.csv (attributes scaled to target, profile kept): Blicavs 65, Stewart 85, English 71, Bontempelli 95, Ben King 85, Dawson 90, Neal-Bullen 70, Butts 70, Bolton 85, Voss 79, Clark 88, Dudley 62, Duursma 70, Ginbey 76, Merrett 89, Nic Martin 75, Harris Andrews 87, Bailey 80, Dunkley 85, McCluggage 85. Also "buff daniel rioli 10%" and "nerf JVR 10%" (OVR and POT, the lead's reading). Overrides must reach list-addition players (#761).
    - A27, "there are 3 duursma bros": add the Willem-Xavier brother pair to data/player_relations.csv; check other families with 3+ siblings (#750).
    - A28 (the lead's audit from the Green comment): established players added to lists late (Green, Josh Kelly, Taylor Adams) were rated as draft prospects. Rule: rate every list addition on senior games from the real record (#769, 54 additions rated on the latest AFL season 2021-25; Green 54 to 86).
    - The 2026-10-18 audit still lists about 60 named OVR/POT corrections as open (PR #761 OVR overrides, #750 potentials); the records do not confirm implementation status.
50. **Potential inflation.** Complaints: "Voss 92-98 POT (above Treacy)", most of the league shown 90+, Viney 49-53 OVR with 78-86 POT. #494 is PARTIAL (true 90+ POT about 5%; Viney now 66, Waterman 83). Still needed: distributions by age, role, club and source, and long-career outcomes. No blanket nerf, no exact values replacing uncertainty, custom-prospect hidden-talent rules preserved. (2026-10-07, ROADMAP-02-03-18)
51. **Generated-player provenance.** Fictional players need a believable entry history. Expansion lists had given 27-30-year-olds a draftee ceiling (age 29, OVR 69, POT 91); fixed in #213 with an age-based ceiling past 21. Long-career soak acceptance includes generated-player p50 OVR within +-3 of the real-list p50 by season 10 [P-05]. (2026-10-05 and 2026-10-18, ROADMAP-19; release-1-0 plan Task 1.4)
52. **Player rating (match).** One whole number per match built only from the box score (never OVR): kick 2, handball 1, mark 3, tackle 3 (tackles 2 and pressure acts 1 after #47), goal 14, behind 1, hit-out 3, I50 1, F50 delivery 3, rebound 50 3, one percenter 1, clanger -2, free against -1 more; floored 0; not AFL Fantasy weights on purpose. Median 70, 90th percentile 111, 99th 143. (undated, DESIGN#Matchday screen)
53. **Team form.** From the last five results weighted 0.30/0.25/0.20/0.15/0.10; used only in clanger rate (x1-0.05f) and stoppage-win chance (+0.010 difference); home edge 0.030; no RNG draws; form 0 leaves the engine unchanged. (undated, DESIGN#Team form)
54. **Expansion chronology.** "Do not load a frozen 2027 roster into a 2028/2030 expansion state and then merely add years to displayed ages." Every existing player reaches the expansion season through the normal sim, with no silent respawns or retroactive history, and long-save validation of ages, histories, stints and no resurrected retirees. (undated, ROADMAP-10-05)
55. **Club Forge enters like every club.** A created club enters with the career and drafts its list in the League Draft like every club; there is no separate concession package (engine in PR #340). (2026-10-06, ROADMAP-9.2)

## 8.F Calibration targets against real AFL

56. **Method.** Every calibration compares the sim with real AFL 2026 per-role and team rates. The calibration suite compares seeded games to 2026 averages; disposals, kicks, marks and I50 are within a few percent; behinds 0.86 and rebound 50s 0.87 are soft outliers; average margin about 29; the Through stars plan is 1.14x FOCUS_CARRY keeping a focused game in the low 30s. tools/sim_harness.py's match half is stale since 2026-10-08 and is not shipped code. PR #885 (clanger_is_free 0.43, hitouts_per_stoppage 0.78) put all 17 calibration rows in band. (2026-10-06 to 2026-11-03, DESIGN#Calibration; SESSION)
57. **Football sanity first.** Normal AFL actions must not be impossible because of simplistic role gates; prefer weighted tendencies over absolute exclusion; do not flatten positional identity or give every player identical event access; diagnose first and classify (real sanity bug, over-restrictive model, balance question, no issue). Result (PR #82): gates became role weights (CARRY_ROLES, SHOT_ROLES rucks 0.35 defenders 0.06, CLEARANCE_ROLES fwd 0.12 def 0.10, ONE_PCT_ROLES). (2026-09-28, ROADMAP-04#ARD-M1-001)
58. **Disposal split.** Real AFL is roughly mids 40%, defenders 34%, forwards 21%, rucks 5%; measured 35.9 / 35.0 / 24.1 / 4.9; no further tuning. A back-third carrier stays weighted by intercept (interceptor defenders winning ball in the back half is football). (2026-09-28, ROADMAP-04)
59. **Per-role 2026 reference.** Disposals DEF/MID/FWD/RUCK 16.58/20.46/11.72/12.49; clearances 0.53/3.23/0.67/3.56; goals 0.10/0.47/1.30/0.38; team disposals about 367.4. On 18+5 the sim gave DEF 16.6, MID 17.6, FWD 8.4, RUCK 6.7 (the sim's counts from kick/handball events miss marked kicks, about 17% low). (2026-10-06, SESSION c01, c02, c04)
60. **Ruck and defender calibration.** Director: "I guess? you work it out", a lukewarm yes: the lead works out ruck clearance weight and the defenders' middle carry toward the real numbers (#373: ruck disposals 8.0 to 12.0; ruck clearances 5.25 vs real 3.56; DEF disposals 20.1 to 19.2 vs 16.6). Guardrails: team disposals, scores per game, goal accuracy and inside-50s each within 2% of #373; forwards' goals per game up no more than 5%; the third weight CARRY_ROLES middle FWD is approved; if neither candidate clears, ship the two-knob result (DEF about 18.6, ruck clearances about 4) and note that centre-bounce clearances have no line weight (MatchSim.gd:1661); no third mechanism. Built as #393 (CLEARANCE_ROLES RUCK 0.25, CARRY_ROLES middle DEF 0.45 FWD 0.6). The HTB rule is role-neutral: do not tune per role (that would be a hidden role modifier). Balance stays the director's to call. (2026-10-06, SESSION c03, c04, c05)
61. **Intercept targets.** Real AFL per Champion Data 2025: 63.4 intercept possessions per team per game (range 55.6-70.5; 2026: 65.4), about 15 intercept marks per team, best player 8.4 (Sam Taylor), top ten 7.7, role shares DEF 57% MID 23% FWD 15% RUCK 4%. The sim had about 52.5 a team and a best defender about 10.4: too few and too concentrated. Targets: team about 63, best 8-9, MID about 2.5, FWD about 1.1. Crediting-only rule: margins and scores unchanged on paired seeds. Intercepts are tracked separately from the intercept attribute; generic rebounds are not intercepts unless possession is actually won from the opposition. On midfield intercepts (sim 1.4 vs real 2.5) the director said "Merge, gap later" (2026-10-06): merge #462 and close the gap later as backlog item 25. (2026-10-06 and 2026-10-10, HO; ROADMAP-05#ARD-M2-004; ROADMAP-08)
62. **Set shots.** Targets (#456 evidence): about 55% of shots are set shots (the sim is at 37.5%-37.8%); conversion set vs general play by distance 0-10 m 100/89, 10-20 96/63, 20-30 85/51, 30-40 71/38, 40-50 54/39, 50+ 33/34. Crumbing rates by position (gen fwd 1.16 and 33%, mid-fwd 1.09/23%, mid 1.09/20%, key fwd 0.66/29%, key def 0.71/26%, ruck 0.48/14%). The play-on/bomb/pack split has no public source, so the sim reports its own rates. Method: count free kicks inside 50 as set shots, send a share of general-play marks inside 50 to a set shot, calibrate conversion by distance with total scoring flat. Baseline on main: shoot call 63% goal, pass 31%, bomb 32%. Measurement only. (2026-10-06, ROADMAP-08#ARD-M4-013; HO item 24)
63. **Free kicks.** Contextual causes only: real football causes (holding the ball, high contact, other sensible infringements) emerge from event context, with no giant umpiring simulator; discipline can influence risk but not overwhelm. League rate was right (17.6 vs real 18.7) but 39% generic and rucks at half the real rate; fixed with a ruck-contest cause (RUCK_FREE_SHARE 0.90, RUCK_FREE_REACH 15 m) and F50_MARK_FREE_SHARE 0.85; forwards' frees are still 0.6 vs real 0.81. Free kicks are paid to the opponent near the ball via a separate stat_rng so match results are unchanged (200-match hash identical). (2026-09-28 to 2026-10-06, ROADMAP-06#ARD-M3-007; FREE_KICKS)
64. **Forward archetype scoring.** Key forwards score more from leads, aerial contests and set shots; small and general forwards from crumbs, loose-ball wins, snaps and pressure; either can still score the other way; attributes and traits drive tendencies, never a listed-position script. Archetypes by height: key from 192 cm, small up to 181 cm; generated forwards include small forwards (172-203 cm, #297). Calibration: goals .96-.98 of real, marks 1.03-1.04; top-3 goalkicker share .42 vs real .38. Sides with 4-5 key forwards score slightly more (13.4 vs 12.0-12.6); a controlled mixed-vs-tall comparison belongs with RPG-004. (2026-10-06, ROADMAP-06#ARD-M3-002)
65. **Marks and pressure.** Contested vs uncontested is decided by mark context, never labelled randomly afterwards (real AFL about 10-11 contested marks a team; measured 10.7). Pressure is a team rating of 0-100 (p10 55, p50 60, p90 66) with zonal pressure; tackles alone do not represent pressure; do not spam a pressure stat for every nearby player; calibration: every team stat within 6% of real 2026. (2026-09-28, ROADMAP-05#ARD-M2-005, M2-006)
66. **Metres gained and efficiency.** Credit actual forward territory to the responsible player; backward movement is never positive; no double counting. Known gap: about 3,200 m a team vs about 5,500 m broadcast (engine territory model, balance-gated). Effective disposal is judged by actual outcome; by line DEF 89 RUCK 88 MID 82 FWD 71; team about 84% vs about 73% real because a non-free clanger does not turn the ball over (engine turnover work, balance-gated). It is not counted as ineffective: "that would be pretending". (2026-09-28, ROADMAP-05#ARD-M2-001, M2-002)
67. **Centre bounces, chains and sources.** Credit CBA only to players who actually contest the bounce (ruck plus three best contested non-wing mids on the ground); the denominator is team centre bounces; never infer CBA from listed position (PR #79). Score involvements use a bounded chain window, one chain from stoppage, kick-in, free or turnover to a score; do not award the whole team; goals and goal assists stay distinct. Score sources are tracked (centre, stoppage, kick_in, free, turnover, general) as coaching and scouting data, not another dashboard; turnover share is low vs real AFL (about 40% of scores), recorded and not tuned. Goal assist is the last kick to a goalkicker, only when a goal is kicked, never to the scorer. (2026-09-28, ROADMAP-05#M2-003, M2-007, M2-008)
68. **Shot-context accuracy.** Report set shot vs open play and distance bands only where the sim genuinely models them; do not create fake precision from coordinates the sim does not meaningfully use. (undated, ROADMAP-05#ARD-M2-009)
69. **Weather.** Weather affects play (wet, windy, hot), calibrated against real numbers; its look has zero effect on the result (ARD-M4-016, 2026-10-06). The director asked: "do your own research into how weather impacts footy and draw your own conclusions". Findings (WEATHER_EVIDENCE.md): wet marks about -13%, wind 20 km/h about -5 points, hot freer play. Wet tackles stay short (+6% against a +12% target) to protect scoring, as built. The wet-weather trait rule is contested at least 78 and disposal at least 72: 42 of 669 real players (6.3%, target 5-8%); Whitfield and Dangerfield qualify only by name (the director chose "Evidence + stats", WET_NAMED in #449), keyed by id so Club Forge players cannot take it. (2026-10-06, BIBLE_AUDIT; SESSION c05, c06, c04)
70. **23-man list rerun.** The director said: "new balance checks will need to be run for the 23 man list, as it will impact fatigue and recovery and stat accumulation and xp accumulation etc". Audit 18+4 vs 18+5: interchanges 41.1 to 47.0, players who took part 21.87 to 22.87, km per player 13.22 to 12.62, goals 11.86 to 12.01, injuries 0.80 to 0.84. (2026-10-06, SESSION c03)
71. **Current AFL rules.** Verify contemporary rules before implementing and record the rule assumptions in comments. The intended starting season is 2027. 2026 rules research (RULES_2026.md, #461, agent research and not a director ruling): kick-in still from the goal square with a play-on option; kick-in time 8 s (down from 12); the centre bounce is scrapped for a centre ball-up; the sub is removed and 23 are named including five on the interchange. (undated and 2026-10-06, ROADMAP-02-1.9; SESSION c06)
72. **50-metre penalties, learning projects, development projects.** 50-metre penalties (balance-gated) trigger from post-mark and free infringements (encroachment, dissent, delay), advance 50 m toward goal respecting field limits, may be influenced by discipline, apply to the AI equally, show the advance clearly, and need long-run frequency evidence before they are called done. For the learning-a-position project, phone feel and balance stay with the director; the open levers (pass margin 3, reach 6, cost against team-mates) are not changed, and the pass margin is tightened first if projects are too sure a thing. Development projects and synergy selection must matter: "why do we have these features if they dont have an impact, fix them"; projects visibly change a career, stay inside POT, and are measured against the #315 OVR economy (baselines: proj lever +0.25 +/- 0.47, syn -0.57 +/- 0.56, paired project change -0.04 +/- 1.03 ladder places). Built: projects-matter #379 (PROJECT_OWN_GAIN 2, LEARN_PAYBACK 1). (2026-10-05 to 2026-10-06, ROADMAP-06#M3-008; ROADMAP-08#M5-003; SESSION c04, c01)
73. **Focus audit.** Paired seeds, at least 200 matches per arm; arms no focus, key forward, inside mid, small defender, key defender; report the focused player's shots, goals, disposals, back-half disposals and team score, metres gained and turnovers. If an expectation fails tell the Boss before changing constants. (2026-10-07, SESSION)
74. **Squad strength is computed from the starting 18 only.** Squad.ruck/contest is not refreshed after interchanges; refreshing changes results and needs its own balance run. (2026-09-28, ROADMAP-04#ARD-M1-002)
75. **League baseline.** A career re-drafts the 669-player pool, so it does not start from real 2026 lists. Harness: tools/balance/league_balance.gd with sources drafted/career/real/sensitivity and policies ai (must be passed explicitly for all-AI shards) and board (default; seeded random among the top 5 by OVR); the CI smoke suite league_balance asserts no balance target. Findings: the draft compresses rating spread 4-8x (selected-22 OVR SD 0.29 all-AI, 0.60 board vs 2.38 real lists); the engine keeps about half the real skill variance (skill SD of wins 3.14 vs about 4.5) and blowouts are about half as frequent (60+ margins 8.4% vs 15.5%); home win 58.8% vs real 59.3%; Squad.strength correlates r=0.88 with real 2026 wins; in a drafted league OVR does not measure match strength; home ground is worth about +4 to +5 whole-list OVR; a naive best-by-OVR drafter finishes near the bottom because the engine rolls role-specific attributes that OVR weights differently (Squad.strength explains little of a drafted season, pooled R2 0.12-0.25; about +3 win-percentage points per realised OVR point; stronger side wins 55.2% vs 61.5% on real lists). Measurement only; no constants were changed. (2026-09-25, LB)

## 8.G Stat integrity and AFL-language authenticity

76. **Stats come from real football events.** "Do not generate player-facing statistics independently merely to make box scores look realistic." Inside 50 from an actual entry, metres gained from territory, effective disposal, intercepts, score involvement and CBA all come from the event; avoid double-crediting and "random stat garnish". (undated, GUARD#1.2)
77. **Stat integrity rules.** Every count comes from real simulated events: no garnish, no fabricated numbers. Ratios derive from counts with consistent denominators and explicit no-rate when the denominator is zero; season rates come from aggregated counts, never averaged match percentages. Never manufacture missing historic data. Required regression tests cover attribution, ruck accounting, chains vs assists, save/load and reconciliation. Missing plumbing is work, not permission to drop a metric. All ten mandatory statistics are recorded for every match including AI v AI (StatBook.KEYS, StatBook.RATES). (2026-10-07 and 2026-10-09, ROADMAP-02-03-17; ROADMAP-01#0.4.1)
78. **Season stats hub.** The director's single highest priority at the time: Season stats hub with Ladder, Player stats, Awards race, Fixture, Trophy room, plus stat integrity. Player stats show season contribution records for players from every team (not attributes, OVR or POT), totals and per-game views, sorting, filters by team, position, age, archetype, trait and stat, and a click for bio and career history. Mandatory metrics: Inside 50s, metres gained (also per game), running bounces, ground-ball gets, kicks and handballs and kick-to-handball ratio, contested possessions and rate, hitouts and hitout win % from real ruck contests, shots at goal, goals, behinds, accuracy %, set-shot/open-play splits only where the sim supports them, score involvements, goal assists; include AI-versus-AI matches. StatBook: KEYS order is append-only (37 stats), team keys add chains, pressure_wins, centre_bounces, kick_ins; rates are ranked only with enough attempts. (2026-10-07, ROADMAP-02-03-17; SESSION c07)
79. **Brownlow records are real.** "retain genuine round/match vote records if the current aggregate-only store lacks them; do not reconstruct or fabricate a count from final totals." Save and resume preserve the reveal position without re-awarding; do not mark complete from a walk-on or static cards. (undated, ROADMAP-10-03)
80. **Veracity standard.** "Veracity standard — footyhead-proof or don't ship it". Claude must be certain of factual accuracy; two strong independent sources for non-trivial claims; record the basis of ambiguous counts; if sources disagree, omit; dynamic claims are computed from save history. "if a true footyhead checks it, the fact should hold up." The AFL knowledge layer (ARD-M7-011, speculative) enriches the news feed and natural football stories (for example "Essendon has won its first final in X days"), not detached trivia; loading-screen fact dumps are lower priority; strict anti-trivia-spam; disputed claims are omitted rather than guessed. (undated and 2026-10-05, ROADMAP-10-07; ROADMAP-24)
81. **Favourite club for real players.** Research the actual publicly documented pre-draft or childhood favourite club; prefer player interviews and official club or AFL profiles; keep source URL and date against the stable player ID; resolve conflicts rather than guess; if unsourced, show a restrained "Not recorded"; never infer from current club, hometown, surname or the director's example. (2026-10-06, ROADMAP-21#FL-005)
82. **Guernsey research.** "Before implementation, Claude must perform a **club-by-club historical design audit**": every AFL Sir Doug Nicholls guernsey from 2014 (plus selected AFLW and state-league), recording club, season, artist, Nation, story, layout, reusable geometry, culturally specific elements not to copy, and provenance; official AFL and club sources preferred. (undated, ROADMAP-10-06)
83. **Long-career integrity.** "Test decades-long / 100+ year careers." ARD-M8-005 (P1) watches save growth, duplicate history, stale records, impossible lists, generated-player degradation, coaching-history corruption, draft and cap dead ends, rollover bugs and performance, using automated long-run sim. Smallest reproducible 5/10/20-year careers come before the integrity soak; the 100+ year goal is kept without rerunning after every change; pair with observed multi-season follow-up on remembered people and desire to continue. CI acceptance: 8 careers x 20 seasons x 4 policies, zero integrity violations, season-20 save under 3x season-2, round time under 1.5x. (2026-10-18, ROADMAP-11-03; release-1-0 plan Task 1.4)

## 8.H Audits as code (director approved 2026-10-18)

84. **Q8 "Audits that protect the fun" is approved.** The director answered "Yes", authorising P-76 to P-81 (bundle 8). CI catches a hovering ball, a clipped label, a slow rollover, a plan that beats everything and an AI club that churns; the director still makes every balance call. (2026-10-18, PASS2#8; ROADMAP-29#8)
85. **P-76: picture and truth never disagree.** Per-tick assert that no disc exceeds footballer speed and none jumps; the ball is always with a player or in flight; 36 discs at 8x; the feed, scoreboard and engine score agree; the intended sheet loaded, not a fallback. This enforces the bible: "a ball that hovers, or acts independently of the player" must never reach the director. (2026-10-18, CPC#C-PC-03; release-1-0 plan Task 1.9)
86. **P-77 and P-78.** A screen sweep at 360x740 with 44 px minimum targets (P-77); time budgets per action (P-78). (2026-10-18, release-1-0 plan Task 1.9)
87. **P-79: no plan wins everywhere.** Seeded round-robin across weak, even and strong lists. The pass line is that no plan beats all comers beyond a stated share. The result is written into the term sheet in football words. Reports only; the director decides. Reason: exploitable match engines turn authorship into meta-following, and OOTP 26's two stuck complaints were exploitable AI and roster churn. (2026-10-18, CPC#C-PC-21; PLAYER/POLISH Q2-Q3)
88. **P-80: fun floor beside the realism floor.** A dynasty bot over 20 seasons on many seeds measures how often it reaches finals within five seasons and wins a flag within fifteen. Today's evidence is 0 flags in 35 autopilot seasons (autopilot does not coach). It feeds the P-20 dial sizes and does not declare balance done. (2026-10-18, CPC#C-PC-22)
89. **P-81: list churn stays real.** Per-offseason counts of AI club delistings, signings, trades and retirements against a real AFL yardstick; fails if far too high or low. Told to him without a decision asked (2026-10-19): AI clubs turn over about 9 players a year (median 9; 66% of club-years inside 8-12) while real clubs lose 4-7 to delistings and retirements plus trades (LIST_CHURN_AUDIT_2026-10-18); balance stays his. (2026-10-18 and 2026-10-19, CPC#C-PC-23; ROADMAP-30)
90. **Market context.** The AFL mobile niche is nearly empty: only Footy Mayhem 26 (3.6/5 from 20 ratings) is on phone and Australian Football Coach is PC only; there is no direct competitor for a polished premium portrait AFL coach sim. (2026-10-18, MOB#Q6; inference)

## 8.I Director's other decisions on evidence and process

91. **Needs-work cutoffs.** Asked whether to change them, the director answered "no": needs-work cutoffs and the 2026 positional benchmark are unchanged. (2026-11-03, ROADMAP-32#19)
92. **Sim speed.** Director, 2026-11-04: #888 sim 35% slower is accepted for now; optimise the weekly AI in a later PR (next task = the sim-speed PR); the coach pool of 57 is fine as is. Bench over 3 seasons: #888 weeks 225 s vs main 146 s (+55%). (2026-11-04, HO scratch-2026-11-02-683516)
93. **Playtest evidence the audit cites.** From the director's phone playtests: four straight losses of about 40 points, "choices feel like clicking and hoping", synergies found opaque, far-receiver pauses; his verdict that "nothing I did meaningfully improved my chances", which he agreed the audit describes accurately. Seeded measurements: plan effect +10 to +23 when the opponent never changes plan, 0 to -5 with a countering AI; Gawn vs no real ruck about 14 points; one hit-out +0.4 points. Melbourne playtests: "every playtest loss had the opposition ruckman as best on ground" (Gawn went to Gold Coast in the draft; contest = 0.42 mid_contest + 0.24 ruck, contest_swing 360). Agent reading: he lost the ruck arms race in the draft with no information that the ruck is the most leveraged position. (2026-09-29, DESIGN_AUDIT#0, #1.3, #Evidence used)
94. **PC playtest comments.** He said: "please read my recent playtest comments on pc / some huge problems / but a lot to like too". They are in ROADMAP §1.11 (2026-10-07); the P0s include AI draft valuation and potential inflation (top), Club Forge repair, draft scroll snap, custom club with no coaches, ruck tagger, a centre ball-up call coming twice, equal decision buttons, draft age and filters. (2026-10-07, SESSION c07)
95. **Pre-season open questions.** Q2-Q5, Q7-Q9, Q11-Q12 and Q14-Q15 default to the sheet's recommendation unless taste or balance. Q4 familiarity and Q9 fitness base and training injuries are balance for the director's playtest. (2026-10-13, preseason_rulings; ROADMAP-26)
96. **Phone-playtest items from the observed-failures list.** Quarter-break "What's happening" info vomit is done (max 3 facts, "An even quarter."); club marker colours were corrected to sourced Pantone references with his sign-off (#231; Carlton and Melbourne keep lifted navy; Tasmania and Canberra unchanged). Still for his phone playtest: whether counts like "clearances 14 to 9" read too statistical, and the uncapped length of "What your calls did". (2026-10-06, ROADMAP-02-03-20)
97. **How-we-play and List Profile audits.** The three-match unlock can feel fixed: prefer sample-size-aware thresholds or shrinkage with "early read" copy and no confidence bars; the profile is recomputed from all season data; tests at 3, 6, 12 and 20+ games. How-we-win and get-beaten surface only material non-duplicate traits (max 2-3); a +1/-2 points-a-game line never headlines; each weakness has a football lever and there is no best-button recommendation. List Profile labels are validated against realised performance (a side reading Strong/Average across five dimensions loses heavily), with no overall power rating and no recommendation. (undated, ROADMAP-02-03-20)

## 8.S Superseded and cut

1. **"Measure more first" on the autopilot slide** (2026-10-05) and "Director wants more measurement before deciding" -- replaced by the 2026-10-05 ruling: "keep the autopilot slide, but make flags a bit more reachable, aiming for a well-managed club to win one every 8-10 seasons". Records that still show the question open (autopilot slide, "Director to decide the intended curve") are closed by that ruling and by "the unmanaged collapse is about right".
2. **Rival opening-draft scouting error 0-4 (#228, AI_EVAL_SD)** -- withdrawn by the director 2026-10-06 because #222 already removed most of the edge; 1-5 is kept.
3. **Backlog item 22, weather research** -- DROPPED (not deferred) because the director asked the lead to do the weather research himself; covered in #417 and WEATHER_EVIDENCE.md.
4. **Earlier intercept figure of 36.2 per team-game** (PR #79, 2026-09-28) -- replaced by the 2026-10-10 Champion Data brief (about 52.5 in the sim vs 63.4 real, target about 63).
5. **Old trade model counting no growth past 24.5** -- replaced by "Rule, 60%" (GROWTH_COUNTED 0.6 to age 28). The patch (docs/handoff/trade_e_growth.patch) was noted at handoff as uncommitted and untested.
6. **Match-motion bounds from "main averages, seeds 40-51"** (2026-11-03) -- replaced by the final noise-safe form (seeds 40-44, five games), director option A.
7. **Greene at 79** -- replaced by the forward 98th-percentile fix (Greene 82); PR #80.
8. **Through-stars check noise suggestions** (raise seeds to 40 or lower the bar to +1.5) -- no director ruling retained, not a rule; the Defensive press outlier did not hold at 1,080 matches and is dropped as a lever.
9. **Non-decisions cut:** the director's message "Try again" after a usage-limit stop (a resume prompt, not a rule); duplicated records of the same decisions (merged above).
10. **Status corrections.** The record showing the difficulty question as open is corrected by the 2026-10-05 ruling at 18:45 (no open question remains). Morale parity is done (2026-10-04 and 2026-10-05), not open.
11. **Contested marks 10.7** -- after the #516 integrity audit (400 paired matches; score 172.9 to 172.5; mark_share_of_kicks 0.330 to 0.327 kept) contested marks read 8.2 and contested-possession share counts only engine-modelled contests (about 24%); the later figure governs.
12. **"Elite should mean top three or two" wording** -- decided: top three (T8.Q2, ELITE 0.15).

## 8.Q Open for the director

1. Coleman cap: goalkicking saturates at 99 for 10 elite forwards because of the 98th-percentile clamp (Ratings.build_norm_params; phone case Brodie Kemp, 48 goals at Round 18). Do you want it normalised against the pool max or the 99.5th percentile, even though it re-rates forward OVR?
2. (Answered: Elite is the top three of eighteen, ListProfile.ELITE 0.15.)
3. ANSWERED 2026-11-12 ("Too harsh"): soften it so a club on autopilot drifts to mid-table, not last. Is the managed-vs-autopilot curve now where you want it? Autopilot slides to about 18th by year five, routine contract and free-agency work holds about 6th, and flags need trading (0 in 40 seasons for contracts plus free agency, 3 for full management). Is autopilot too harsh for a casual player, and does a flag every 8-10 seasons now hold when you play it on the phone?
4. Do you want more massacres for realism? Momentum's edge is the lever (100+ margins about 0.5% vs real about 1.9%; 60+ about 6.7-8.4% vs about 15.5%), as its own balance-gated item.
5. What sizes do you want for the two difficulty dials, board patience and rival recruiting, once you have played them?
6. Should the key forward v key defender full-time line ("beat/held", MatchNotes._duel_line) show only with a minimum of 6 contests, be changed in the engine, or be left alone?
7. Should wet tackles stay short (+6% against a +12% target) to protect scoring?
8. Do you want the midfield intercept gap closed now (sim 1.4 vs real 2.5, structural: midfield kicks intercepted about 5 times a team a game)? It is deferred under "Merge, gap later".
9. Do you want the set-shot share raised to about 55% of shots (the sim is at about 37.5%) and conversion calibrated by distance with total scoring flat?
10. Do you want the development drift fixed? Real players' contested bull rises from 3.5% (2027) to about 11% by 2033, and hothead from 6.2% to 32.1% by 2034 (discipline refits). Find the cause and propose the smallest fix first?
11. Should the broad classifier bias toward MID (about 85 listed forwards and 21 listed defenders read MID) be fixed via real_pos? It would move 122 players and 110 OVRs and pull the forward median about 7 below midfield, so classification and forward rating must be settled together.
12. Should every weakness be compulsory in the team builder (at least one, a second optional, no extra overall power)? Evidence and a recommendation come to you first.
13. Should unproven potential be discounted further in TradeValue, given that the trade market was exploitable by trading stars for unproven teenagers (#224 did a first pass)?
14. Expectation fairness: do the board goals now use bands, previous finish and trajectory, and have you seen a long-save probe show the human is not disproportionately sacked?
15. Learning-a-position projects: should the pass margin (3) be tightened first if projects are too sure a thing, with reach (6) and the cost against team-mates left alone?
16. Are the believability audits not yet run worth running now: comeback rules, AI list building, run-of-goals cadence, AI plan adaptation, blowout distribution, vignette reachability in close finishes, how-we-play maturity, how-we-win materiality, List Profile vs results, PlanFit spread across 18 sides, bomb goal odds, LOOSE_MARK_EDGE, peak-timing odds refresh, combine grade error, free-agency culture bonus and AI scouting errors?
17. Are the roughly 60 named OVR and POT corrections (PR #761, #750) and the role audit (#756, #764, A24 Tom Green) in the build as you want them, and are any still wrong?
18. PART ANSWERED 2026-11-12 ("Yes, at a break"): weather may change at most once a match, announced at a break, with the odds in the forecast; the rest of the system is still to design. How does weather affect a match, in system terms? It is decided that it matters; the system is not designed. (carried from the bible)
19. Do you want Q4 familiarity and Q9 fitness base and training injuries sized in your next playtest?
20. ANSWERED 2026-11-12 ("Speed up the engine", drift "Inside AFL bands"): speed the match engine up; a speed-up may shift stats only while every stat stays IN or NEAR the real AFL bands on tools/balance.sh match 1000 paired. Should the sim-speed work (weekly AI optimisation after #888) be the next task, and is the 35% to 55% slowdown still acceptable on a long career?

# Chapter 9. Open questions, contradictions, parked and rejected ideas

Precedence used here: a later director decision wins; the 2026-11-03 story-first order (1 story generator, 2 deep simulator, 3 approachable game) ranks everything. No Chapter 9 record carries a 2026-11-03 director ruling that overrides an item below, so nothing here is re-ranked by it; where an item is parked, cut or open, the story-first order is the tiebreak when the director next rules. Items marked "Claude's note" are agent statements, not director words. Source tags are as recorded.

## 9.A Current rules

### 9.A.1 Standing rejections and guardrails

1. **The feature test is "justifiable based on intent".** The assistant's split into "reasons" (need one) and "standards" (need all) was rejected; the director chose "justifiable based on intent". [2026-10-09, brainstorm#Rejected and why; docs/brainstorms/2026-10-09-design-bible.md#Rejected]
2. **"Ratings reflect reality" is not a separate third goal.** The director placed it inside the balance work, which is still settling. [2026-10-09, brainstorm#Rejected and why]
3. **Balance is never declared done by an agent.** It is settled only when testing shows numbers similar to real AFL and the director's own playtest passes. No balance target has been chosen; the candidates (skill share about 60-75%, stronger side wins about 62-68%, 60+ margins around 12-15%) are proposals only. P-79 and P-80 report; balance stays his. [2026-09-25, docs/LEAGUE_BALANCE.md#Reading the baseline; 2026-10-18, roadmap/29#Rules]
4. **Excluded outright:** any light theme, monetisation, and a cosmetic that touches the sim. [2026-10-18, GENRE.md#What-this-report-does-not-propose, Claude's note restating director and bible]
5. **Light mode is parked.** Director, 2026-10-13: "im also happy to abandon 'light mode' for now, i dont want to waste time on it". No new light-theme work, captures or tests until he says otherwise; the code stays. [2026-10-13, project CLAUDE.md]
6. **Re-declined in pass 2 (in the forms that avoid the decline):** a 3D match view (superseded 2026-11-14, rule 2.74) (2D reads shape better; goals covered by the 2.5D vignette), spoken commentary (licensed voices turn "monotonous"), and more story cards before the cadence audit (repetition, not count, is the failure). [2026-10-05, docs/GENRE_ENJOYMENT_RESEARCH.md#7 RC-001; also AUDIT.md#Already-proposed]

# Index of source documents

Every source named in the chapters' records files (the `source:` field of each record), normalised to one path per document. The number in brackets after a chapter is how many records from that document fed the chapter. Short names used in the records (GENRE, PASS2, CPC, c01 and so on) are expanded to the file they stand for; records that cite a section or a ruling number rather than a file are counted under the document that holds it.
